# Astro Portal API v1

This contract defines the private, versioned resource boundary consumed by the
mobile application. All timestamps are ISO 8601 UTC strings. Identifiers in
paths are server-generated UUIDs unless a field is explicitly named `clientId`.
Private records always derive `tenantId` from authenticated context; clients
never send it.

## Authentication

| Mode                   | Use                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ---------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Web session + CSRF     | Device pairing, device listing/revocation, and optional web plan reads.                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Device bearer token    | Capabilities, briefing, mobile plan reads/writes/deletions/restores, favorites, the owner's equipment inventory and its writes (« Mon matériel »), and entitlements. The token is returned once by claim and only its SHA-256 hash is stored.                                                  |
| Either credential      | Sessions and their events, the debrief queue and the debriefing decision, session supplements, uploads and the private capture list. A cookie-authenticated **write** additionally carries the CSRF token.                                                                                     |
| Anonymous device token | A phone with no space: its capabilities, its purchases, and the routes that read nothing personal — the reads of a place that a capability opens (the weather trend, the light-pollution map), the application error logs. Never a space's route. See [Anonymous devices](#anonymous-devices). |
| None                   | The shared target catalogue and the weather, sky-quality, place-resolution and place-search proxies, all public on the web portal as well.                                                                                                                                                     |

Plan **writes** stay device-only: the browser planner has its own
whole-document route and never needed a v1 write. Sessions and the debriefing
have no such web equivalent, and the debriefing is by definition a state both
surfaces answer — "as soon as one has debriefed, the other never asks again" —
so every route of
[Observation sessions and debriefing](#observation-sessions-and-debriefing) and
[Uploads and captures](#uploads-and-captures) accepts either credential. A
bearer carries no browser cookie, so no CSRF token applies to it; a cookie
session presenting a write does carry one, exactly as `POST
/v1/devices/pairings` already does.

How a device obtains its bearer token depends on the service, which says so
in `GET /v1/service` ([Service identity](#service-identity)): a private
server signing in by password pairs it with a code its account page shows
([Device pairing](#device-pairing)); a service whose spaces are open signs it
in with an e-mail address and a code received by e-mail, and creates the
space of an address that has none
([Sign-in by e-mail code](#sign-in-by-e-mail-code)); a private
server signing in by e-mail code does the same for its owner's address alone
(`spaceCreation: "closed"`). All hand out the same device token and the same
web session; every other route below behaves the same on all.

On a service signing in by e-mail code, open or private, the
token of a device detached from its space — from « Mes appareils », by the
device itself, or by its attachment to another space — answers `401`
`device_detached` on every route that reads a bearer, instead of
`device_authentication_required`: the application erases its copy of that
space ([Space devices](#space-devices)). A private server signing in by
password keeps `device_authentication_required` for a revoked token.

A public endpoint still refuses a credential it cannot honor: when an
`Authorization` header is present it is an intentional device attempt, and an
invalid, revoked, or non-device bearer answers `401`
`device_authentication_required` rather than falling back to the anonymous
reading.

An active device token also requires its recorded user to retain an active
membership of its recorded tenant. A revoked token or disabled user is not
authenticated. Device calls update `lastSeenAt`; briefing and plan
synchronization — reads, writes, deletions and restores — also update
`lastSyncAt`. Catalogue and favorite calls do not: they are a shared-library
preference, not plan synchronization.

Session and debriefing calls are plan synchronization and update `lastSyncAt`
too — reads included, as for the plan calls. This is not bookkeeping
detail: `lastSyncAt` is the single input of the unsynchronized-device warning,
so a route that updates it is asserting "this device has said what it knows
about the night". Uploads and the capture list therefore update `lastSeenAt`
only: transferring a processed image proves a network connection, not that the
phone pushed its field notes, and treating it as synchronization would silence
a warning that is still true.

## Error envelope

Every error uses:

```json
{
  "error": {
    "code": "machine_readable_code",
    "message": "Human-readable explanation."
  }
}
```

`PUT /v1/plans/{planId}` additionally returns this body for a stale write:

```json
{
  "error": {
    "code": "plan_conflict",
    "message": "The plan changed on another surface."
  },
  "serverRevision": 7,
  "serverPayload": {
    "id": "browser-plan-1",
    "date": "2026-09-14",
    "name": "Tonight",
    "items": []
  },
  "versionId": "8a25c9d2-7f0b-4b3a-9c9e-2f7e6f0b1a3d"
}
```

`versionId` names the immutable `set_aside` version that the rejected submission
was just stored as. It is present whenever the conflict archives a submitted
payload — every stale `PUT` — so a client can call
`POST /v1/plans/{planId}/versions/{versionId}/restore` directly from the
conflict response, with no separate lookup. A stale `DELETE` (see below)
carries no payload to set aside and therefore omits `versionId`.

## Endpoints

| Method and path                                             | Authentication                                      | Success                                                                                                       | Failure behavior                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ----------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `POST /v1/devices/pairings`                                 | Web session + CSRF                                  | `201` pairing code and expiry                                                                                 | `401`/`403` for missing session or CSRF; `404` `not_found` wherever a device signs in by e-mail code (open spaces, a private server in the `email-code` mode).                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `GET /v1/devices/pairings/{code}`                           | Web session                                         | `200` `{ "claimed": boolean }`                                                                                | `404` `pairing_not_found` for an unknown, expired-and-purged, or another tenant's code; `404` `not_found` wherever a device signs in by e-mail code. Additive route, see [Device pairing](#device-pairing).                                                                                                                                            |
| `POST /v1/devices/claim`                                    | None                                                | `201` one-time device token                                                                                   | `400` malformed request; `409` expired, claimed, or invalid code, `device_limit_reached`; `429` `pairing_rate_limited`; `404` `not_found` wherever a device signs in by e-mail code.                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `GET /v1/devices`                                           | Web session                                         | `200` tenant devices                                                                                          | Never exposes token hashes. Wherever a person signs in by e-mail code: either credential, « Mes appareils », see [Space devices](#space-devices).                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `PATCH /v1/devices/{deviceId}`                              | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` the renamed device                                                                                      | `400` `invalid_request`; `404` `device_not_found`. Wherever a person signs in by e-mail code (`404` `not_found` on a private server signing in by password), see [Space devices](#space-devices).                                                                                                                                                      |
| `DELETE /v1/devices/{deviceId}`                             | Web session + CSRF                                  | `204`                                                                                                         | `404` when the device is outside the current tenant. Wherever a person signs in by e-mail code: either credential (+CSRF for a session); `409` `device_change_limited`, see [Space devices](#space-devices).                                                                                                                                           |
| `GET /v1/capabilities`                                      | Device token or anonymous device token              | `200` complete neutral capability map                                                                         | `401` for a missing, invalid, revoked, or non-device credential. An anonymous device reads its own capabilities, see [Anonymous devices](#anonymous-devices).                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `GET /v1/service`                                           | None                                                | `200` service identity                                                                                        | Never fails for a running portal; no database read. See [Service identity](#service-identity).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `POST /v1/sign-in/code`                                     | None                                                | `202` a code is on its way                                                                                    | `400` `invalid_request`/`email_invalid`; `403` `not_owner` and `409` `not_initialized` on a private server signing in by e-mail code; `429` `sign_in_rate_limited`/`sign_in_locked`; `502` `email_delivery_failed`; `503` `sign_in_unavailable`/`service_starting`; `404` `not_found` on a private server signing in by password.                      |
| `POST /v1/sign-in/verify`                                   | None                                                | `200` space, device token or session                                                                          | `400` `invalid_request`/`email_invalid`/`sign_in_code_incorrect`/`sign_in_code_expired`/`sign_in_link_invalid`; `403` `space_unavailable`; `429` `sign_in_locked`/`sign_in_rate_limited`; `503`; `404` on a private server signing in by password.                                                                                                     |
| `POST /v1/sign-in/attach`                                   | None                                                | `200` space and device token                                                                                  | `400` `invalid_request`/`sign_in_code_expired`; `403` `space_unavailable`; `409` `device_change_limited`; `429`; `503`; `404` on a private server signing in by password. See [Space devices](#space-devices).                                                                                                                                         |
| `GET /v1/space`                                             | Device token or web session                         | `200` the space and this device                                                                               | `401` `authentication_required`/`space_deleted`/`device_detached`; `404` `not_found` on a private server signing in by password. See [Sign-in by e-mail code](#sign-in-by-e-mail-code).                                                                                                                                                                |
| `DELETE /v1/space`                                          | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `202` the dates of the erasure                                                                                | `400` `invalid_request` without `{ "confirm": true }`; `401` `authentication_required`/`space_deleted`; `403` `csrf_invalid`; `404` `not_found` on a private server. See [Deleting a space](#deleting-a-space).                                                                                                                                        |
| `GET /v1/briefing`                                          | Device token                                        | `200` compact briefing                                                                                        | No precise coordinates are included.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `GET /v1/plans`                                             | Device token or web session                         | `200` plan list                                                                                               | Tenant-scoped only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `GET /v1/plans/{planId}`                                    | Device token or web session                         | `200` plan resource                                                                                           | `404` when absent or outside the tenant.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `PUT /v1/plans/{planId}`                                    | Device token, or web session + CSRF                 | `200` updated plan, `201` created plan                                                                        | `428` missing `If-Match`; `409` stale/frozen/invalid creation revision.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `DELETE /v1/plans/{planId}`                                 | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `204` no content: the plan is in the trash                                                                    | `428` missing `If-Match`; `400` malformed; `404` absent; `409` stale or refused status; `409` `plan_in_progress` to a web session when its night runs on a device (`?force=true` deletes it anyway). See [The trash of the plans](#the-trash-of-the-plans).                                                                                            |
| `GET /v1/plans/deleted`                                     | Web session                                         | `200` the plans and multi-night plans in the trash                                                            | `403` `web_session_required` (a device). Never gated. See [The trash of the plans](#the-trash-of-the-plans).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/restore`                           | Web session (+CSRF)                                 | `200` `{ plan }`                                                                                              | `403` `web_session_required`; `404` `plan_not_found`; `409` `plan_limit_reached`/`plan_night_place_conflict`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/erase`                             | Web session (+CSRF)                                 | `200` `{ planId, erased: true }`                                                                              | `403` `web_session_required`; `404` `plan_not_found`; `409` `plan_not_deleted`. Definitive.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/versions/{versionId}/restore`      | Device token                                        | `200` new revision                                                                                            | `404` for a version outside the tenant/plan; `409` for a frozen plan.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `GET /v1/framings`                                          | Either credential                                   | `200` framing list (archived included)                                                                        | Tenant-scoped only. See [Framings](#framings).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `GET /v1/framings/{framingId}`                              | Either credential                                   | `200` framing resource                                                                                        | `404` `framing_not_found`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `PUT /v1/framings/{framingId}`                              | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` updated, `201` created                                                                                  | `428` missing `If-Match`; `400` `invalid_request`; `403` `capability_required`; `409` `framing_conflict`/`framing_locked`/`framing_archived`.                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `DELETE /v1/framings/{framingId}`                           | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `204`, or `200` `{ archived: true }`                                                                          | `428`; `404`; `409` `framing_conflict`/`framing_in_use`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `POST /v1/framings/{framingId}/restore`                     | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` desarchived resource                                                                                    | `404`; `409` `framing_not_archived`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `GET /v1/projects`                                          | Either credential                                   | `200` `{ projects }` (archived included)                                                                      | Tenant-scoped only; read-only on a service without projects (what the space holds). See [Projects](#projects).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}`                              | Either credential                                   | `200` project resource                                                                                        | `404` `project_not_found` (read-only on a service without projects).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `PUT /v1/projects/{projectId}`                              | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` updated, `201` created                                                                                  | `428` missing `If-Match`; `400` `invalid_request`/`invalid_if_match`; `403` `capability_required` (`capability: "planner.mosaic"`)/`entitlement_read_only`; `404` `project_not_found`; `409` `project_conflict` (with `project`)/`invalid_creation_revision`.                                                                                          |
| `DELETE /v1/projects/{projectId}`                           | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` `{ archived: true, project }`                                                                           | `428`; `400`; `403` `capability_required` on a service without projects; `404`; `409` `project_conflict`. Never deletes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `POST /v1/projects/{projectId}/trash`                       | Web session (+CSRF)                                 | `200` the multi-night plan in the trash                                                                       | `403` `web_session_required`; `404` `project_not_found`; `409` `project_in_progress` (`{ "force": true }` deletes it anyway). See [The trash of the plans](#the-trash-of-the-plans).                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `POST /v1/projects/{projectId}/restore`                     | Web session (+CSRF)                                 | `200` `{ project, plansRestored, plansKept }`                                                                 | `403` `web_session_required`; `404` `project_not_found`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `POST /v1/projects/{projectId}/erase`                       | Web session (+CSRF)                                 | `200` `{ projectId, erased: true, plansErased }`                                                              | `403` `web_session_required`; `404` `project_not_found`; `409` `project_not_deleted`. | `PUT /v1/projects/{projectId}/integrations`                 | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` integrations and totals by filter                                                                       | `400` `invalid_request`; `403` `multi_night_not_active` (with `capability`); `404` `project_not_found`/`session_not_found`; `409` `integration_outside_night`.                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `POST /v1/projects`                                         | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `201` project resource with `nights`                                                                          | `400` `invalid_request`; `403` `capability_required`/`multi_night_not_active` (from existing nights); `404` `session_not_found`/`plan_not_found`; `409` `target_mismatch`/`invalid_creation_revision`. See [Nights of a multi-night plan](#nights-of-a-multi-night-plan).                                                                              |
| `GET /v1/projects/candidates?targetId=\                     | framingId=`                                         | Either credential                                                                                             | `200` `{ candidates }`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `200` with `[]` when the target has no night; `400` `invalid_request` without a target. Never needs a capability.        |
| `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}/nights`                       | Either credential                                   | `200` `{ projectId, nights, candidates }`                                                                     | `404` `project_not_found`. Never needs a capability.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `POST /v1/projects/{projectId}/nights`                      | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` `{ projectId, nights, candidates }`                                                                     | `400` `invalid_request`; `403` `multi_night_not_active` (with `capability`); `404` `project_not_found`/`session_not_found`/`plan_not_found`; `409` `target_mismatch`.                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `DELETE /v1/projects/{projectId}/nights/{nightId}`          | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` `{ projectId, nights, candidates }`                                                                     | `403` `multi_night_not_active`; `404` `project_not_found`/`night_not_found`. Idempotent.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}/analyses`                     | Either credential                                   | `200` `{ projectId, nights, cumulative }`                                                                     | `404` `project_not_found`. Never needs a capability. The nights' guiding and frames, night by night and together (`project.schema.json` `nightsStatistics`).                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `GET /v1/plans/debrief-queue`                               | Either credential                                   | `200` nights to debrief                                                                                       | Never blocks and never truncates; an empty queue is `200`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/debrief`                           | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` decision, or the existing one                                                                           | `400` `invalid_request`/`set_aside_version_required`/`unknown_target_adjustment`; `404` `plan_not_found`; `409` `plan_not_debriefable`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `POST /v1/sessions`                                         | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `201` new session, `200` existing one                                                                         | `400` `invalid_request`; `404` `plan_not_found` for a plan outside the tenant; `409` `session_limit_reached`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `GET /v1/sessions`                                          | Either credential                                   | `200` session page                                                                                            | `400` `invalid_request` for a malformed `since` or `limit`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `GET /v1/journal`                                           | Either credential                                   | `200` the sessions, groups, stats                                                                             | `400` `invalid_request`. Never gated. See [The Journal](#the-journal).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `DELETE /v1/sessions/{id}`                                  | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` the session, deleted, with its grace                                                                    | `404` `session_not_found`; `409` `session_not_finished`. Soft: kept 30 days. See [Deleting a session](#deleting-a-session).                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `GET /v1/journal/deleted`                                   | Web session                                         | `200` the deleted sessions                                                                                    | `403` `web_session_required` (a device). Never gated. See [Deleting a session](#deleting-a-session).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/restore`                            | Web session (+CSRF)                                 | `200` the session, live again                                                                                 | `403` `web_session_required` (a device); `404` `session_not_found`; `409` `session_plan_taken`. See [Deleting a session](#deleting-a-session).                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/erase`                              | Web session (+CSRF)                                 | `200` what went with the session                                                                              | `403` `web_session_required` (a device); `404` `session_not_found`; `409` `session_not_deleted`. Definitive. See [Deleting a session](#deleting-a-session).                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/archive`                           | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` the plan, archived                                                                                      | `404` `plan_not_found`; `409` `plan_not_frozen`. See [History of a plan](#the-history-of-a-plan).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/unarchive`                         | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` the plan, as it was                                                                                     | `404` `plan_not_found`. | `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/session`                           | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `201` the plan and its new session                                                                            | `404`; `409` `plan_not_debriefed`, `plan_archived`, `session_exists`. The mode of a night changed in « Nuit en cours »: `{ piloting }`. Idempotent. | `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/events`                             | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` per-event classification                                                                                | `400` `invalid_request`/`event_batch_too_large`, `409` `session_event_limit_reached`, with nothing written; `404` `session_not_found`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

Additive route, see below. `?includeReplaced=true` keeps the signals a rework replaced.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `PUT /v1/sessions/{id}/rework`                              | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` the session, reworked                                                                                   | `400` `invalid_request`/`invalid_if_match`; `404` `session_not_found`/`event_not_found`; `409` `session_rework_conflict`/`night_not_finished`/`event_not_removable`/`event_already_replaced`/`event_not_replaceable`; `422` `target_not_found`; `428`. Free, and never held by the read-only window. See [Reworking a session](#reworking-a-session).  |

Idempotent.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/reopen`                             | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` session                                                                                                 | `400` `invalid_request`; `404` `session_not_found`; `409` `session_debriefed`. Idempotent; a stale replay changes nothing. See [Reopening an observation session](#reopening-an-observation-session).                                                                                                                                                  |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/supplements/{sid}`                  | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` decided supplement                                                                                      | `400` `invalid_request`; `404` `supplement_not_found`; `409` `supplement_already_accepted`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/imports/preview`                    | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` dry-run report, persists nothing                                                                        | `400` `invalid_request`/`invalid_path`; `404` `session_not_found`; `409` `asset_not_uploaded`; `422` `checksum_mismatch`. | `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/imports/confirm`                    | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `200` import summary, idempotent                                                                              | Same refusals as `preview`, plus `403` `entitlement_read_only`. See [Open session package import](#open-session-package-import).                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/analyses`                           | Either credential (+CSRF)                           | `201` `{ analysis, replaced, steps }`                                                                         | `400` `invalid_request`; `403` `analysis_not_entitled`/`entitlement_read_only`; `404` `session_not_found`; `413` `analysis_too_large` (a report v3 above 2 MB); `422` `analysis_empty`. Replaces the night's analysis — see [Session analyses](#session-analyses).                                                                                     |
| `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/analyses`                            | Either credential                                   | `200` the night's analysis, report included                                                                   | `404` `session_not_found`/`analysis_not_found`. Never gated by a capability.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

The steps stay.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

## Service identity

`GET /v1/service` answers, with no credential and no database read,
`{ "service": "astro-portal", "apiVersion": 1, "version": "<software version>" }`
([`service.schema.json`](service.schema.json)). Clients call it before saving a
self-hosted address (the address is kept only when this route answers with the
`astro-portal` marker and an API version the client supports) and to tell
whether the service they use answers at all. The body never carries an
operator-configured name, a host name or any account data; later versions may
add fields, which clients ignore. `parseServiceIdentity` in
`astro-contracts/service-identity` is the shared check, fixtured in
`fixtures/service-identity.json`.

### How a person signs in: `signIn`

The same body carries, since the space sign-in, an additive `signIn` object
([`service.schema.json`](service.schema.json) `$defs/signIn`). It depends
only on the service's settings, never on a database read, and the
applications read it to show the right screen:

| `signIn.method` | Service                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Screen of the application                                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `email-code`    | An e-mail address, then a six-digit code received by e-mail. A service whose spaces are open: an address without a space gets one (`spaceCreation: "open"`); `termsUrl`/`privacyUrl` are present once configured. A private server (`ASTRO_PORTAL_AUTH_MODE=email-code`): one space, its owner's; any other address is refused (`spaceCreation: "closed"`). | « Adresse e-mail » → « Saisissez votre code » ([Sign-in by e-mail code](#sign-in-by-e-mail-code)); on `closed`, the words of a portal of one person (« votre portail »), never the creation of a space. |
| `pairing`       | A private server signing in by password (`ASTRO_PORTAL_AUTH_MODE=password`): one space, created at its installation; no e-mail address asked of the device.                                                                                                                                                                                                 | The QR code scanner and the eight-character code, as today ([Device pairing](#device-pairing)).                                                                                                         |
| `none`          | A service whose private API is switched off (`ASTRO_PORTAL_AUTH_MODE=disabled`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Nothing to attach to; the public catalogue still works.                                                                                                                                                 |
| _absent_        | A private server older than this field.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Read as `pairing`.                                                                                                                                                                                      |

```json
{
  "service": "astro-portal",
  "apiVersion": 1,
  "version": "1.0.1",
  "signIn": {
    "method": "email-code",
    "spaceCreation": "open",
    "codeLength": 6,
    "termsUrl": "https://www.example.test/terms",
    "privacyUrl": "https://www.example.test/privacy"
  }
}
```

A service that names its terms and privacy pages per language (the settings
hold `{lang}`) announces the English pages in
`termsUrl` / `privacyUrl`, which any client opens, and the address templates
beside them, additive:

```json
"signIn": {
  "method": "email-code",
  "spaceCreation": "open",
  "codeLength": 6,
  "termsUrl": "https://www.example.test/en/terms/",
  "privacyUrl": "https://www.example.test/en/privacy/",
  "termsUrlTemplate": "https://www.example.test/{lang}/terms/",
  "privacyUrlTemplate": "https://www.example.test/{lang}/privacy/"
}
```

A client that knows the templates replaces `{lang}` with the tag of the
language it is shown in (`fr`, `ja`, `zh-Hans`, `pt-BR`: the tags of the locale
registry; the English page where it has none); an older client keeps the
English address.

A private server signing in by e-mail code announces:

```json
{
  "service": "astro-portal",
  "apiVersion": 1,
  "version": "1.0.1",
  "signIn": {
    "method": "email-code",
    "spaceCreation": "closed",
    "codeLength": 6
  }
}
```

`closed` is additive (the API version stays `1`): a client that knows only
`open` reads it as `open`, and the refusals below (`not_owner`,
`not_initialized`) still tell the person what happened. An unknown `method`
is a newer service: the application says it cannot sign in to it and
suggests updating. Recorded exchanges:
`fixtures/api/service-identity.json`.

## Device pairing

A pairing code is an eight-character Crockford base32 string (excludes `I`,
`L`, `O`, `U` to avoid look-alikes), `device-pairing.schema.json`'s own
`pairing.code`/`claimRequest.code` pattern:
`^[0-9A-HJKMNP-TV-Z]{8}$`. `POST /v1/devices/pairings` creates one, live for
five minutes or until claimed once, whichever comes first; `POST
/v1/devices/claim` exchanges it for the mobile app's own device bearer
token.

### The pairing QR code

The account page's pairing panel shows the code both as text and as a QR
code, encoding the code plus the address the mobile app needs to reach this
portal — with the `qrcode` package, not a new one. Format, version 1:

```
planastro-pair://v1?origin=<origin, percent-encoded>&code=<code>
```

- `origin` is exactly `window.location.origin` as the browser reports it —
  scheme, host and port, nothing else (no path, query, fragment or
  credentials). This is the _only_ way the mobile app learns which portal a
  scanned code belongs to; the code alone is ambiguous between portals.
- `code` is the same eight-character pairing code shown as text.
- Nothing else is ever encoded: no durable token, no account or tenant
  identifier, no device identifier. The QR disappears the moment the code
  does — claimed, expired, or a fresh code requested — so it never outlives
  the plain-text code sitting right next to it.

A scanner reading anything else (wrong scheme, missing `v1`, a missing
`origin`/`code` parameter, an origin that fails the same validation the
manual "private service address" field already applies) refuses the scan
rather than guessing; manual entry remains available regardless. The
reference implementation building this payload is
`astro-contracts`' own `buildPairingQrPayload`
(`packages/astro-contracts/src/pairing.ts`), consumed by
`packages/portal/src/components/account/AccountPage.js`.

### Watching a pairing code from the browser

The account page polls `GET /v1/devices/pairings/{code}` every 2-3 seconds
while a pairing code is on screen, so the panel updates itself the moment
the phone finishes pairing rather than requiring a manual refresh. The response
carries nothing beyond whether the code has been claimed:

```json
{ "claimed": true }
```

Never a device token, never the code itself, never which device claimed it
— the panel already holds a `GET /v1/devices` result it re-fetches on
`claimed: true` to show the newly paired device, the same list the page
already renders. Polling stops on the code's own expiry, on the panel
closing, when the tab is backed away from (`document.visibilityState`), and
on unmount — never a loop that survives the pairing code it was watching.

## Sign-in by e-mail code

Only a service announcing `signIn.method: "email-code"` answers the routes of
this section; any other answers `404` `not_found`. Where spaces are open
(`spaceCreation: "open"`) there is one entry for creating a space and for
signing in: an address that has no space gets one at its first successful
verification. On a private server (`spaceCreation: "closed"`) the same
routes and rules serve its owner's address alone: another address is
refused with `403` `not_owner` and receives nothing, and before the
installation every address is refused with `409` `not_initialized` and the
`bootstrapPath` of the installation page (`/auth/bootstrap`); a private
server has nothing to hide from enumeration, and the person must know at
once. There is no deletion of a space there (`DELETE /v1/space` answers
`404`): its owner deletes the server. There is no password and no
authenticator application. The normative shapes are
[`sign-in.schema.json`](sign-in.schema.json); the recorded exchanges are
`fixtures/api/sign-in-code-request.json`, `fixtures/api/sign-in-verify.json`,
`fixtures/api/sign-in-link.json` and `fixtures/api/space-current.json`.

Every `POST` of this section requires `Content-Type: application/json`; a
request without it is `400` `invalid_request` (a cross-site form cannot send
it, which keeps another site from signing a browser in). Unknown fields are
ignored.

### Requesting a code

`POST /v1/sign-in/code`

```json
{
  "email": "vous@exemple.fr",
  "language": "fr",
  "device": {
    "application": "planner",
    "installationId": "uwiNSxC6OIQqqkmdEeoVUB6dUVpI1kds",
    "name": "iPhone 16 Pro",
    "platform": "ios"
  }
}
```

- `email` is given one form, so that two spellings of one address never
  open two spaces and an invisible character never makes an address look
  like another: composed Unicode (NFC); spaces, tabs, line breaks, no-break
  and ideographic spaces removed; any other control or formatting character
  (U+0000–U+001F, U+007F, U+200B–U+200F, U+202A–U+202E, U+2060–U+206F,
  U+FEFF) refused, never removed; the local part in ASCII « dot-atom »
  (letters, digits and `!#$%&'*+/=?^_`{|}~-`, dots between them), 64
characters at most, lowercased — dots and `+` stay, no rule of a
provider; the domain in punycode (`bücher.example`→`xn--bcher-kva.example`), lowercase, without its final dot, two labels or
more, the last not all digits; 254 characters at most. Otherwise the
answer is `400` `email_invalid`.
- `language` (a shipped locale tag: `fr`, `en`, `ja`, `zh-Hans`, `zh-Hant`,
  `ru`; absent or other: `en`) is the language of the message.
- `device` is optional here; it is what recognises the device (see
  [Space devices](#space-devices)).

`202`, the same answer, in the same time, whether the address already has a
space or not:

```json
{
  "email": "vous@exemple.fr",
  "codeLength": 6,
  "expiresAt": "2026-09-29T21:24:00.000Z",
  "expiresInSeconds": 600,
  "resendAt": "2026-09-29T21:15:00.000Z",
  "resendAfterSeconds": 60
}
```

The message has left. Any code sent earlier to this address stops being
valid: only the last message counts (« Vérifiez le dernier message reçu »).
`resendAt` is when « Renvoyer le code » unlocks.

### Verifying

`POST /v1/sign-in/verify`, with the address and its code, or with the
address and the token of the link — never a code and a token together.
`client` is `app` (the mobile application or Astro Sync, which then
describe their `device` and receive a device token) or `web` (the browser,
which receives a session).

```json
{
  "email": "vous@exemple.fr",
  "code": "481207",
  "client": "app",
  "device": {
    "application": "planner",
    "installationId": "uwiNSxC6OIQqqkmdEeoVUB6dUVpI1kds",
    "name": "iPhone 16 Pro",
    "platform": "ios",
    "model": "iPhone17,1"
  }
}
```

```json
{
  "linkToken": "L6-hEKAbdZ63mYLsAi7VuGXcWm0-5FMU-QRAuYxF39s",
  "email": "vous@exemple.fr",
  "client": "app",
  "device": { "…": "as above" }
}
```

- `code`: six digits; spaces and hyphens typed between them are ignored.
- `linkToken` goes with the address the person typed on this device. The
  application uses a link only when it has itself a request in progress on
  this device, for the address the person typed; without one it ignores
  the token and opens its sign-in screen. The service refuses a token that
  does not belong to the address sent (`400` `sign_in_code_incorrect`,
  counted as a failed attempt for that address): a link someone forwarded
  from a code asked for their own address never attaches another person's
  phone to their space.
- `device.application`: `planner` (the mobile application) or `sync` (Astro
  Sync). `installationId` is an identifier the application generates once
  and keeps in the keychain (iOS) or its protected store (Android, desktop),
  16 to 128 characters of `A-Z a-z 0-9 _ -`. `name` is the name shown in «
  Mes appareils », by default the model of the phone (iOS no longer gives
  the personal name); the person can change it later. `platform` is `ios`,
  `android`, `macos`, `windows` or `linux`. `proof` is optional (a platform
  attestation, `{ "kind", "token" }`), see [Space devices](#space-devices).

`200` for the application:

```json
{
  "created": true,
  "space": {
    "id": "4ec83832-fbb7-4f8d-91ca-8c7eefd8ab71",
    "email": "vous@exemple.fr",
    "webUrl": "https://portal.example.test"
  },
  "device": {
    "id": "6daf99d9-1c14-4206-bae2-3cec9456a714",
    "name": "iPhone 16 Pro"
  },
  "deviceToken": "wl2zxC3Dt4HJgMfhEiU4pUwq0oeW9ODmY8T3moyhKm4"
}
```

- `created` is `true` when this verification created the space (the
  application then shows « Votre espace est prêt »), `false` when the address
  already had one.
- `space.webUrl` is the address of the space on the web, where one signs in
  with the same e-mail address.
- `device` is the device registered by this sign-in, under `device.name`;
  `deviceToken` is its bearer credential, returned once, 43 base64url
  characters, used exactly like the token of a pairing.

`200` for the web: `{ "created": …, "space": { … } }`, and the response sets
the cookies of the same web session a private server opens:
`__Host-astro_portal_session` (HttpOnly) and `__Host-astro_portal_csrf` when
the cookies are `Secure` — so that no other
host of the domain and no page over plain http can set them —
`astro_portal_session` and `astro_portal_csrf` over plain http in
development, where a browser refuses the `__Host-` prefix. The service reads
the names of its own setting only.

A code serves once. A verification that succeeds uses up the code and the
link of the same message together.

**Waking the service first.** The service may need a moment to start; its
first answer can be slow. Before sending a code for verification a
client calls `GET /v1/service` until it answers, then sends the verification
once, as Astro Sync already does before a pairing claim. A verification that
never received an answer is not replayed blindly: the code may have been
used; the person asks for a new one.

### Refusals

| Code                     | Status | When                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Extra fields                                      |
| ------------------------ | ------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- |
| `invalid_request`        | `400`  | Not JSON; a code that is not six digits; a code and a link token together; a link token without its address; `client: app` without its `device`.                                                                                                                                                                                       | —                                                 |
| `email_invalid`          | `400`  | The address has no normalised form (see « Requesting a code »).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | —                                                 |
| `sign_in_code_incorrect` | `400`  | The code does not match the last one sent to this address; the code stays valid until it was compared five times. Also a link token that belongs to another address.                                                                                                                                                                   | `attemptsRemaining` (4 to 1)                      |
| `sign_in_code_expired`   | `400`  | No valid code for this address: more than 10 minutes old, already used, replaced by a newer message, already compared five times, or never asked. Also a link whose code is more than 10 minutes old.                                                                                                                                  | —                                                 |
| `sign_in_link_invalid`   | `400`  | A link token that was used, replaced by a newer message, or never issued.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | —                                                 |
| `sign_in_locked`         | `429`  | Codes cannot be typed for this address: five failures of it from this network origin, or twenty from all origins, within the hour. Returned by the failure that locks, then by every code verification of the address (from that origin, or from all) and every request for a code from that origin until `retryAt`. Never for a link. | `retryAt`, `Retry-After`                          |
| `sign_in_rate_limited`   | `429`  | A new code less than 60 seconds after the last one; more than 5 codes to one address within the hour; more than 20 code requests, or 30 failed verifications, from one network origin within the hour. A message still leaving counts.                                                                                                 | `retryAt`, `Retry-After`                          |
| `email_delivery_failed`  | `502`  | The sending service refused the message or could not be reached. Nothing is counted as sent; the earlier code stays valid; the request may be repeated at once.                                                                                                                                                                        | —                                                 |
| `sign_in_unavailable`    | `503`  | The e-mail sign-in is switched on but its settings are incomplete. Or, with `retryAt`, the e-mail budget of the whole service is spent (per minute and per hour): the same for every address, nothing is sent.                                                                                                                         | `retryAt`, `Retry-After` when the budget is spent |
| `space_unavailable`      | `403`  | The code is right, but the address belongs to a person who can no longer sign in (a disabled person, or a deleted space whose erasure is not done yet). Nothing is used.                                                                                                                                                               | —                                                 |
| `service_starting`       | `503`  | The service cannot reach its database yet. Nothing was checked, sent or used.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `retryAt`, `Retry-After`                          |
| `not_owner`              | `403`  | A private server (`spaceCreation: "closed"`): the address is not its owner's. Nothing is sent, nothing is counted.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | —                                                 |
| `not_initialized`        | `409`  | A private server not installed yet: no address signs in before its owner installs it on the web.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `bootstrapPath`                                   |
| `not_found`              | `404`  | A service that does not sign in by e-mail (a private server signing in by password).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | —                                                 |

With `client: "app"`, once the address is proven, the device itself may
be refused, where spaces are open to any address: `409`
`device_attached_elsewhere` (a phone of another space; `attachment`) or `409`
`device_change_limited` (a phone still counting in another space;
`retryAt`). See [Space devices](#space-devices).

Extra fields sit beside `error`, as `serverRevision` does for a plan
conflict:

```json
{
  "error": {
    "code": "sign_in_code_incorrect",
    "message": "This code does not match."
  },
  "attemptsRemaining": 4
}
```

A gateway in front of a sleeping service may answer `502`, `503` or `504`
without this envelope, or not in time: clients read it as `service_starting`
(« Le service démarre. Cela peut prendre une minute. »), keep the address or
the code, and try again.

### The rules of a code

- Six digits drawn from a cryptographically secure generator; valid 10
  minutes; one use.
- Never stored in clear: the service keeps a keyed digest (HMAC-SHA-256 with
  a key of the service) and compares digests in constant time.
- A code is compared five times at most, whatever the number of requests
  sent together: each comparison is reserved on the code before it is made.
  After the fifth, the code answers `sign_in_code_expired`; the link of its
  message still opens.
- Five failures for an address from one network origin lock the address
  for that origin; twenty from all origins together, within the hour, lock
  it for all. A locked address cannot have its codes typed; a new message
  cannot be asked from the locked origin. The wait lasts 15 minutes, then 1
  hour, then 24 hours when a lock comes back within 24 hours of the end of
  the previous one; a success lifts the lock and forgets it. Every failure
  is counted before the attempt is judged, so that simultaneous attempts
  cannot pass the limit together.
- A lock never holds back the link of a message: its token cannot be
  guessed, and whoever holds the message may use it. A lock voids nothing
  already sent.
- A new message at most every 60 seconds per address, 5 per hour per
  address, 20 per hour per network origin; 30 failed verifications per hour
  per network origin. A message is counted from the moment its place is
  reserved, before it leaves (a message whose sending failed is not
  counted), so that simultaneous requests send one message.
- The e-mail budget of the whole service: 20 messages a minute and 80 an
  hour by default (`ASTRO_PORTAL_MAIL_MINUTE_BUDGET`,
  `ASTRO_PORTAL_MAIL_HOURLY_BUDGET`), below the sending service's own limit,
  so that a few network origins cannot spend it for everyone unnoticed. When
  it is spent, `503` `sign_in_unavailable` with `retryAt`, the same for every
  address, and an alert in the service's log, without any address.
- A network origin is the address of the client, read by the one function
  every limit of the service counts against, in every mode, from two
  settings: `ASTRO_PORTAL_CLIENT_IP_HEADER` (required by `email-code`) and
  `ASTRO_PORTAL_TRUSTED_PROXIES`. Absent or `socket`, it is the
  connection's own address; `x-forwarded-for`, the value the trusted
  proxies appended, counted from the right (never one further left, which
  a client could have written); another header (`cf-connecting-ip`), its
  one value, which the proxy writes over the client's. A header that those
  proxies could not have written alone counts the connection's address
  instead. An IPv4 address counts whole, an IPv6 address by its /64, an
  IPv4-mapped address as its IPv4; anything that is not an address counts
  as one origin, `unknown`.
- The answers and their timing do not depend on whether the address has a
  space: the space is looked up only after a code is proven.
- The space is created at the first successful verification of an address
  without one — organisation, person without a password, owner membership,
  in one transaction. Two verifications racing for the same new address
  create one space.
- The lock of an address can be triggered by someone who knows the address
  and does not have the code; from one network origin it holds back only
  that origin, and from many origins it holds back the codes, never the
  link. It never reveals or opens anything.

### The message and its link

The message (French, English or Japanese, after `language`) has the code in
its subject (« Votre code de connexion : 481 207 »), the code in large
digits, a button « Ouvrir PlanAstro » and the sentence « Si vous n'avez rien
demandé, ignorez ce message ». It has a text version and an HTML version and
loads no remote image.

The button is the link:

```
https://<service>/auth/link#v=1&token=<linkToken>&code=<code>&expires=<date-time>&lang=<language>
```

- `<service>` is the public address of the service (`space.webUrl`); the
  path is always `/auth/link`; the rest is a fragment of URL-encoded pairs
  ([`sign-in.schema.json`](sign-in.schema.json) `$defs/linkFragment`).
- **On a phone where the application is installed**, the operating system
  hands the whole address to the application: a universal link on iOS and a
  verified App Link on Android, both declared for the path `/auth/link` of
  the service's host (the service then serves
  `/.well-known/apple-app-site-association` and
  `/.well-known/assetlinks.json`, with the identifiers of the application).
  The application uses the link only when it has itself a request in
  progress on this device, for the address the person typed: it reads
  `token` and calls `POST /v1/sign-in/verify` with `linkToken` and that
  address. Without a request in progress it ignores the token and opens its
  sign-in screen.
- **Anywhere else**, the browser opens `/auth/link`: a static page that reads
  the fragment in the browser and shows « Votre code de connexion », the
  code, and how long it stays valid — nothing else. It opens no session.
- **A visit is not a use.** The fragment never reaches the service: a mail
  scanner that fetches the link sends neither the token nor the code, and
  the service answers the same page to every visit, reading and writing
  nothing.

### The web pages

Where spaces are open, `/auth/sign-in` is the web sign-in (address, then
code) and `/auth/link` the page of the link. The installation of a private
server (`/auth/bootstrap`), its sign-in by password (`/auth/login`), its
recovery codes (`/auth/recovery`) and their routes (`/api/auth/bootstrap/*`,
`/api/auth/login`) answer `404` there. A private server signing in by
e-mail code has `/auth/sign-in`, `/auth/link`, `/auth/bootstrap` and
`/auth/recovery`; one signing in by password has `/auth/login`,
`/auth/bootstrap` and `/auth/recovery`, and its `/auth/sign-in` and
`/auth/link` answer `404` (docs/PRIVATE_NOTEBOOK_AUTH.md).

### The current space

`GET /v1/space`, with a device token or a web session, answers the space of
the credential and the calling device (`null` for a web session):

```json
{
  "space": {
    "id": "4ec83832-fbb7-4f8d-91ca-8c7eefd8ab71",
    "email": "vous@exemple.fr",
    "webUrl": "https://portal.example.test"
  },
  "device": {
    "id": "6daf99d9-1c14-4206-bae2-3cec9456a714",
    "name": "iPhone 16 Pro"
  }
}
```

## Space devices

The devices of a space. The normative shapes are
[`space-devices.schema.json`](space-devices.schema.json); the recorded
exchanges are `fixtures/api/space-devices.json`, replayed against the
service by its tests.

- **The device registers itself at sign-in**, under its name; « Mes
  appareils » lists it (`GET /v1/devices`, either credential; `current`
  marks the caller; Astro Sync appears with `application: "sync"`),
  renames it (`PATCH /v1/devices/{deviceId}` `{ "name": … }`) and detaches
  it (`DELETE /v1/devices/{deviceId}`, `204`, either credential, CSRF for a
  session). A device may detach itself: that is « Détacher cet appareil »,
  the only way out of a space on a device; there is no separate sign-out.
  The list holds the attached devices only: the caller first, PlanAstro
  before Astro Sync, then the most recently seen first.
- **The same device signing in again** to the same space (the same
  `installationId`) keeps its row, its identifier and the name the person
  gave it, and receives a new token: the previous token stops working
  (`401` `device_authentication_required`, not `device_detached`).
- **Where spaces are open to any address**, the devices follow further
  rules: a phone belongs to one space at a time, signing it in to another
  one is answered `409` `device_attached_elsewhere` and attached by
  `POST /v1/sign-in/attach`, and it changes space once per 24 hours at most
  (`409` `device_change_limited`, `retryAt`). They are the hosted service's
  and are described in its part of the contract (`v1-hosted.md`, « Devices
  of many spaces »). A private server has one space and none of them:
  `device.proof` is ignored, no attach token exists there (any token
  answers `400` `sign_in_code_expired`), and a device that detaches itself
  may sign in again at once.
- **A detached device** (from the web, from another device, by itself, or
  attached elsewhere) receives `401` `device_detached` on its next call, on
  every route that reads a bearer (`GET /v1/space` included), and erases
  its copy of that space; plans made without a space stay.
- **Astro Sync** signs in the same way (`application: "sync"`) but is not a
  device under the rule: it may be signed in to several spaces (once per
  space), is never refused, never limited and never announced to another
  space. It only sends. It is listed, renamed and detached like any device.
- **Pairing** by code (`/v1/devices/pairings`, `/v1/devices/claim`) does not
  exist where spaces are open (`404` `not_found`): it would attach a device
  around the rule.
- `PATCH` and `DELETE` of a device outside the space, or already detached,
  answer `404` `device_not_found`; `PATCH` with a name that is empty or
  longer than 120 characters, `400` `invalid_request`.
- **Which build a device runs**: `appVersion` (`1.0.4+126`) and `osVersion`
  (`iOS 18.1`) are what its [client identity](#client-identity) header last
  named, `null` until it sends one. Additive: a client ignores them when
  absent.

## Client identity

The mobile application and Astro Sync say which build makes a request, on
every request to their portal (never to anyone else), with one header:

```
X-PlanAstro-Client: planastro-ios/1.0.4+126 (iOS 18.1)
X-PlanAstro-Client: astro-sync-macos/1.0.0+6aa28f767 (macOS 15.1)
```

- The form: `<product>/<version>[+<build>][ (<system>[ <system version>])]`.
  The product in lowercase letters, digits and hyphens (`planastro-ios`,
  `planastro-android`, `astro-sync-macos`, `astro-sync-windows`,
  `astro-sync-linux`); the version, the build (a number for the
  application, a commit for Astro Sync) and the system version in
  `[0-9A-Za-z._-]`, 40 characters at most each; the system a word of
  letters (`iOS`, `Android`, `macOS`, `Windows`, `Linux`); the whole header
  200 characters at most.
- It names nothing personal: no identifier, no model, no language.
- The service never refuses a request for it: a value not of this form is
  ignored whole, a missing header changes nothing.
- A request authenticated by a device's bearer records `<version>[+<build>]`
  and `<system>[ <system version>]` on that device with its `lastSeenAt`
  ([Space devices](#space-devices)); a request that names none keeps what
  was recorded. Where devices without a space exist
  ([Anonymous devices](#anonymous-devices)), theirs are recorded the same
  way.

## Anonymous devices

A space serves the personal data
alone — synchronized plans, the journal, captures. Whatever the service gives
without touching them is open to the application without a space.

A phone with no space may hold an identity of its own, a bearer that starts
with `anon_`, carries no tenant and no user, and is sent as
`Authorization: Bearer anon_…`. A server that gives no such identity (a
private server) knows no `anon_` bearer: it is an unknown one there.

Every route of a space answers such a token `401`
`device_authentication_required`, as an unknown bearer. The public reads
ignore it (read as no credential). Where such identities exist, the routes
below take it:

| Route                                     | What the anonymous device gets                                                             | Its caps                                      |
| ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------- |
| `GET /v1/capabilities`                    | The free profile.                                                                          | —                                             |
| `GET /v1/places/climate-trend`            | The trend, once `planner.mosaic` is granted to it (`403` `capability_required` otherwise). | 120 an hour per device, 600 per origin        |
| `GET /v1/sky-quality/map/{z}/{x}/{y}.png` | The tile, once `planner.mosaic` is granted to it (`403` `capability_required` otherwise).  | 1,200 an hour per device, 3,000 per origin    |
| `GET`/`POST /v1/app-logs`                 | The error logs, under the diagnostic token as for a space's device.                        | 30 batches an hour per device, 120 per origin |

Every cap answers `429` with `Retry-After` and the route's own code
(`climate_trend_rate_limited`, `sky_light_map_rate_limited`,
`app_log_limit`). A space's device and session keep their own limits and
are not counted here.

## Deleting a space

`DELETE /v1/space` with `{ "confirm": true }`, either credential (CSRF for a
session), from inside the application or from the web. `202`
`{ "deletion": { "requestedAt", "completesBy" } }`: every device
and session of the space is detached at once, and the erasure of its plans,
sessions, captures, logs, address and settings completes by `completesBy`. Any later
call with a credential of the space answers `401` `space_deleted`, and the
application erases its copy. Shapes:
[`space-deletion.schema.json`](space-deletion.schema.json); exchanges:
`fixtures/api/space-deletion.json`.

- A body other than `{ "confirm": true }` answers `400` `invalid_request`
  and erases nothing; a session without its CSRF token answers `403`
  `csrf_invalid`. On a private server the route answers `404` `not_found`:
  the space belongs to the instance.
