- For a web session, the `202` also expires the session cookie and the
  cookie of the forms' protection token (CSRF), as signing out does.
- A device of a deleted space answers `401` `space_deleted`, never
  `device_detached`, on every route that reads a bearer (`GET /v1/space`
  and `/v1/devices` included), while the erasure runs and after it.
- Its phone belongs to no space at once and may create or join one
  straight away: the deletion is no departure of its devices — it neither
  opens the 24 hours nor ends those an earlier departure opened — and no
  space is told that a device left it.
- Until the erasure is done (usually within seconds of the answer), the
  address of the deleted space opens nothing: a right code answers `403`
  `space_unavailable` and is not used. Once it is done, the same address
  creates a new, empty space.

## Plan writes and divergence

`If-Match` is mandatory and contains a quoted positive revision such as `"7"`.
`If-Match: "0"` is the sole creation form: it succeeds only when the
tenant-scoped server UUID in the path does not already exist. A successful
update increments the revision and records the prior payload as `history`, and
re-derives the plan's `nightStartAt` and `nightEndAt` from the payload that
becomes current — see
[The night boundaries of a plan](#the-night-boundaries-of-a-plan). They are
projections of the payload, so moving a plan to another date or another place
moves them with it; a client never sends them and never needs to.

A stale write never overwrites the resource. It returns `409`, stores the
submitted payload as a recoverable `set_aside` version, and retains every
distinct stale submission. The `409` response body names that version's
`versionId` (see [Error envelope](#error-envelope)). A restore identifies that
immutable version with `versionId`, then creates a new plan revision. A
non-active plan is frozen and returns `plan_frozen` rather than accepting a
synchronization write.

### A finished night is carried by the plan (`closedAs`)

`PUT /v1/plans/{planId}` writes only active plans, yet a phone that has run
its night holds the plan as `executed` or `partial` until the debriefing
decides it (« Terminé » on [Cette nuit], the plan card, the sheet of a plan
that can be resumed). So that the plan sync never turns that state back into
« Prêt », the payload carries it in the optional `closedAs`
(`executed`, `partial`, `abandoned` or `archived`; see
[`plan.schema.json`](plan.schema.json)):

- **A device writes it** whenever the plan it holds is in one of those
  states, and **omits it** for an active plan: that omission is how
  « Reprendre la nuit » reopens the plan on every device. Any other value is
  refused with `400` `invalid_payload`.
- **A night that is too far ahead is not closed**: a write that sets
  `closedAs` to `executed`, `partial` or `abandoned` — a change of the value
  the plan carries, or a creation — answers `409` `plan_night_not_begun`, and
  nothing is written, when the plan's night begins more than 24 hours after the
  service's clock (`nightStartAt`; without a derived night, the midnight UTC of
  the plan's date). The interfaces offer the marks once the night has begun
  (« Marquer comme exécuté », « abandonné »); the service is laxer on purpose,
  so that an execution launched in the morning of its day and finished before
  the dusk is never refused. `archived` is not a mark of a night that
  happened and is never refused. The web planner's whole-document save refuses
  the same change of `status`, naming the plan in `planIds`.
- **A night has one place**: the plans still to plan of a date — pending
  and not closed (`closedAs`) — share the place, compared on the coordinates, the
  time zone and the horizon mask like the planners do. A write (a creation, a
  change of date or of place, a restored version) that would give a date two
  places answers `409` `plan_night_place_conflict`, and nothing is written. The
  body adds `planIds` (the client ids of the plans refused), `date` and
  `nightPlace` (`{ location, plans: [{ id, name }] }`: the place the other plans
  of the night hold, and which plans). A plan that ran, was abandoned or archived
  keeps the place it was observed at and takes no part; a plan whose date and
  place are those it already has is never refused (data stored before the rule is
  not locked by it). A device that moves a night plan by plan names the plans
  that follow in the header `X-Astro-Night-Move` (their resource ids,
  comma-separated): the stored place of those plans does not count, or the first
  write of the move would always be refused. The web planner's whole-document save
  (`PUT /api/plans`) checks the document as it will be stored, so a night moved in
  one save is never refused halfway, and answers the same code with the same
  fields.
- **The plan stays active for the service**: the resource `status` is still
  `active`, the plan is not frozen, it stays writable and in the debriefing
  queue. Only the debriefing decision (or the web planner) freezes a plan,
  and then the resource `status` is the authority. **`abandoned` is the
  exception**: a night given up needs no debriefing, so the write that sets
  `closedAs` to `abandoned` (a creation, or a change of the value the plan
  carries) is also the decision « Abandonné », taken in the same transaction:
  the answer reads `status` `abandoned` and `frozenAt` set, the plan is frozen
  (the next write of it is refused `409 plan_frozen`, as for any decided plan)
  and it is never in the debriefing queue. No session is made and none is
  touched: the execution a device ran keeps its journal, which reaches the
  service by its own routes, and the night stays in the Journal as an
  abandoned night. A device that sends the `abandoned` decision afterwards
  (an older build) gets the replay of the decision (`replayed: true`); if the
  plan holds no session at all, that call also keeps the night as a finished
  session of `origin` `debrief`, with the reason and the note it carries. A
  plan that already carries `closedAs` `abandoned` while active (« Recréer la
  session » hands it back to the debriefing) is not decided again by a later
  write that leaves the value as it is. Plans closed as abandoned before this
  rule were decided once by migration 112. « Reporter » is no closure: the
  plan stays active at its new date.
- **A reader** takes the resource `status` when it is not `active`, and the
  payload's `closedAs` otherwise (`active` when absent). A device never
  answers a push by falling back to `active` over a state it holds.
- **The server keeps it**: stored and served untouched like any payload
  field; a whole-document save of a browser that does not carry the field
  (an older build) never removes it, and a stale copy is set aside by the
  revision guard as ever. When two versions of a plan are merged, `closedAs`
  follows the rule of any other field (the side that changed since the base,
  the server's copy otherwise).

### What to shoot: the acquisition lines of a plan

A plan says, per target, what to shoot (« Prise de vue par cible »). The fields are additive and optional, `schemaVersion` does not move,
and a plan without them reads « everything suggested », exactly what it shows
when the person chose nothing ([`plan.schema.json`](plan.schema.json),
[`planner-document.schema.json`](https://github.com/sqound/astro-portal/blob/main/packages/astro-contracts/schemas/planner/planner-document.schema.json)):

| Where                            | What                                                                                                                                                 | Written by                                                   |
| -------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `payload.acquisitionDefaults`    | one `acquisitionLine`: the plan's defaults, for every target that does not choose its own                                                            | the web and mobile planners, when the person chooses a value |
| `items[].acquisition`            | `{ "lines": [acquisitionLine, …] }`, 1 to 8 lines: the target's own choice; absent while nothing was chosen (« Revenir aux suggestions » removes it) | the web and mobile planners                                  |
| `launchedSchedule[].acquisition` | `{ "lines": […] }`: the EFFECTIVE lines at the launch, the suggestion resolved; never rewritten                                                      | the mobile launch (`NightSessionController.launchPlan`)      |
| `launchedSchedule[].ruleVersion` | the version of the suggestion rule that resolved them (1 to 64 characters)                                                                           | the mobile launch                                            |

An `acquisitionLine` is the line of a capture's « Intégrations »
(`capture.schema.json`), same names, same units, same bounds, plus
`binning`, so that what was planned and what was measured compare field by
field. Every field is optional; an absent one is **inherited** — the target's
line, then the item's `filter` for the filter of the first line, then the
plan's defaults, then the suggestion, which is computed on reading and never
stored:

| Field             | Value                                                                            | `null`                       |
| ----------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| `filter`          | the filter as the person names it, 1 to 40 characters, trimmed, never normalised | « no filter »                |
| `exposureSeconds` | one exposure in seconds, more than 0, at most 86 400 (`0.005` for 5 ms)          | not allowed                  |
| `gain`            | the camera's gain, or a camera body's ISO: an integer from −1000 to 100 000      | « the device's own setting » |
| `binning`         | an integer from 1 to 8                                                           | not allowed                  |
| `frameCount`      | the number of exposures, an integer from 1 to 100 000                            | not allowed                  |

- **A write** (`PUT /v1/plans/{planId}`, a version restored, the web
  planner's `PUT /api/plans`, a data import) refuses a value out of these
  bounds, or more than eight lines, or an empty list of lines, with `400`
  `invalid_payload` naming `error.field` — the value's path in the payload,
  `items[0].acquisition.lines[0].exposureSeconds` — and `error.limit` (the
  bound crossed, `null` for a type), the vocabulary of a capture line's
  `invalid_metadata`. `PUT /api/plans` answers in its own shape,
  `{ "error", "code": "invalid_payload", "field", "limit", "planIds" }`, the
  field prefixed by `plans[i].`. Nothing is written. A field a line does not
  define is dropped, not refused.
- **A reader** keeps what it understands and discards the rest: an unknown
  field, a value out of bounds, a line that is not an object, the lines
  beyond the eighth; an empty `acquisitionDefaults` or an `acquisition`
  without a line is removed. One rule, `normalizePlanAcquisition`
  (`packages/portal/src/planner/plan-acquisition.js`, re-exported by
  `packages/planner/src/plan-acquisition.js`), pinned by the
  conformance fixtures `plan-acquisition` and `plan-acquisition-refusal`.
  The service applies it on every write and every read.
- **The item's `filter` stays the truth for older readers.** When the first
  line of an item names a filter, every writer copies it into the item's
  `filter` (`""` for its `null`), and the service aligns it on every write and
  read: the first line wins. The iCalendar export, the printable sheet and an
  installed application of an earlier version, which do not know
  `acquisition`, keep showing the right filter.
- **Nothing else changes**: no capability is needed, the guards of the
  multi-night plans are not involved, the merge of two versions treats
  `acquisitionDefaults` as any field it has no row for, and the data export,
  the data import and the single-plan archive carry the fields as they are.

### Merging the two versions of a diverged plan

A divergence is resolved by writing one plan (« Deux versions de ce plan »):
its targets composed from the two versions, and every other field of the plan
chosen setting by setting. The settings are composed by one rule, shared by
the server, the web and the phone and pinned by the conformance fixture
`plan-merge-fields` (`mergePlanFields`, `portal/planner/plan-fields.js`):

- each field outside `items` that differs is a row, `equipment` split into
  `equipment.mode`, `.telescope`, `.mount`, `.camera`, `.eyepiece`,
  `.modifier`, `.filters` (compared as a set), `.restrictToEquipment` and
  `.windTolerance`; `date` carries `nightId`;
- a row's default is the side that changed since the common base (the
  revision the set-aside write was based on); when both sides changed it has
  no default and must be chosen; without a base, the more recent version;
- never a row: `view` (the writer's own), `launchedSchedule` (the server's
  copy always wins), `migrationReview`/`locationReview` (the server's copy),
  and any other field, `status` included (the side that changed since the
  base, the server's copy otherwise).

A phone composes the merged plan itself and writes it with an ordinary
`PUT /v1/plans/{planId}` whose `If-Match` is the server's revision from the
`409`. The web uses `POST /api/plans/{planId}/versions/resolve` (web session +
CSRF) with `{ "choice": "merged", "items": [...], "fields": { "<row>": "local"
| "other" } }`, `local` being the browser's set-aside version; `GET |
/api/plans/{planId}/versions` returns the `base` payload and the `newer` side
the modal needs. `fields` naming no row is refused with `400`
`invalid_plan_field`; a row both versions changed left without a choice with
`400` `plan_field_choice_required` (both list the rows in `fields`).
`keepBrowser` and `keepOther` keep the whole version they name, settings
included. **Compatibility:** a request without `fields` (a browser build
that sends none) gets the earlier behaviour — the Résultat's targets on
the live plan's own settings.

A frozen plan that already has a session refuses the write the same way and
**keeps the submission** instead of discarding it: the payload is stored as a
`proposed` session supplement and the `409` body names it with `supplementId`
and `sessionId` (see
[Late field data: session supplements](#late-field-data-session-supplements)).
This is the exact symmetry of the stale write above — `versionId` names where a
stale submission went, `supplementId` names where a late one went — and it is
what makes an application that reconnects and finds the plan frozen push
its version as a session supplement, never as a plan update. A frozen plan with **no** session keeps the bare `plan_frozen`
body unchanged: there is no journal to append the payload to, and the client
already holds it. Both bodies are described by
[`plan.schema.json`](plan.schema.json); a client that reads neither
`versionId` nor `supplementId` behaves exactly as before.

The persistent web planner keeps its browser `clientId` in the payload. Its
whole-document synchronization is not a v1 write protocol; server code keeps
the normalized web representation and the v1 payload/revision atomically in
sync.

## Plan deletion

`DELETE /v1/plans/{planId}` is a plan write and carries the same mandatory
`If-Match` as `PUT`: a missing header returns `428` `precondition_required`, a
malformed one — `"0"` included, since that is the creation form and never
names an existing plan — returns `400` `invalid_if_match`, and a stale
revision returns `409` `plan_conflict` with the same `serverRevision` and
`serverPayload` body a stale update returns, but without `versionId`: a
deletion carries no submitted payload to set aside. An `active` or `abandoned`
plan may be deleted at once. An `executed`, `partial` or `archived` plan is
history: it may be deleted once **no live session names it** (its sessions in
the trash or erased), and otherwise answers `403` `plan_not_deletable` with
`sessionIds`, the live sessions that hold it — a refusal of the request itself,
not a conflict of versions nor a frozen write, which an interface says as one
(« Mettez d'abord sa session à la corbeille. ») and which the resource announces
(`liveSessionId`). The Journal keeps its lines by the session, so the deletion
of such a plan never takes a line out of it. The
web planner's whole-document save refuses the same way the explicit deletion of
a stored `executed` or `partial` plan (a session depends on it), naming it in
`planIds`; an archived plan a document no longer carries is cleared as ever. A plan outside the
tenant, or already deleted, answers `404` `plan_not_found`. Success is `204`
with no body.

The deletion is soft, per the retention doctrine. The plan disappears from
`GET /v1/plans`, from `GET /v1/plans/{planId}`, and from the whole document
the web planner reads and writes; a legacy whole-document save that still
carries the deleted plan does not resurrect it. The plan's history and
set-aside versions are retained, the payload current at deletion time is
archived as one more `history` version, and
`POST /v1/plans/{planId}/versions/{versionId}/restore` brings the plan back as
a new revision. A device that writes to a deleted plan therefore receives the
usual `409` `plan_conflict` and its submission is preserved as a restorable
`set_aside` version. The plan goes to the trash for 30 days (below), then it
is erased.

`DELETE /v1/plans/{planId}` takes either credential: a paired device, or the
web session of « Mon espace » with its CSRF token. A web session is told first
when the plan's night runs on a device: `409 plan_in_progress` with the open
`executionSessionId` and `lastSignalAt`, the last time the service heard of it
(`plan.schema.json`, `inProgressError`); `?force=true` deletes the plan anyway
and takes that open session to the trash with it, at the same instant. A
device keeps the rule it always had (neither told nor followed).

### The trash of the plans

A plan of one night or a multi-night plan the owner deletes stays **30 days**
in « Plans supprimés », restorable, then the hourly maintenance erases it —
the rule of the sessions ([Deleting a session](#deleting-a-session)). The
shapes are in [`plan.schema.json`](plan.schema.json) (`deletedList`,
`deletedPlanEntry`, `deletedProjectEntry`, `restoreResponse`, `eraseResponse`)
and [`project.schema.json`](project.schema.json) (`trashResponse`,
`restoreResponse`, `eraseResponse`). Nothing here is held by a right. The
routes of the trash answer the web session alone (a device's token is refused
`403 web_session_required`); a device deletes a plan by `DELETE` as before.

- **`GET /v1/plans/deleted`** lists the plans of one night (`plans`, each with
  `deletedAt`, `purgeAt`, `daysRemaining` and the `projectId` of the
  multi-night plan it went with, else `null`) and the multi-night plans
  (`projects`), the most recently deleted first; `graceDays` is 30.
- **`POST /v1/plans/{planId}/restore`** brings a plan of the trash back: `200
{ plan }`, its revision up by one, so that a device's stale copy conflicts
  instead of overwriting it. A plan still to run meets the rules of a new plan:
  `409 plan_limit_reached`, `409 plan_night_place_conflict` (another plan holds
  its night at another place), and it stays in the trash. The open session that
  went with it comes back; a link to a multi-night plan that is no longer live
  is taken out of its items.
- **`POST /v1/plans/{planId}/erase`** erases a plan of the trash at once: its
  items, framings and versions go; a session, a capture or an upload that still
  names it keeps what it is and loses the plan. `409 plan_not_deleted` for a
  live plan. It cannot be undone.
- **`POST /v1/projects/{projectId}/trash`** — « Supprimer ce plan multi-nuits »
  — sends the multi-night plan to the trash with **its nights still to come**
  (its plans of one night not run: `active` or `abandoned`, with no session),
  listed in `planIds`. Every **night already run** — a plan with its session, a
  session attached by itself — is **detached**: it stays in the Journal as an
  ordinary session, with its integrations (`detachedSessionCount`). When a
  night of it runs on a device it answers `409 project_in_progress` (with
  `planIds`); `{ "force": true }` deletes it anyway, that night's open session
  with it. `DELETE /v1/projects/{projectId}` still archives.
- **`POST /v1/projects/{projectId}/restore`** brings it back with the plans that
  went with it (`plansRestored`; one whose night is taken since, or beyond the
  plan limit, stays in the trash: `plansKept`) and attaches again the nights it
  was detached from, those still free.
- **`POST /v1/projects/{projectId}/erase`** erases it at once with the plans
  that went with it and its integrations; its image stays in the captures.

**The devices.** `GET /v1/plans` carries, additively, `deletedPlanIds`: the
plans of the space deleted on any surface (in the trash, or deleted before it
existed). A device drops its copy of such a plan, unless it holds a local edit
not sent yet; a plan restored is in `plans` again, and an erased one is no
longer named. A plan in the trash is in neither list of a device: it is
restored on the web only. A plan deleted before the trash existed stays the
hidden row it was: never listed in the trash, restored nor erased.

**The signals.** A deletion, a restoration and an erasure emit `plans` (and
`projects` for a multi-night plan, `sessions` when a session moves with it,
`framings` when the plan held some), and record an audit event
(`plan.restored`, `plan.erased`, `project.deleted`, `project.restored`,
`project.erased`).

### The history of a plan

A plan a debriefing froze is **history**: `PUT` refuses it (`409
plan_frozen`), `DELETE` refuses it (`403 plan_not_deletable`). What the owner
may still do is archive it, give it back, and make its session again. The shapes
are in [`plan.schema.json`](plan.schema.json) (`archiveResponse`,
`recreateSessionResponse`, and `sessionMissing` and `deletedSessionId` of the
`resource`). None of it is gated by a right.

- **« Session manquante ».** A plan a debriefing froze (`executed`, `partial`,
  `abandoned`, or archived after one) that holds **no live session** — it was
  deleted, or erased — carries `sessionMissing: true` in `GET /v1/plans` and
  `GET /v1/plans/{planId}`, and `deletedSessionId` when the deleted session can
  still be restored (the one deleted last). A plan with a live session, and a
  plan never debriefed, carry neither. The plan itself is unchanged: it is still
  « exécuté », archivable, and in « Exécutés » of the list.
- **`POST /v1/plans/{planId}/session`** — « Recréer la session à partir du
  plan » — makes the session again: `201 { plan, session }`. The plan goes back
  to the debriefing: it is **active and not frozen** again, its debriefing is
  erased (it lived with the deleted session) and its closure rides in
  `payload.closedAs` (`executed`, `partial` or `abandoned`), **as on a phone
  that ran the night**, so that every list still shows it « exécuté »; its
  revision goes up by one and the payload it had is kept in its history. The
  session is **finished**, of `origin` `debrief`, carries **no event**, and is
  « À débriefer » (`outcome: null`): its night is the plan's, and the debriefing
  — the web's, the phone's — decides it again, like any other. The plan is in
  the debrief queue again. A deleted session of the plan stays deleted (and can
  no longer be restored: the plan holds the new one). The signals are `plans`,
  `sessions` and `debrief`. Refusals: `404 plan_not_found`; `409
plan_not_debriefed` (the plan was never frozen by a debriefing: nothing to
  make again); `409 plan_archived` (give it back first); `409 session_exists`
  with the `sessionId` (a plan holds one session — a second call is therefore a
  `409` that names the session made by the first).
- **`POST /v1/plans/{planId}/archive`** archives a plan **the service froze**:
  `200 { plan }`, the `status` `archived`, the revision up by one, the payload,
  the debriefing and the session **untouched**. The status the plan had is kept
  (`plans.archived_from`) and the **session still reads its outcome**
  (`executed`, `partial` or `abandoned`): archiving a plan never turns its
  session back into « À débriefer ». Idempotent. This is the route of the
  phone's « Archiver ce plan » on a plan that is history: `PUT` writes
  active plans only, so before it the archive stayed on the device. An **active**
  plan is not archived here but by writing it with `closedAs: "archived"` (`409
plan_not_frozen`). The web planner archiving a debriefed plan keeps the
  status the same way. A full-document save (`PUT /api/plans`) never brings an
  archived plan back to `executed`, `partial` or `abandoned`: such a copy
  predates the archive (the web gives an archived plan back only as planned),
  and the plan is left archived; the way back to the outcome is the route below.
  In the read-only window of an ended subscription the same save writes an
  archive (the status, its date and `archivedFrom`, nothing else the copy
  carries), as this route does; any other change of the plan is still refused
  (`403 entitlement_read_only`).
- **`POST /v1/plans/{planId}/unarchive`** — « Rétablir » — gives the plan the
  status it had (`200 { plan }`; a plan archived by the web before this had none
  recorded: `executed` if a debriefing froze it, else active). A plan that is
  not archived is returned as it is.
- **`archivedFrom`** — the plan resource says the state an archived plan had
  before its archive: `executed`, `partial` or `abandoned`, else `null` (a plan
  that is not archived, one archived while it was still planned, one archived
  before the service recorded it). An archive replaces the status, so this is
  what lets a card read « Abandonné » then « Archivé » instead of « Archivé »
  alone. It is read-only and additive: a write that carries it does not set it,
  a client that predates it ignores it, and a reader whose service does not send
  it (the field is absent, not `null`) falls back to the `archivedFrom` of the
  payload. It rides `GET /v1/plans`, `GET /v1/plans/{planId}` and the answers of
  the archive, unarchive and session routes. A plan a device archived without
  the service freezing it (`status` `active`, `closedAs` `archived`) reports the
  state the device wrote beside the closure, in `payload.archivedFrom`
  (`executed`, `partial` or `abandoned`; omitted when the plan was still planned
  or is not archived); the service stores it untouched. The web planner's
  document (`GET /api/plans`) carries the same fact, read-only, as `archivedFrom`
  on an archived plan of a closed night (absent otherwise).

### The internal plan of a guided evening (`guidedEvening`)

A phone that runs a guided evening with help to aim, a mount or a shared screen
makes a plan of its own for it, only so that the evening has a session and
events: the payload carries `guidedEvening: true` (with `blankExecution: true`)
and its `name` is the evening's title (see [`plan.schema.json`](plan.schema.json)).
That plan is written, read, deleted and restored like any other, but it is
outside the rules of a night:

- **It is never refused** by `409 plan_limit_reached` (the night of a space
  without `planner.multiple`, the numeric guard `sync.plan_limit`) nor by
  `409 plan_night_place_conflict`, and it is not counted by them: it lives the
  same night as the night's ordinary plan, at another place if need be, and is
  never named in `otherPlans`. Its session is created and finished as any other.
- **It is never in the debrief queue** (`GET /v1/plans/debrief-queue`) and is
  never asked for a debriefing; the Journal shows its session as « Soirée
  guidée » (`guidedEvening: true` on the line, `state` `no_plan`), never as a
  night to debrief, and does not list the plan alone.
- **It is not a plan of the web planner**: the whole document (`GET /api/plans`)
  leaves it out and a save of that document keeps it as it is; « Mon espace »
  lists it as « Soirée guidée · {name} ».

### A night that runs on a device

A plan whose night is carried out on a phone is **« Nuit en cours » everywhere
else**, read-only: the web planner and its plan dialog, a second phone of the
same space. The plan resource says so, additively:

- **`executionState`** — `running` (a live session of the plan is open) or
  `paused` (it is open and the last `pause` event of the session has no
  `resume` after it). **Absent means `none`**: no session of the plan is open
  (never started, finished, abandoned, debriefed). It is derived by the
  service from the session and never written: a client that sends it does not
  set it.
- **`executionSessionId`** — the open session, present exactly when
  `executionState` is.

Both ride `GET /v1/plans` and `GET /v1/plans/{planId}` (and the answers of the
archive, unarchive and session routes). The web planner's document
(`GET /api/plans`) carries the same fact as the additive, read-only
`executionByPlanId` — `{ "<plan id>": { "state": "running" | "paused",
"sessionId": "…" } }`, absent when no night runs — which a write never stores
(a document read and written back is accepted as it is).

A reader shows the plan's **`launchedSchedule`** — written once by the phone at
launch — as the frozen « Prévu » rail, and offers no edit, no deletion (the web
planner's `PUT /api/plans` answers `403 plan_not_deletable` with
`inProgressPlanIds`) and no execution of that plan. Joining the execution from
another device is not part of this: the QR sharing of a night in progress stays
the only way. The state ends with the session: finished, abandoned or
debriefed, the field is gone at the next read.

**The signal.** Opening a session of a plan, finishing it, reopening it, and
ingesting a `pause` or a `resume` event emit `plans` **and** `sessions` (a
session with no plan, and every other event, emit `sessions` alone), so that a
page or a phone that follows the plans reads the new state at once.

**When the phone sends it.**: the phone opens
the session at the launch, not at the debriefing. Right after the launch is
written on the device, and with no gesture of the person, it sends, in the
order of its offline outbox: the plan (with its `launchedSchedule`) when the
space does not hold it yet, then `POST /v1/sessions` — with no event when the
launch skipped no target — then the events as they happen, in batches a moment
after the last one, then `finish` at the end, and the debriefing after. Offline,
nothing is lost and the same order is played when the network returns; the
`clientSessionId` makes the creation replayable, so a creation sent twice is
one session.

### The main filter of the list of plans

The list of plans
has a **main filter with two values**, « À exécuter » and « Exécutés », and no
other word for what is not executed (« Non exécuté » named two sets and is gone;
the status of a night given up is « Abandonné »). One rule for the service, the
web and the phone (`planMainFilterOf`, `portal/planner/plan-main-filter.js`),
over the state a plan reads on every surface — the resource `status` when the
service froze it, else the closure a device recorded (`payload.closedAs`), else
`active`:

| State of the plan     | Main filter  | Shown by default | Badge              |
| --------------------- | ------------ | ---------------- | ------------------ |
| `active` (planned)    | `to_execute` | yes              | Planifié           |
| `executed`, `partial` | `executed`   | yes              | Exécuté, En partie |
| `abandoned`           | `executed`   | **no**           | Abandonné          |
| `archived`            | `archived`   | **no**           | Archivé            |

A plan **executed that waits for its debriefing** is in `executed`, not in
`to_execute`. A plan **abandoned** is filed under « Exécutés » too (a night given up is over, it is no longer « À
exécuter »), but, like an archived plan, it is **not shown by default**: the
default view of « Exécutés » lists the executed and partial plans only, and an
abandoned plan appears through « Statut · Abandonné », as an archived plan
(in neither main value) appears through « Statut · Archivé ».
`GET /v1/plans?filter=to_execute|executed|abandoned|archived` answers the plans
under one value (anything else is `400 invalid_request`):

- `to_execute` and `executed` are the default views of the two main values:
  neither lists an abandoned or an archived plan;
- `abandoned` and `archived` list those plans, the same way (the inclusion
  of one set is the inclusion of the other: a view that shows both asks for
  both values, or lists without `filter`);
- without `filter` the service answers every plan, as before; a reader that
  holds the plans (the phone) applies the rule above itself
  (`planMainFilterOf`, `planShownByDefault`,
  `portal/planner/plan-main-filter.js`).

The wire value of a night given up has **not changed**: it is `abandoned`
everywhere — the plan `status` and `closedAs`, the debriefing `outcome`, the
session `outcome` and the Journal `state` — and what Astro Sync reads is
therefore unchanged. « Abandonné » replaces « Non exécuté » as the word the
interfaces write for it; the state palette (planned gold, to debrief blue,
executed / partial / abandoned grey, archived dark grey) is the interfaces',
not the contract's.

## Framings

A framing is an account-wide entity, independent of the plans that reference it: a plan item
carries `framingId` and is hydrated/dehydrated at every read/write (see
`plan.schema.json`, `planItem`). The normative shape is
[`framing.schema.json`](framing.schema.json).

`GET /v1/framings` always returns the complete list (a few dozen framings at
most), archived ones included, deleted ones excluded — the same "absent means
locally deleted" rule `GET /v1/plans` already follows.

A framing's `state` is never written directly, except `archived` (a stored
gesture; `deleted` has no field, a removed row simply stops appearing). It is
computed by `framingUsage` (`packages/astro-core/src/framing-usage.ts`,
the rules of the framing states) from the plans and observation sessions
that currently reference the framing:

- `free` — no non-deleted plan references it: any write succeeds directly.
- `shared` — referenced only by plans that are not yet executed: a write
  is accepted, but the caller is expected to have already warned the person
  and to offer "modify for all these plans" or "create a copy" — the server
  itself never refuses a `shared` write.
- `locked` — referenced by at least one executed plan. A write is
  refused `409 framing_locked` UNLESS it changes only `name` (renaming a
  locked framing is explicitly allowed; geometry, equipment and mosaic stay
  frozen). `DELETE` on a `locked` framing archives it instead
  (`200 { archived: true, framing }`).
- `archived` — an explicit `DELETE` on a `locked` framing. Any write other
  than `POST .../restore` is refused `409 framing_archived`.

`PUT /v1/framings/{framingId}` follows the same `If-Match` discipline as a
plan write: `"0"` creates (`201`), any other quoted positive revision updates
at that exact revision (`200`). A stale write (wrong revision) or a write that
arrives after the framing became `locked`/`archived` on another surface is
**never** discarded and never overwrites the server's version: it answers
`409` (`framing_conflict`/`framing_locked`/`framing_archived`) naming the
server's current `framing` in the body, and the convention across every
client is that this refusal becomes a **new, unattached copy** in the caller's
own library, named "<name> (copy)" — the same rule as for a manual
"Modify" on a locked framing, applied automatically after the fact. Only a session-authenticated write additionally requires CSRF, exactly
as `eitherV1WriteAccess` already does elsewhere in this document.

`DELETE /v1/framings/{framingId}` also carries `If-Match`. On a `free`
framing it deletes (`204`). On a `shared` framing it refuses
`409 framing_in_use` and names the still-referencing `pendingPlans`: the
**client**, after warning the person, removes the framing from those plans
with ordinary plan writes and then replays `DELETE`, which then succeeds. On a
`locked` framing it archives instead of deleting (`200`).

A framing's `payload.angleDegrees` is its position angle (N.I.N.A.'s
`Position Angle (East)`: the top of the frame, from north towards east), a
number in `[0, 360)`; a missing value reads as `0`, anything else outside that
range answers `400 invalid_request`. The service stores it as sent, in its
`angle_degrees` column and in the payload, and mirrors a non-zero angle at
`target.framing.angleDegrees` (where the exports and the previews read it);
a `0°` framing keeps the shape it always had.

Creating, updating or restoring a **mosaic** framing (`target.framing.mosaic`
set) requires the `planner.mosaic` capability, exactly as the planner UI
greys the mosaic section out; a framing of a single field
never does, and reading the library never does — a
profile that loses the capability keeps every mosaic it already made (no
data loss on downgrade), it can simply no longer write one.

## Projects

A **project** is the unit of intention that sits above the
night plans: one target or one saved framing, an integration goal, and the
snapshot of what a new night inherits — place, horizon mask, equipment,
constraints. « Project » is an internal name, in the code and the contracts;
an observer always reads « plan multi-nuits » (« multi-night plan »,
「複数夜のプラン」), and the plans of a project are « les nuits » of that
multi-night plan. The normative shape is
[`project.schema.json`](project.schema.json).

**The night plan stays the unit of execution.** A project never contains a
plan. The link is carried by the interval: a plan item may carry `projectId`
(`plan.schema.json`, `planItem`), so one night can serve two projects, or a
project and a free target. The server stores and serves `projectId` on every
plan path — `PUT /v1/plans/{planId}`, the web planner's `PUT /api/plans`, a
version restore, the debriefing and its queue — and checks it when it
**appears or changes** on an item, with
the rules of `POST /v1/projects/{projectId}/nights`: `403
multi_night_not_active` (`capability: "planner.mosaic"`) without « Planification
avancée » — and on every private server — `404 project_not_found` for a
multi-night plan the space does not hold, `409 target_mismatch` for an item
that is not of its target; a night belongs to one multi-night plan at most,
so a new link moves it; taking the link away detaches (it needs the same
right, and is idempotent). The check comes after the revision check: a stale
copy is still `409 plan_conflict`. A write that leaves every `projectId` as it
was is never checked, so a client without the capability, or an older one,
shows a normal plan and writes the field back as it read it. In the read-only
window of an ended subscription only such a link write passes, and only for a
space that still holds « Planification avancée » by a purchase that does not expire.

**A project carries no progress.** Acquired minutes, losses and the nights
still to place are computed from the debriefings of the plans whose items
name the project; nothing about progress is stored on it.

**Paid, read everywhere.** Creating or updating a project requires
`planner.mosaic` — the capability of the mosaics, deliberately shared so that
the capability list read by the mobile and by Astro Sync does not change.
Reading never requires it: a space that loses the capability keeps reading
every project it made (no data loss on a downgrade), and archiving one stays
allowed — the person tidying their own data. A write during the read-only
window of an ended subscription answers `403 entitlement_read_only`.

**A service without projects.** The open-source composition, and a private
server, register no projects service: the multi-night plans a space already
holds (brought by [Data import](#data-import)) are read-only there.
`GET /v1/projects` answers `200 { projects }` with them (`[]` when the space
holds none), `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}` the one asked for, or `404
project_not_found`; `PUT` and `DELETE` answer `403 capability_required` with
`capability: "planner.mosaic"`, and nothing is written. The routes exist,
with the same authentication, so a client never has to tell the two kinds of
service apart.

### The resource

```json
{
  "id": "5d0c6b1e-2f4a-4c8e-9b7d-3a1f0e6c2b90",
  "revision": 3,
  "status": "active",
  "target": {
    "targetId": "ngc7000",
    "name": "NGC 7000",
    "commonName": "Nébuleuse de l’Amérique du Nord"
  },
  "goalMinutes": 720,
  "place": {
    "name": "Col des Mélèzes",
    "latitude": 45,
    "longitude": -30,
    "timeZone": "UTC",
    "horizonMask": { "enabled": true, "points": ["… 36 points …"] }
  },
  "equipment": { "telescopeId": "lunette-72", "cameraId": "couleur" },
  "constraints": {
    "minAltitude": 30,
    "moonSeparationMinimum": 25,
    "moonSeparationMinimumByFilter": { "Ha/OIII": 15 }
  },
  "createdAt": "2026-06-20T19:40:00.000Z",
  "updatedAt": "2026-09-19T08:12:00.000Z"
}
```

- `target`: exactly one of `targetId` (a catalogue object) or `framingId` (a
  saved framing), with `name` and an optional `commonName`.
- `goalMinutes`: the integration goal, a whole number of minutes from 30 to
  6 000 (100 h), one goal for the whole target (a per-filter goal comes
  later, additively).
- `place.horizonMask` is written back on the planner's 36-point grid.
- `equipment` is the plan's equipment object, stored as given.
- `status`: `active`, `finished` or `archived`.

### The routes

`GET /v1/projects` returns the complete list, archived projects included,
most recently updated first, the same « absent means gone » rule as
`GET /v1/plans` and `GET /v1/framings` — nothing is deleted by these routes,
so nothing is absent once listed, except a multi-night plan the owner sent to
the trash from the web ([The trash of the plans](#the-trash-of-the-plans)).

`PUT /v1/projects/{projectId}` takes the body
`{ status?, target, goalMinutes, place, equipment, constraints }`
(`project.schema.json`, `write`) and follows the `If-Match` discipline of a
plan write: `"0"` creates (`201`), any other quoted positive revision updates
at exactly that revision (`200`), and each write moves the revision by one.
`status` is optional: absent, a creation is `active` and an update keeps the
current status (an archived project stays archived); `active` or `finished`
sets it, which is also how an archived project comes back. A write at a stale
revision is **never** applied and never discarded silently: it answers
`409 project_conflict` with the server's current `project`, for the client to
merge or to offer to the person. An identifier already taken — in this space
or in another — answers `409 invalid_creation_revision`, the same both ways;
a multi-night plan of this space in the trash at that identifier is erased and
replaced (see « Identifiers are global »).
A missing `If-Match` answers `428 precondition_required`; a malformed one
`400 invalid_if_match`; a body outside the schema `400 invalid_request`.

`DELETE /v1/projects/{projectId}` **archives**, never deletes:
`200 { archived: true, project }`. It carries `If-Match` like a write (`428`,
`400`, `409 project_conflict` with the `project`); archiving an archived
project answers the same `200` without a new revision. The nights stay
ordinary plans, their items keep their `projectId`, and the project stays
readable.

Every successful write and archive emits the change-signal domain `projects`
and records an audit event (`project.created`, `project.updated`,
`project.archived`).

### Integrations of a multi-night plan

Astro Sync integrates
each night of a multi-night plan by **family** of frames — filter, exposure,
gain, binning, temperature — and sends **one line per night, target and
family**: an **integration**
([`integration.schema.json`](integration.schema.json)). It is metadata only,
never a file: the masters stay on the computer that made them, and the image
of the plan is made there too, then sent as a capture of role `plan`
([uploads.md](uploads.md), « The image of a multi-night plan »).

`GET /v1/projects/{projectId}/integrations` (either credential) answers
`200 { projectId, integrations, totalsByFilter, totalSeconds, nightCount }`:
the integrations, the earliest night first, and their totals by filter in the
order the filters first appear (`filter` as the frames name it, never
normalised; `null` is « Sans filtre »). **Reading never needs a capability**,
on any service: a space whose subscription ended, or a private server holding
what « Importer mes données » brought, reads its integrations as they are.
`404 project_not_found` for a project the space does not hold.

`PUT /v1/projects/{projectId}/integrations` (either credential, CSRF for the
web) takes `{ integrations: [ … ] }`, one to 200 lines (`$defs.write`:
`nightId`, `targetId`, `filter`, `exposureSeconds`, `gain`, `binning`,
`temperatureC`, `frameCount`, `eveningDate`, `sensor`, `computerLabel`,
`installationId`), and answers `200` with the same body as `GET`. It is
**idempotent**: a line is keyed by its night, its target and its family;
sending it again replaces it (its `frameCount`, `sensor`, computer…), never
adds a second one. `totalSeconds` is computed by the service
(`frameCount × exposureSeconds`), never sent. The lines are all checked before
one is written.

**Sending a night complete (`replace`).** Replacing by key never removes a line:
a night stacked again under another filter would leave the line of the earlier
filter in the plan, counted beside the new one. A body may therefore carry
`replace: [{ nightId, targetId }]` (1 to 200 sets) beside `integrations`: each
listed set — everything one night holds for one target — is sent **complete**.
In one transaction the service writes the lines received, then removes the
**measured** lines this plan holds for each listed set whose family (filter,
exposure, gain, binning, temperature) is not among them. The declared lines
(`source: "declared"`) are the service's own and are not removed; when a set
is left without any measured line, the declared line of its night is computed
again. `integrations` then holds 0 to 200 lines (none empties the sets); every
line must belong to one of the listed sets and a set is listed once, else
`400 invalid_request`. The night of each set is checked like the night of a
line (`404 session_not_found`, `409 integration_night_not_in_project`), whatever
the number of lines, and the capability gate is the same. The statistics (the
totals by filter, the target and instrument statistics, the plan's page) are
computed from the stored lines when they are read, so they follow at once.

The body **without** `replace` is the earlier form, unchanged: it adds or
replaces its lines and removes none, so a client that predates `replace`
never erases the lines of a night it did not send. A `replace` body sent to a
service that predates it is answered `400 invalid_request` (unexpected
property); a client then sends the earlier form. Astro Sync sends the
complete form for every night it sends, but never an empty set: a master
folder it cannot read (a disk that is not mounted) is not a night without
masters, and would otherwise wipe the plan. The bodies are in
`fixtures/integrations-put-bodies.json`.

- `403 multi_night_not_active`, with `capability: "analysis.progression"`:
  the multi-night plans of Astro Sync are part of « Synchronisation
  avancée+ » (`analysis.progression`, no capability of their own); a space
  without it, a subscription in its read-only window, and **every private
  server** are refused, whatever their profile says: the integrations stay readable,
  none is added.
- `409 integration_outside_night`, with the line's `nightId` and the night's
  own `eveningDate`: `eveningDate` is not the date of the night `nightId`
  (its plan's date, else the evening of its start at the plan's longitude,
  local noon to local noon, never a time zone). No capability lifts it.
- `404 session_not_found`: the night is not one of the space's;
  `404 project_not_found`; `400 invalid_request` for a body outside the
  schema.

`GET /v1/projects/{projectId}` (the project read alone, never the list) also
carries `integrations`, `totalsByFilter`, `planCapture` (the newest image of
the plan: its capture `id`, `createdAt`, `combinedNightIds`, `totalsByFilter`,
`render`; `null` before the first) and `planCaptureCount`. The progress of the
plan stays computed from the debriefings: the integrations never change it.

### Nights of a multi-night plan

A night already run — the
three nights of the Heart run « sans plan multi-nuits » — or a plan of one
night is **attached** afterwards to a multi-night plan of its target,
**detached**, or brought into a **new** multi-night plan « à partir de plans
déjà existants ». The full rules are in [projects.md](projects.md); in short:

- **One truth.** The link stays on the plan item of the target
  (`planItem.projectId`), the field every surface already reads; only a night
  with no plan item of the target (a night run without a plan, a target met in
  the field) carries it itself. A night belongs to one multi-night plan at
  most: attaching it elsewhere moves it.
- `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}/nights` answers
  `{ projectId, nights, candidates }` (`project.schema.json`, `nights`): the
  nights of the plan and the nights of the journal and plans of one night of
  the same target that are not among them. Never gated.
- `GET /v1/projects/candidates?targetId=…` (or `?framingId=…`) answers
  `{ candidates }` (`project.schema.json`, `candidates`): the nights of the
  journal and the plans of one night of a target that has **no multi-night
  plan yet**, each with the other multi-night plan it already belongs to
  (`projectId`, or `null`); what « À partir de plans déjà existants » lists
  before `POST /v1/projects` creates the plan. Never gated.
- `POST /v1/projects/{projectId}/nights` takes `{ sessionIds?, planIds? }`
  (`nightsWrite`) and attaches them all, or none: `409 target_mismatch` (with
  the `sessionId` or `planId`) for another target, `404 session_not_found` /
  `plan_not_found`.
- `DELETE /v1/projects/{projectId}/nights/{nightId}` detaches a night of the
  journal, or a plan of one night by its own id; idempotent;
  `404 night_not_found`.
- `POST /v1/projects` (`create`) creates a multi-night plan without `If-Match`,
  with `fromSessionIds` / `fromPlanIds` to attach in the same request (all
  checked before anything is created); `201` with its `nights`.
- **Read everywhere.** Attaching, detaching and creating from existing
  nights answer `403 multi_night_not_active` (`capability:
"planner.mosaic"`) without « Planification avancée » (in the read-only
  window only a purchase that does not expire counts), and on every private
  server; reading never needs a capability. The
  integrations of a plan and its image keep `analysis.progression`.
- **The integrations follow the night**: attached, its integrations of the
  target count for the plan; detached, they leave it.
- `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}` also lists `nights`; `GET /v1/projects`
  takes `?sessionId=` or `?planId=` to keep the multi-night plans that night,
  or that plan, belongs to.

**The sessions of a multi-night plan.** The
plan's page is **one page** that gathers what is still to come and what is
done. Each night of `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}/nights` and of the project
read alone gains `state`: `upcoming` (a promise: a plan not run yet, or a
session still open or paused), `to_debrief` (the night is over and its
debriefing is still to be done), `done` (debriefed, or a finished session
without a plan); the project read alone gains `allSessionsDone` (no night
`upcoming` or `to_debrief`, at least one `done`). A session attached
afterwards (above) joins the plan's sessions and the Journal's group like any
other.

**Declared and measured lines.** An integration line gains `source`
(`declared` | `measured`; absent = `measured`) and `sessionId` (the same value
as `nightId`). A night attached to a multi-night plan gets, at its debriefing
(`executed`, `partial`) or when it is attached afterwards, one `declared` line
per target of the plan (family key `declared`); Astro Sync's `PUT` for that
night and target **replaces** the declared line by its measured ones. **Reading
the declared lines needs no capability** (any profile, any private server);
the measured lines' `PUT`, and the plan image, keep `analysis.progression`.
`totalsByFilter` and the answer say what is declared and what is measured
(`declaredSeconds`, `measuredSeconds`, adding up to `totalSeconds`).
`PUT /v1/projects/{projectId}/integrations` refuses a night that does not
belong to the plan: **`409 integration_night_not_in_project`** (with
`nightId`), after the capability gate and before `integration_outside_night`.

## Shared catalogue and favorites

`GET /v1/targets` accepts the query parameters, filters, pagination and result
shape of the web portal's target library: `locale`, `q`, `type`,
`constellation`, `catalogue`, `selection`, `mode`, `hemisphere`, `season`,
`offset`, `limit`, `featured`, `favorites`, and `favoriteMode`, plus the
ordering parameters `sort`, `latitude` and `minimumAltitude` below. Anonymous responses are
publicly cacheable. When a valid device bearer is present every entry of
`targets` additionally carries `photoFavorite` and `visualFavorite` for the
device owner, the response is `no-store`, and `favorites=1` restricts the
search to that owner's favorites — optionally narrowed to one mode by
`favoriteMode`. `favorites=1` without a device bearer is `401`
`device_authentication_required`.

The result envelope's `counts` (`{ total, best, extended }`), `catalogueOptions[].count` and
`typeOptions[].count` are **faceted**: each describes the option group's own
dropdown (selection, catalogue, type respectively) with every _other_ active
filter applied but that group's own filter ignored, so picking
`selection=best` never changes `counts.extended`, and picking a `catalogue`
never changes `typeOptions[].count` for a type that has nothing to do with
it. A portal predating this fix instead filtered `counts` by the active
`selection` too, so `selection=best` collapsed `total`/`best`/`extended` onto
the same, already-`best`-restricted number — the bug this facet rule fixes.
`countsUnfiltered` (`{ total, best, extended }`) and each catalogue/type
option's `total` are additive, `n`-of-`x/n` companions: the same counts with
_no_ filter applied at all, constant for a given catalogue revision. A client
predating these fields simply never reads them and falls back to the single
number `counts`/`catalogueOptions[].count`/`typeOptions[].count` already
carried, exactly as before.

The two editorial selections are disjoint (TARGET-SELECTIONS-TAGS-01):
`selection=best` is « Essentielles », `selection=extended` « Étendues », the
targets beyond the Essentials — an Essential is not Extended (`isBest` and
`isExtended` are never both true). Each target of a selection carries `modes`,
the modes it is proposed in (`["visual", "photo"]` or a subset, in that order;
both for a target of a selection that carries none; empty out of the
selections). `mode` (`visual` | `photo`; absent or empty: any) keeps, of the
`selection` asked, the targets of that mode, and `counts.best`/`.extended` and
`countsUnfiltered` count each list in it; without a `selection` it filters
nothing: a catalogue is whole. Any other `mode` is `400` `invalid_request`.

Results are ordered by designation. `sort=notoriety` asks instead for the
order of the planner's catalogue screens, independent of the observer and of
the night: the editorial selection first (Essentielles, then Étendues, then
the other targets), inside a selection the rank of the target's best-placed
catalogue (Système solaire, Messier, Caldwell, NGC, IC, Sharpless, Barnard,
LBN, Abell, LDN), then the designation, numerically (`NGC 891` before
`NGC 7000`). It is total and stable, drops nothing, and takes neither
`latitude` nor `minimumAltitude`. `sort=planner` asks instead for the web
planner's own relevance ranking — `plannerCandidateScore`: editorial tier,
object type, brightness, apparent size and culmination height, with the
designation order as the tiebreak — so that one bounded page is the page worth
planning against rather than whichever designations sort first. It accepts two
optional observer parameters, `latitude` (degrees, `-90`..`90`) and
`minimumAltitude` (degrees, `0`..`89`, default `30`); with no latitude the
ranking simply carries no visibility term. A target the score cannot rank (no
declination, or a culmination below `minimumAltitude`) is excluded, so `total`
and every counter above (`counts`, `countsUnfiltered`, `catalogueOptions`,
`typeOptions`) describe the ranked set only — under `sort=planner` a target
the observer's site can never see does not inflate `n` either. `latitude` is
an observing location in a query string: clients send it coarsely — the
mobile planner rounds to the whole degree — because the score needs no
better.

Every response echoes the ordering it applied in `sort` (`"designation"`,
`"planner"` or `"notoriety"`). A portal predating the parameter omits the field and answers by
designation whatever was asked, which is how a client detects that it must
fall back to ranking locally. An unknown `sort`, or a `latitude` or
`minimumAltitude` outside its range, is `400` `invalid_request`.

`GET /v1/targets/{targetId}` returns the same target knowledge as the web
portal's planner target route, with the same two favorite flags added for a
paired device.

A knowledge text is never blank. When the requested `locale` has no curated
text for a target, `story` and `storyParagraphs` are the English ones and the
target carries `storyFallbackLocale: "en"` (the language they are written
in); the field is absent when the text is in the requested locale. Names
follow the same rule without a flag: a type or an object name the locale has
no word for reads the English one, and a constellation its English or Latin
name.

A target with a published page (the long catalogue texts, ten languages)
also carries `storySections`, the same text cut at its intertitles
(`[{ heading, paragraphs }]`, the opening having a `null` heading), and
`researchNote`, the note on the licences of its sources; `researchSources`
are then written in the language of the text. `storyParagraphs` stays the
whole text without the intertitles, for a client that shows paragraphs only.

The target knowledge may carry two optional spoken texts for the read-aloud
voice, beside the written story: `spokenParagraphs`, the same facts written
to be heard (short sentences, numbers and names written as they are said),
and `childrenSpokenParagraphs`, the children's variant. They are present only
in a locale the spoken templates are written for, and never beside
`storyFallbackLocale`. A reader without them speaks the written text through
its pronunciation lexicon; which text is spoken is the `spoken-text-choice`
rule (`packages/portal/src/spoken-text.js`).

## Compact target index

`GET /v1/target-index` is the same shared catalogue, with the same filters,
the same orderings, the same pagination parameters and the same
`400 invalid_request` refusals, projected down to what a planning candidate
needs. A target presented in full is roughly 7 kB — the knowledge article, the
observing guide's prose, every image credit — which is right for a catalogue
page and wrong for a planner that needs several hundred candidates and reads
almost none of it. This route omits all three; a client showing one target
fetches it through `GET /v1/targets/{targetId}`, which is what it already did.
The catalogue's own attribution and the image sets' attributions are not
per-target and stay in the envelope.

The notation is columnar. `fields` states the column names once, and each
target is a positional array in `rows`, in that order, so no key name is
repeated across the page. A reader resolves a column by looking its name up in
`fields`: it ignores a column it does not know and tolerates one that is
absent, which is how a column is added later without negotiating anything. `v`
is the notation's own version and is `1`. `types` and `constellations` are
`[key, localizedLabel]` dictionaries the `type` and `constellation` columns
index into — a catalogue of several hundred targets draws on a few dozen of
each, the key is what a client's rules match on, and the label is sent because
a client cannot be assumed to localize a taxonomy key offline. `null` is the
absent value throughout, and numbers are rounded to the precision they are
useful at: 1e-4 degree or hour for coordinates, a tenth for magnitude and
apparent size.

`ra` is in catalogue **hours** and `dec` in degrees, exactly as `coordinate`
carries them in the full view, so one client-side conversion serves both.

Two columns appended later, `distanceLy` and `distanceSource`, are the target's
distance: whole light-years and the layer it comes from, both `null`
when no layer has one. The rule, applied at import in this order: `published`
(the curated table of about a hundred published values, each cited with its
reference; it wins), `parallax` (OpenNGC's Gaia parallax, `1 / Pax`, for an
open or globular cluster, a planetary nebula or a nebula at 0.5 mas or more,
never for a galaxy), `redshift` (a galaxy at 1 000 km/s or more, Hubble's law
at 70 km/s/Mpc), otherwise absent. `redshift` is a model, not a measurement:
a client that shows it writes « estimation ». A full view (`GET /v1/targets`,
`GET /v1/targets/{targetId}`) carries `distance: { lightYears, source,
reference } | null`, where `reference` is the bibliographic reference of a
`published` value; the catalogue's attribution carries `distances: true` when
some target has one, and the credit lines to write are in
`astro-core/catalogue-sources` (`distanceSourceCredits`).

The column appended last, `modes`, is the selection's modes as bits: 1 visual, 2 photo,
3 both (a target of a selection with none: 3), 0 for a target of no selection.
`best` and `extended` are disjoint.

`limit` is capped at 1000 here rather than 96. `include=extended` additionally
appends, after the page, every target of the same filtered and ranked set
that belongs to one of the two editorial selections (in `mode`, when one is
asked) that the page did not already hold: that is the web planner's own pool
— the highest-ranking candidates plus every target of the selections, so the
planner's « Étendues » (the Essentials and the Extended together) is complete
rather than truncated — obtained in one request. `rows` is then longer than `limit`, which is why the
supplement has to be asked for. `total` still describes the whole filtered,
ordered set.

`revision` identifies the catalogue the rows came from. A reader walking the
whole index page by page compares it between pages: an import landing mid-walk
changes it, and `total` and the designation order with it, so those pages must
not be stitched together.

The response carries a strong `ETag` and honours `If-None-Match` with a `304`:
the index is a pure function of the catalogue revision and the query, so a
client that already holds the page revalidates instead of downloading and
decoding it again. The `Cache-Control` policy is `GET /v1/targets`' own,
unchanged — publicly cacheable for a guest, `no-store` once a bearer makes the
answer one device's. Rows never carry `photoFavorite` or `visualFavorite`; a
paired device reads `GET /v1/favorites` and joins on the id, and `favorites=1`
restricts the index to the owner's own targets as it does on `GET /v1/targets`.

A portal predating this route answers `404`. That is the whole negotiation: a
client that gets one falls back to `GET /v1/targets` and that route's 96-entry
page cap.

## Offline pack manifest

`GET /v1/offline-pack` states what _this_ self-hosted portal can serve for an
offline pack — components, revisions, sizes and URL templates — in one round
trip, so a client never has to guess from a fixed list baked into an app
version whether an instance has a light-pollution pack raster installed or its
HiPS tile mirror enabled. A component the portal cannot currently serve is
simply absent from `components[]`; there is no separate "unavailable" flag. As
with `GET /v1/target-index`, a portal predating this route answers `404`, and a
present `Authorization` header is still an intentional device attempt: an
invalid, revoked, or non-device bearer answers `401`
`device_authentication_required`. The manifest carries nothing personal to a
device or a user — the same document is served to every caller — so it is
cached `public, max-age=60` and carries a strong `ETag`, honouring
`If-None-Match` with a `304` exactly like `GET /v1/target-index`.

The manifest is never a minute's wait. While a component is recomputed in the
background, the answer arrives within about two seconds and is the last
manifest this portal knows, marked `X-Offline-Pack-Freshness: stale` with
`Cache-Control: no-cache` (a current one carries `fresh` and
`public, max-age=60`); a client uses a stale manifest as it would any other and
asks again a little later, without saying anything worrying to the person. A
portal that has no manifest at all yet — its very first computation — answers
`503` with the v1 error envelope, `error.code` `offline_pack_preparing` (also
repeated as `code` at the top of the body, with `retryAfterSeconds`) and a
`Retry-After` header: the client keeps its wait, asks again after that many
seconds, and tells the person the service is preparing the data — never an
error. A `502`, `503` or `504` with no such code is the hosting platform's
front door while the service starts: a client waits and asks again for a
couple of minutes before saying the service does not answer.

The envelope's `catalogue` (`{ revision, targets, locales }`) mirrors
`GET /v1/target-index`'s own `revision` and `total` for the same catalogue —
comparing it costs nothing extra once a client already fetched this manifest,
instead of a dedicated `limit=1` request. `components[]` is open-ended:
`component.kind` is a string a reader dispatches on, and an entry whose `kind`
it does not recognize is ignored rather than rejected, which is what lets a
later component ship without a client version bump.

Three components exist today. `target-index` (`kind: "index-pages"`, always
`required: true`) describes the compact catalogue index of
[Compact target index](#compact-target-index) above, walked page by page in
designation order: `pageSize`, `pages`, `estimatedBytes` and
`estimatedEncodedBytes` (gzip) are measured against the real, current document,
never hand-typed. `sky-quality` (`kind: "file-set"`, always `required: true`)
is the downloadable 6' light-pollution raster and is present only when a
portal has one prepared; its `files[].checksum` and its own `revision` are
`sha256:`-prefixed digests of the actual bytes, computed on demand rather than
from a fixed list, because the file can be built lazily. `sky-backdrop`
(`kind: "hips-tiles"`, always `required: false`) is the whole-sky HiPS tile
backdrop, present unless the instance's `ASTRO_PORTAL_SKY_TILE_MIRROR`
environment variable is `off` — the lever for an instance that has not
settled its imagery licensing for public distribution. Its `tiers[]`
(`essential`/`standard`/`comfort`/`quality` today — the list is open: a client
ignores or labels generically a tier id it does not know) carry HEALPix-exact
cumulative tile counts (1,020 / 4,092 / 16,380 / 65,532 for max orders 3/4/5/6)
and a `state` of `ready` or `on-demand`, reflecting the HiPS mirror's real disk
cache: `ready` once every archive that tier is made of already exists there
(see [Sky-tile mirror](#sky-tile-mirror) below), `on-demand` otherwise. Tile
size grows with the order, so sizes are computed per order:
`meanTileBytesByOrder` (additive) gives the mean the estimate uses for each
order — the documented measurement until the mirror has itself observed enough
tiles at that order — and a tier's `estimatedBytes` is the sum over its orders
plus the real tar overhead; once a tier is `ready`, `estimatedBytes` is the
exact size of its archives on disk and `exactBytes: true` says so.
`meanTileBytes` is kept for compatibility (the weighted mean of the lowest
tier). `lowOrderArchive` (additive)
spells out the fixed cumulative archive's own address
(`{ order, chunkOrder, chunkPix, tiles }`) explicitly, for a client that would
rather read it than derive it from `archiveChunkDelta`.

Each tier also carries `archives` (additive): `{ "ready": 12, "total": 61 }`,
how many of the archives the tier is made of the mirror already has, and how
many it needs (1 / 13 / 61 / 253 for `essential` / `standard` / `comfort` /
`quality`). It is counted from the mirror's archive index — one small file, no
walk of the disk — so it is exact and cheap on every request; a tier is
`ready` exactly when `ready` equals `total`. The manifest is cached for a
minute (`max-age=60`): to follow a preparation, read its own state document
(below), not the manifest. `sky-backdrop.prepareUrlTemplate`
(additive; `"/v1/offline-pack/sky-backdrop/tiers/{tier}/prepare"`, absent from
a static pack) names the route by which a client asks the portal to build a
tier's missing archives itself: see
[Sky-backdrop preparation](#sky-backdrop-preparation).

```json
{
  "id": "comfort",
  "maxOrder": 5,
  "tiles": 16380,
  "estimatedBytes": 1150754260,
  "state": "on-demand",
  "archives": { "ready": 12, "total": 61 }
}
```

`sky-quality`'s two `files[].url` values are served by
`GET /v1/offline-pack/sky-quality/manifest.json` and
`GET /v1/offline-pack/sky-quality/raster.bin`
a strict
whitelist of exactly those two names — anything else is the same `404` as an
unavailable pack, never a path resolved from the request. Both stream from
disk with guest access identical to the manifest route above. `raster.bin`'s
`ETag` is always `"sha256-<hex>"` of the file's UNCOMPRESSED bytes — the same
digest as the component's own `revision`/`files[].checksum` — honoured by
`If-None-Match` with a `304` regardless of which encoding was negotiated; it
is served `Content-Encoding: gzip` from a precomputed sibling file when the
request's `Accept-Encoding` allows it (`Vary: Accept-Encoding` always set),
identity otherwise. Both files — like every `sky-tiles` file below — honour
one byte range over the uncompressed bytes (`206` with `Content-Range`,
`416` with `Content-Range: bytes */<size>` past the end, the whole file for
several ranges or an `If-Range` naming another version), advertise
`Accept-Ranges: bytes`, and carry `Cache-Control: no-transform` so that no
compression layer re-encodes them. The pack raster itself is a 6' block-averaged reduction
of the portal's live 2' raster — `packages/portal/src/sky-quality.js`'s
`deriveSkyQualityPackGrid`, produced either up front
(`prepare-sky-quality-raster.mjs --pack-arcminutes`) or lazily and
disk-memoized on first request, re-derived only when the installed source
raster's checksum changes; see `docs/SKY_QUALITY_RASTER.md`.

`GET /v1/favorites` returns `{ "photoTargetIds": [], "visualTargetIds": [] }`
for the device owner. `PUT /v1/favorites/{targetId}` takes
`{ "mode": "photo" | "visual", "favorite": true }` and returns the same
document. The two modes are independent. A bearer credential carries no
browser cookie, so no CSRF token applies; the audit trail is the web route's.

`POST /v1/favorites` takes `{ "action": "import", "items": [{ "targetId",
"mode": "photo" | "visual" }] }` (500 items at most): the favorites a phone kept on the device — made without a space
and sent once the device is attached to one, or ticked while offline — added
to its owner's in one transaction. It is additive and idempotent: an item
already a favorite in that mode is kept, nothing is ever removed, the same
item twice in one import counts once, and sending the same import again
changes nothing. A target the catalogue no longer has is skipped rather than
refused. The answer is the favorites by mode plus `imported`, one
`{ targetId, mode, status }` per distinct item in order, `status` being
`added`, `kept` or `unknown_target`
([`target-favorites.schema.json`](target-favorites.schema.json)
`importRequest` / `importResponse`). The device's right is that of
`PUT /v1/favorites/{targetId}`: its bearer only, and the soft-downgrade gate
of an added favorite (`403` `entitlement_read_only`).

## Sky-tile mirror

The three `GET /v1/sky-tiles/{survey}/…` routes serve the `sky-backdrop`
component's tiles and archives from this portal's own disk cache, fetching
from the CDS on a client's behalf rather than a device downloading tiles in
bulk directly from it (a self-hosted HiPS mirror). `{survey}`
is resolved only against the server-side survey table, never from the
request. Full detail — the archive-aggregation rule, the `503`
`sky_tiles_archive_building` build protocol with its `Retry-After` and
`progress`, cache behaviour, and the politeness configuration towards the CDS
— lives in [`docs/SKY_TILES_MIRROR.md`](https://github.com/sqound/astro-portal/blob/main/docs/SKY_TILES_MIRROR.md).

Every binary response carries `Content-Length` and a strong
`ETag: "sha256-<hex>"` of its exact body, honouring `If-None-Match` with a
`304`. A tile or an archive is served `Cache-Control: public,
max-age=31536000, immutable` and never revalidated once cached; `properties`
is revalidated every 30 days instead. `ASTRO_PORTAL_SKY_TILE_MIRROR=off`
answers `404` on every one of these routes, matching `sky-backdrop`'s absence
from the manifest above.

## Sky-backdrop preparation

A portal whose sky-tile mirror is empty builds an archive only when a client
asks for it — about a minute from the CDS — and stops building as soon as
nobody asks: a phone downloading a tier waits on one `503`
`sky_tiles_archive_building` after another. `POST
/v1/offline-pack/sky-backdrop/tiers/{tier}/prepare` asks the portal to build
**every archive the tier lacks, by itself**, and `GET` on the same path says how
far it got, so a client can tell the person "12 parts ready out of 61". `{tier}`
is a `sky-backdrop.tiers[].id` of the [manifest](#offline-pack-manifest), which
names the route in `sky-backdrop.prepareUrlTemplate`; a portal predating it
answers `404`, the whole negotiation — the client goes on requesting archives
one by one, as before.

Access is exactly that of the `sky-tiles` routes it feeds: none. Nothing here
is personal to a device or a user, and an `Authorization` header is ignored.
Both methods answer `404` `sky_tiles_mirror_disabled` when the mirror is off and
`404` `sky_backdrop_tier_unknown` for a tier the portal does not offer, and are
never cached (`Cache-Control: no-store`). Error bodies carry `code` and, when
there is one, `retryAfterSeconds` at their top, as the `sky-tiles` errors do.

`GET` reads the state and starts nothing. `POST` (no body) starts the
preparation of the tier, or joins the one already covering it — the same
request twice is one task — and answers at once with the same state document:
`202` while something is left to do, `200` when the tier is already `complete`
(nothing is started). A client that has just been told `idle` by a `GET`
sends the `POST` again; that is how a preparation resumes. Repeated triggers
are limited to ten `POST`s a minute per client, then `429`
`sky_backdrop_prepare_rate_limited` with `Retry-After`; reading is not limited.

```json
{
  "tier": "comfort",
  "state": "running",
  "archives": { "ready": 12, "total": 61 },
  "current": {
    "path": "/v1/sky-tiles/dss-colour/Norder4/Chunk0/11.tar",
    "order": 4,
    "chunkOrder": 0,
    "chunkPix": 11,
    "tiles": { "done": 36, "total": 256 }
  },
  "remainingSeconds": 2940,
  "remainingBasis": "measured",
  "reason": null,
  "nextAttemptAt": null,
  "runningTier": "comfort",
  "queuePosition": null,
  "retryAfterSeconds": 15,
  "startedAt": "2026-09-29T01:00:23.282Z",
  "serverTime": "2026-09-29T01:12:28.306Z"
}
```

| Field                                | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `tier`                               | The tier asked about.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `state`                              | `idle`: nothing runs for this tier — the state after every restart of the service, whatever is already on its disk; `queued`: another tier is being prepared first; `running`; `waiting`: the task is on hold, see `reason`; `failed`: it stopped, see `reason`, and a new `POST` starts it again; `complete`: every archive of the tier exists.                                                                                                                                |
| `archives`                           | `{ ready, total }`, the same count as the manifest's, read from the mirror's index.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `current`                            | The archive being built: `path` (below the API root), `order`, `chunkOrder`, `chunkPix`, and `tiles` `{ done, total }`. `null` unless `running`, `waiting` (the archive it is stuck on) or `failed`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
