| Outcome and state                    | Events emitted for that item                                                    |
| ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `executed` (no adjustments are read) | `target_started`, then `target_finished`                                        |
| `partial`, `A.state = "done"`        | the same two                                                                    |
| `partial`, `A.state = "modified"`    | the same two, plus a `note` at `now` carrying `A.note` when `A.note` is present |
| `partial`, `A.state = "partial"`     | the same as `modified`                                                          |
| `partial`, `A.state = "skipped"`     | one `target_skipped`                                                            |
| `partial`, no matching `A`           | one `target_skipped`, carrying no `reason` — treated as skipped                 |

`executed` skips nothing, by definition, and `abandoned` produces no event of
any kind (it creates, or keeps, an empty finished
session of outcome `abandoned` so the Journal keeps the night; it used to create
no session): a plan frozen `abandoned` already says the whole night did not
happen, and repeating it once per target would add noise, not information.

**When the two target events happened.** `recordedAt` and `timing` follow one
ordered rule, and the first branch that applies wins:

1. **The item's own slot.** `P.nightStartAt` is non-null **and** `I` has a
   slot → `target_started` at `nightStartAt + slotStart(I)` minutes and
   `target_finished` at `nightStartAt + slotEnd(I)` minutes, both
   `timing: "planned"`.
2. **The session window.** Otherwise — `I` has no slot, or the night is
   unknown — `target_started` at the session's `startedAt` and
   `target_finished` at its `finishedAt`, both `timing: "reconstructed"`.
   This branch is `reconstructed` even when the window came from `D.session`:
   the person declared when the _night_ ran, not when _this target_ ran.

**When a skip happened.** A `target_skipped` event follows the same ordered
rule, reduced to one instant because a skip has no duration: the **start of
the planned slot**, `nightStartAt + slotStart(I)` minutes with
`timing: "planned"`, when branch 1 applies; the session's `startedAt` with
`timing: "reconstructed"` otherwise. That places the skip where the target
would have been rather than at the moment somebody filled in a form, which is
what a timeline of the night needs; and `reconstructed` says plainly that no
clock measured it. Nothing here is a claim that the person decided at that
instant — `timing` is exactly the field that keeps an interface from
presenting it as one.

**When the slots and the declared window disagree, neither is corrected.** A
slot-derived instant falling outside `[D.session.startedAt,
D.session.finishedAt]` is emitted **unchanged** and stays `planned`. It is not
clamped, not shifted, not dropped. Two reasons, and either alone would settle
it: a clamped instant is a time nobody planned and nobody measured, and
inventing one is exactly the automatic resolution the doctrine forbids; and a
`recordedAt` that depended on the request would put request data back into the
journal's only machine-readable statement about the night. The person's
declaration wins where it is a measurement — the observation session's own
`startedAt`/`finishedAt` — and the plan wins where nothing was measured at all.
A divergence between the two is real information about the night, and the
`timing` field is what lets an interface show it as "planned 21:31, you say you
started at 21:50" rather than hiding it.

Both target events carry the payload the registry defines for them and nothing
else: `targetId` is `I.targetId` when a plan item carries one and `I.id`
otherwise, and `planItemId` is `I.id`. A per-target `note` event carries
`{ text: A.note, targetId: <the same targetId> }` and is `timing: "observed"` —
it really was written at `now`.

A `target_skipped` event carries the same two identification fields,
`{ targetId, planItemId }`, plus `reason: A.reason` and `notes: A.note` when
the adjustment supplies them and nothing when it does not. `A.reason` is the
registry's own closed list — `weather`, `equipment`, `time`, `visibility`,
`other` — and `A.note` lands in the registry's `notes`, whose bound is 300
characters rather than the 500 a `modified` target's `note` event may use;
`debrief.schema.json` states that narrower bound for a `skipped` adjustment so
a request carrying a longer one is refused up front instead of producing a
payload the registry would reject. A `reason` on a `done` or `modified`
adjustment is read by nothing and emitted nowhere, exactly as a `note` on a
`done` one already was.

The mapping does not carry the plan item's `filter`: the registry's target
payloads are closed and have none — which is why `sessionAdjustments` has no
`filter` field to adjust either. A `modified` target means "done, but not as
planned", and the person's own `note` is where how is said.

**A skipped target is stated, not passed over in silence.** The first draft
of this mapping emitted nothing for it, on the ground that the journal records
occurrences and a target that was never observed produced none. The frozen
plan does still say the target was planned, so nothing was strictly lost — but
everything had to be recovered by comparing two documents, and the one fact
that only the person knew, _why_ it did not happen, was recorded nowhere at
all. That is the fact a multi-night project needs in order to answer "why is
this target not progressing?", and it cannot be reconstructed after the night.
So the registry gained `target_skipped` and the mapping emits one, with the
person's reason when they gave one.

What is emitted is still not a verdict: `target_skipped` says the target was
not observed and, optionally, why. The done/modified classification remains
the debriefing's, which is why `target_finished` still carries no outcome and
why a skip produces its own type rather than a `target_finished` with a
"skipped" flag. The same reasoning makes an unadjusted target under `partial` a
skip: `partial` means the person ticked what was done, an unticked target was
not done, and the journal now says so rather than leaving a hole — with no
`reason`, because none was given.

**Hiding a skip is an interface preference, never a filter.** A surface MAY
offer to hide `target_skipped` lines from a session's journal, as a preference
held per session and as a global default; that preference governs **display
only** and MUST NOT become a server-side filter, a query parameter, or any
form of deletion — `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/events` returns every stored event
whatever any surface has chosen to show, and the journal stays append-only.

An adjustment naming a `targetId` absent from `B.items` is `400`
`unknown_target_adjustment`. It is not a harmless typo: it means the client is
ticking a target list from a different version of the plan, which is precisely
the divergence the `baseVersion` choice exists to settle.

**The free note.** `D.note`, when present, becomes one `note` event carrying
`{ text: D.note }` and recorded at `now`, not during the night — it was written
at debriefing time and the journal says so, with `timing: "observed"`. `D.note`
and each `A.note` obey the registry's own 500-character bound on `note.text`.

**What is NOT copied.**

- **Equipment.** `B.equipment` is not turned into events and is not copied onto
  the session. Equipment is a state of the whole night, not a dated occurrence,
  and the frozen plan is already its authoritative, unrewritable record;
  duplicating it would create a second source of truth that could drift. The
  session names `planId`, and `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` reads the equipment back
  **through** that link — see [Equipment on a session](#equipment-on-a-session)
  below, which is how "equipment pre-filled" is
  honoured without a copy. Making equipment an event would require an
  `equipment_set` type the registry does not define, which is a registry
  decision, not an API one.
- **Location.** Never copied. A session response exposes no coordinates beyond
  what the plan payload already carries — and the plan payload does carry them,
  in `payload.location`, which is what the night boundaries are derived from.
  The derived boundaries themselves are instants, not a position.
- **The frozen catalogue snapshot.** A plan item's `target` object — the
  planner's portable copy of the catalogue entry, coordinates included — is not
  copied into an event. The registry's target payloads are closed and identify
  a target by id or label.
- **Planning constraints and interface state** — `minAltitude`,
  `moonSeparationMinimum`, `moonSeparationMinimumByFilter`, `mountSafety`,
  `catalogueSelection`, `view`, and the payload's browser `id`. They describe
  how the plan was built, not what happened. `mountSafety` in particular is a
  constraint the schedule was computed under, already reflected in the slots it
  produced; it is not a fact about the night.

The first draft listed **time slots** here, on the ground that a plan item is
`{ id, filter }`. That is true of this contract's own minimal fixtures and of
`plan.schema.json`, which is deliberately permissive, and false of every plan
the two planners actually write. The slots exist, they are what the design means by "time slots … become initial events
per an explicit mapping", and they are now mapped.

**Deterministic event identifiers.** A generated event's `eventId` is not
random, and it is not derived from `recordedAt` either. It is a valid ULID
built from two parts:

- its first 10 characters are the **order instant** in milliseconds since the
  epoch, Crockford base32, most significant character first — the standard
  ULID time part;
- its last 16 are the first 80 bits of `SHA-256(sessionId + U+001F + eventKey)`,
  taken most significant bit first and encoded five bits per character in the
  same alphabet. `sessionId` and `eventKey` are joined by one U+001F unit
  separator and hashed as UTF-8.

The **order instant** is `anchor + k` milliseconds, where `k` is the event's
emission ordinal:

| Event                        | `eventKey`                                           | `anchor`                                        | `k`                  |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- | -------------------- |
| target started               | `plan/<R>/target/<itemId>/started`                   | `P.nightStartAt`, else `<B.date>T00:00:00.000Z` | `4 × c(I)`           |
| target finished              | `plan/<R>/target/<itemId>/finished`                  | the same                                        | `4 × c(I) + 1`       |
| per-target note (`modified`) | `plan/<R>/target/<itemId>/note`                      | the same                                        | `4 × c(I) + 2`       |
| target skipped               | `plan/<R>/target/<itemId>/skipped`                   | the same                                        | `4 × c(I) + 3`       |
| the debriefing's free note   | `plan/<R>/note`                                      | the same                                        | `4 × count(B.items)` |
| accepted supplement, started | `supplement/<supplementId>/target/<itemId>/started`  | the supplement's `createdAt`                    | `4 × c(I)`           |
| accepted supplement, ended   | `supplement/<supplementId>/target/<itemId>/finished` | the same                                        | `4 × c(I) + 1`       |

`c(I)` is the item's index in the chronological order defined above, over the
whole of `B.items` — every item, whether or not the outcome makes it emit
anything. The `recordedAt` of each event is the one the mapping rules above
give it, and it is **not** what the identifier encodes.

**`target_skipped` takes offset `3`, the one this scheme had reserved.** It is
the only free slot in a four-wide group, so the event type added after the
fact costs nothing: every identifier already issued for a `started`, a
`finished` or a per-target `note` keeps the ordinal it had, the free note keeps
`4 × count(B.items)`, and a journal written before this section changed reads
back unchanged. A skip and the started/finished pair of the same item never
coexist in one decision, so within a group offset `3` only ever appears alone;
across groups it keeps the skip sorted after its own item and before the next
one, which is the planned order a reader expects. There is no fifth offset
left, and that is deliberate: a ninth event type generated by this mapping
would have to widen the group, renumber every ordinal and therefore rename
every event a past decision generated — so it will get its own key space
instead.

**Why the identifier no longer encodes `recordedAt`.** The first draft did
exactly that and claimed row-level idempotency from it. The claim was false.
`recordedAt` depends on `D.session`, which two surfaces answering the same
night may fill differently or not at all, and on `now`, which differs between
two surfaces by definition. Two racing decisions would then have produced two
different ULIDs for the same `eventKey`, the primary key `(sessionId,
eventId)` would not have collided, and the session would have held every
generated event twice.

Every input of the identifier as defined above is a stored value or a pure
function of one: the observation session's `id`, the frozen payload `B`, its
revision `R`, the derived `nightStartAt`, and a supplement's own `id` and
`createdAt`. **Nothing from the request appears** — not `D.session`, not
`D.sessionAdjustments`, not `D.note`, not `now`. Two decisions racing on the
same plan therefore produce byte-identical identifiers for every event they
both emit; the rows collide on `(sessionId, eventId)` and the idempotent union
absorbs them, keeping the first stored event untouched exactly as it does for a
resubmitted client event. A decision carrying different adjustments emits a
subset, never a variant: that is why `k` is computed over all of `B.items` and
not over the emitted ones. The decision-level replay rule already makes a
second call a no-op; this is the row-level guarantee underneath it, and it now
actually holds.

**`target_skipped` keeps that property, and pays the assumed price for it.**
Its `reason` and `notes` come from the request, and the identifier does not:
two surfaces answering the same night, one saying `weather` and the other
`time`, produce the **same** `eventId` for the same skipped target, so the
journal holds one skip rather than two contradictory ones. Which payload
survives is the union's ordinary rule — **the first row written wins, and the
second is counted as a duplicate and dropped, reason and note included**. That
is a real loss of a sentence somebody typed, and it is accepted knowingly: the
alternative is hashing the reason into the key, which would put request data
back into the identifier and let one target be skipped twice, for two
different stated reasons, in one journal. A person who wants to correct a
recorded reason appends a `note` event, as they do for every other correction
in an append-only journal.

The order instant is an identifier component, not a claim about clock time —
the same convention as the night-of anchor above. It keeps generated events
sorted in planned order when two of them share a `recordedAt`, which is the
common case for a plan whose items have no slots.

Including `R` in the key means a debriefing based on a restored set-aside
version produces different identifiers from one based on the payload it
replaced, which is correct: those describe different intentions.

### A worked example

A realistic plan — a place, an equipment selection, items carrying the
timeline's slots, and one item that carries none — its debriefing, and the
exact session and events that result. Every value below is reproducible, and
`packages/astro-contracts/tests/sessions.test.mjs` recomputes the night, the
timestamps and the identifiers from the rules above and asserts these exact
strings.

```json
{
  "id": "9f1c0b4a-5d3e-4a21-9c7f-2b8e6d4a1c30",
  "revision": 4,
  "status": "active",
  "nightStartAt": "2026-09-16T00:46:37.000Z",
  "nightEndAt": "2026-09-16T08:52:44.000Z",
  "frozenAt": null,
  "updatedAt": "2026-09-15T18:42:00.000Z",
  "payload": {
    "id": "browser-plan-1",
    "date": "2026-09-15",
    "name": "Autumn nebulae",
    "location": {
      "id": "place-home",
      "version": 1,
      "name": "Home",
      "locality": "Sainte-Julie",
      "latitude": 45.5,
      "longitude": -73.6,
      "elevation": "40",
      "timeZone": "America/Toronto",
      "horizonStatus": "unverified"
    },
    "equipment": {
      "mode": "imaging",
      "telescopeId": "askar-50p",
      "mountId": "am5",
      "cameraId": "asi533mc-pro",
      "filterIds": ["l-extreme"],
      "restrictToEquipment": false
    },
    "minAltitude": 35,
    "mountSafety": { "enabled": true, "meridianFlip": true },
    "items": [
      {
        "id": "ngc7000",
        "filter": "Ha",
        "startMinute": 200,
        "endMinute": 320,
        "durationMinutes": 120
      },
      {
        "id": "m31",
        "filter": "L",
        "startMinute": 45,
        "endMinute": 195,
        "durationMinutes": 150
      },
      { "id": "m45", "filter": "L" }
    ]
  }
}
```

`nightStartAt` and `nightEndAt` are not in the payload: the server derived them
from `date` and `location`, and they are the astronomical night of 15 September
2026 at 45.5° N, 73.6° W — 8 h 06 min of Sun below −18°. The slots are counted
from `nightStartAt`: `m31` is planned for minutes 45→195 of the night and
`ngc7000` for 200→320. `m45` was added from the catalogue and never placed on
the timeline, so it carries no slot. Payload order is not chronological order,
which is the point of the example: `m31` (45) comes first, then `ngc7000`
(200), then `m45` (no slot), so `c(m31) = 0`, `c(ngc7000) = 1`, `c(m45) = 2`.

`POST /v1/plans/9f1c0b4a-…/debrief`, answered on the web at
`2026-09-16T13:20:00.000Z`:

```json
{
  "outcome": "partial",
  "baseVersion": "current",
  "session": {
    "startedAt": "2026-09-16T01:50:00.000Z",
    "finishedAt": "2026-09-16T06:20:00.000Z"
  },
  "sessionAdjustments": {
    "targets": [
      { "targetId": "m31", "state": "done" },
      {
        "targetId": "ngc7000",
        "state": "modified",
        "note": "Guiding lost after the flip, kept 40 minutes."
      },
      { "targetId": "m45", "state": "skipped", "reason": "weather" }
    ]
  },
  "note": "Clouds rolled in around 2 a.m."
}
```

The session created, with `id` `4d2b7e10-8a6c-4f53-9b1d-7e0a3c5f2b84`:

```json
{
  "id": "4d2b7e10-8a6c-4f53-9b1d-7e0a3c5f2b84",
  "planId": "9f1c0b4a-5d3e-4a21-9c7f-2b8e6d4a1c30",
  "status": "finished",
  "startedAt": "2026-09-16T01:50:00.000Z",
  "finishedAt": "2026-09-16T06:20:00.000Z",
  "createdAt": "2026-09-16T13:20:00.000Z",
  "updatedAt": "2026-09-16T13:20:00.000Z",
  "eventCount": 7,
  "supplementCount": 0,
  "pendingSupplementCount": 0
}
```

The seven events, in emission order — `m45` contributes a skip line:

| `eventId`                    | `type`            | `recordedAt`               | `timing`        | `eventKey`                       |
| ---------------------------- | ----------------- | -------------------------- | --------------- | -------------------------------- |
| `01M2KTW3E8Y14VK0GHW4D4X5FK` | `target_started`  | `2026-09-16T01:31:37.000Z` | `planned`       | `plan/4/target/m31/started`      |
| `01M2KTW3E9WA4FM883DWFG7T4P` | `target_finished` | `2026-09-16T04:01:37.000Z` | `planned`       | `plan/4/target/m31/finished`     |
| `01M2KTW3ECC6Q61KMJCECWEY4T` | `target_started`  | `2026-09-16T04:06:37.000Z` | `planned`       | `plan/4/target/ngc7000/started`  |
| `01M2KTW3EDTEEYHQ9KZKFK9SWJ` | `target_finished` | `2026-09-16T06:06:37.000Z` | `planned`       | `plan/4/target/ngc7000/finished` |
| `01M2KTW3EESN9NT3BEWM2DP6W9` | `note`            | `2026-09-16T13:20:00.000Z` | `observed`      | `plan/4/target/ngc7000/note`     |
| `01M2KTW3EKBG7YHZEY4VGAJGZG` | `target_skipped`  | `2026-09-16T01:50:00.000Z` | `reconstructed` | `plan/4/target/m45/skipped`      |
| `01M2KTW3EMT4EB3V04ZNDNCRSV` | `note`            | `2026-09-16T13:20:00.000Z` | `observed`      | `plan/4/note`                    |

Every identifier shares the time part `01M2KTW3E…` plus its ordinal `k` — 0, 1,
4, 5, 6, 11 and 12 — because the anchor is `nightStartAt` for all of them. The
first five and the last are byte-for-byte the ones this example carried before
`target_skipped` existed: the new type took the offset the group already
reserved, so nothing was renumbered. `m45`'s own 8, 9 and 10 stay unused, and
the ordinals are reserved for it whether or not it emits, which is what keeps a
decision with different adjustments from renaming anybody else's events.

`m45` was added from the catalogue and never placed on the timeline, so its
skip falls to the session-window branch: recorded at the declared
`01:50:00.000Z` and marked `reconstructed`, because nothing measured it. Had
`m31` been the skipped one instead, its skip would have carried its own slot
start, `2026-09-16T01:31:37.000Z`, marked `planned`, under the key
`plan/4/target/m31/skipped` and the identifier `01M2KTW3EBR5YT8GN1MF5KD630`.
And had `m45` simply been left unticked rather than ticked `skipped`, the very
same event would have been emitted under the very same identifier, carrying
`{ "targetId": "m45", "planItemId": "m45" }` and no `reason` at all: the
identifier states which fact is recorded, the payload states how much the
person chose to say about it.

`m31`'s planned start, `01:31:37Z`, is 18 minutes **before** the
`01:50:00.000Z` the person declared as the start of their night. It is emitted
unchanged, marked `planned`, and the session keeps the declared window. Nothing
resolves the two: an interface shows both, because that gap is a fact about the
night rather than a conflict to settle.

All seven carry `receivedAt: "2026-09-16T13:20:00.000Z"` and
`originDeviceId: null` — they originate from this mapping, not from a device,
so the two surfaces produce byte-identical events. The target events carry
`{ "targetId": "m31", "planItemId": "m31" }` and
`{ "targetId": "ngc7000", "planItemId": "ngc7000" }`; the skip carries
`{ "targetId": "m45", "planItemId": "m45", "reason": "weather" }`; the
per-target note carries `{ "text": "Guiding lost after the flip, kept 40
minutes.", "targetId": "ngc7000" }` and the free note `{ "text": "Clouds
rolled in around 2 a.m." }`. The plan answers `status: "partial"`,
`frozenAt: "2026-09-16T13:20:00.000Z"`, and the decision answers
`createdSession: true`, `eventsCreated: 7`, `replayed: false`. Calling the same
route again answers the same plan and the same session with
`createdSession: false`, `eventsCreated: 0`, `replayed: true`.

A second surface answering this same night at the same moment, saying `time`
rather than `weather` for `m45`, emits a `target_skipped` with the identifier
`01M2KTW3EKBG7YHZEY4VGAJGZG` above — the same one, because neither the reason
nor the note reaches the identifier. Whichever transaction writes first keeps
its row; the other's skip is counted as a `duplicate` and its `time` is
dropped. One skip is recorded for `m45`, never two, and the price is the
losing sentence.

Had the same plan carried no `location` — `nightStartAt` null — the seven
events would have kept these exact identifiers only for their hash tails: the
anchor would have been `2026-09-15T00:00:00.000Z`, every target event would
have been recorded at the declared session window's bounds and marked
`reconstructed`, and the plan would never have appeared in the debrief queue at
all.

### Equipment on a session

A session is generated "with targets, time slots
and equipment pre-filled". Targets and time slots are events, by the mapping
above. Equipment is not, and must not be: it is a state of the whole night, it
has no instant, and the frozen plan is already its unrewritable record.

A real plan's `payload.equipment` is the planner's equipment **selection** —
`{ mode, telescopeId, mountId, cameraId, eyepieceId, modifierId, filterIds[],
restrictToEquipment }` (`normalizedEquipmentSelection`); `telescopeId`,
`cameraId` or `eyepieceId` `null` (or absent) is nothing chosen yet.
Its ids point into the owner's own inventory, which `GET /v1/equipment` already
serves. So "pre-filled" is honoured by **reading it back through the link the
session already has**: `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` returns a `plan` projection
carrying `planId`, `status`, `revision`, `frozenAt`, `nightStartAt`,
`nightEndAt` and `equipment`, resolved from the frozen plan at request time and
stored nowhere else. One round trip, one source of truth, and `revision` names
exactly which reading of the plan the equipment was taken from.

The alternatives were weighed and rejected: copying the selection onto the
session row creates a second copy that can drift from a plan that a restore may
still re-revise before freezing; and an `equipment_set` event would need a
registry type that does not exist, for a fact that is not an occurrence. No new
event type is required by this contract.

The night may hold an
equipment of its own, set by its owner (`PATCH /v1/sessions/{id}/night`):
`night.equipment` then says `source: "night"`, and the `plan` projection
keeps echoing the frozen plan's selection, unchanged
([The structured night](#the-structured-night)).

### Late field data: session supplements

When a `PUT /v1/plans/{planId}` reaches a frozen plan that has a session, the
submitted payload is stored as a `proposed` supplement and named in the `409`
body (see [Plan writes and divergence](#plan-writes-and-divergence)). A
supplement is reviewed with
`POST /v1/sessions/{id}/supplements/{sid}` carrying
`{ "decision": "accept" | "dismiss" }`.

- **accept** appends, for each item of the supplement payload's `items` that no
  `target_started` event of this session already names, the same
  `target_started` + `target_finished` pair the mapping above produces, in the
  same chronological slot order, under the keys
  `supplement/<supplementId>/target/<itemId>/started` and `.../finished` and
  anchored on the supplement's own `createdAt`. Their `recordedAt` follows the
  mapping's own ordered rule: the item's slot placed on the session's plan
  `nightStartAt` when both are available (`timing: "planned"`), and otherwise
  the supplement's `createdAt` for both events (`timing: "reconstructed"`) —
  the arrival time of a late payload is not a claim about when anything was
  observed. Nothing else of the payload is copied, by the same list as above.
  Accepting is the person's own statement that this describes their night — the
  same statement `executed` makes — which is why the derivation is allowed to
  assert observations; skipping targets the journal already names is what stops
  an acceptance from duplicating the debriefing's own events. The supplement
  becomes `accepted` and lists the `eventIds` it produced. The alternative
  considered was appending one free `note` instead, which asserts nothing but
  leaves the field target list unreadable by anything but a human;
- **dismiss** appends nothing and marks the supplement `dismissed`. It deletes
  nothing: the payload is retained exactly as submitted.

**A target the journal already marks `target_skipped` is not exempt.** The
guard above is "no `target_started` event of this session already names it",
and a `target_skipped` is not a `target_started`: the acceptance appends the
pair, and the skip line stays exactly where it is. That is the append-only
rule applied without an exception, and it is also the truthful reading — at
debriefing time the person said the target had not been observed, and the
supplement is them saying afterwards that it had been after all. Both
statements are facts about how the night came to be recorded, and a journal
that silently deleted the first would be hiding a correction rather than
showing one. So a reader shows both, in `receivedAt` order, which puts the
supplement's pair after the skip because it arrived later; the two may well
carry the **same** `recordedAt`, since a placed item's skip and its
supplemented `target_started` are both the slot's start, and `receivedAt` then
`eventId` is what orders them. An interface presents that as "skipped at
debriefing, recorded later from the field", never as a contradiction to
resolve. The alternative — having the acceptance withdraw the skip — was
rejected twice over: there is no delete endpoint by design, and the first
statement is itself information a multi-night project will want.

Replaying the same decision is `200` with `replayed: true` and creates nothing.
A **dismissed supplement may still be accepted later** — the data was never
destroyed, and reversing a mis-tap must not be the one way to lose field truth.
An accepted supplement can no longer be dismissed (`409`
`supplement_already_accepted`): its events are already appended and appended
events are never withdrawn.

## The Journal: a plan carried out becomes a session

**A plan is a promise; a plan
carried out becomes a session**: the plan enriched with what the night really
gave. In the interface "session" is now the word of that notion; this
document keeps saying "observation session" for the resource and "web
session" for authentication, and it is the same observation session. The word
for the time from evening to morning stays "night", the action in progress
stays "execution" (« Nuit en cours »), the page of all the sessions is the
**Journal**. Everything below is **additive**: no field, route or enum value
of the sections above is removed or renamed.

The machine-readable shapes are [`journal.schema.json`](journal.schema.json)
(the list), [`session-rework.schema.json`](session-rework.schema.json) (the
rework), and the additions to [`session.schema.json`](session.schema.json),
[`debrief.schema.json`](debrief.schema.json),
[`capture.schema.json`](capture.schema.json),
[`manage-captures.schema.json`](manage-captures.schema.json),
[`integration.schema.json`](integration.schema.json) and
[`project.schema.json`](project.schema.json). [journal.md](journal.md) holds
the worked example and the rules of the list.

### The session resource: outcome, origin, evening date

`GET /v1/sessions` and `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` give each session six more
fields, written by the service on every answer (optional in the schema only
so that a reader of an earlier answer still validates):

- `outcome` — `executed`, `partial`, `abandoned` or `null`: the outcome of the
  debriefing of the session's plan. `null` for a session not debriefed yet and
  for a session without a plan.
- `origin` — `execution` (« Nuit en cours »), `debrief` (created by a
  debriefing), `import` (an import: a night package, the old portal's nights,
  a night rebuilt from captures), `astro-sync` (Astro Sync's « Nuits »),
  `capture` (a night born from a capture the person adds: not an
  import, not read-only). Set by the creation path, never changed.
  `POST /v1/sessions` accepts `origin` `execution` (the default),
  `astro-sync` or `capture` only; the two others answer `400
invalid_request`.
- `projectId` — the multi-night plan the session belongs to: the link carried
  by the plan item of the session's target first, else the link the session
  carries itself, else `null`.
- `eveningDate` — the night's date, **the definition of the integrations**
  (`integration.schema.json`): the plan's date, else the evening of the
  session's start at the place's longitude, local noon to local noon, never a
  time zone. **`capture.nightDate` keeps its own definition** (the night of
  the capture's plan, else of its session) and is not changed.
- `reworkRevision` (0 at creation) and `reworkedAt` — see
  [Reworking a session](#reworking-a-session).

`GET /v1/sessions/{id}` also gives `targets`, the session's cards (see
[The Journal](#the-journal)), and its `events` are the **effective** events.

**Names of a session's id.** `sessionId`, `nightId`, `combinedNightIds`,
`fromSessionIds` and `sessionIds` all name an observation session's id: the
integrations and the nights of a multi-night plan call it `nightId` (and now
also give `sessionId`, the same value), the image of a plan lists the
`combinedNightIds` it combined, a multi-night plan created from existing nights
takes `fromSessionIds`, and the Journal's groups list `sessionIds`.

### The debriefing makes the session

The debriefing ([above](#the-debriefing-decision)) is a **state of the
session**, not a zone apart. What changes in the contract:

- an `abandoned` decision **keeps a session**: it creates (or keeps) a
  finished session of outcome `abandoned`, `origin: "debrief"`, with its
  `reason` (a `skipReason`), and still no event of any kind. The guard stays: a
  session that already holds a field event refuses `abandoned` (`409
plan_not_debriefable`). The other outcomes write `outcome` on the session;
- a target adjustment may carry `frameCount`, `startedAt`, `finishedAt` and
  `removed` (« Retirée au débriefing », sent with `state: "skipped"`, the
  value an older reader understands), and the request `addedTargets[]`
  (`targetId`, `startedAt`, `finishedAt`, `acquiredMinutes`, `frameCount`,
  `filter`): the targets observed that the plan did not hold. All are stored
  with the debriefing and given back in `debrief.targets`, `debrief.addedTargets`;
  the session keeps a `removed` target as `removed` and an added one as `added`,
  never erasing either;
- `addedTargets` is read for `executed` and `partial`; `reason` for
  `abandoned`; the adjustments for `partial` only, as before. A target of
  `addedTargets` that neither the catalogue nor the space knows answers `400
unknown_target_adjustment` with the `targetId`, nothing written;
- the progress of a multi-night plan still counts from the debriefings; these
  figures do not change it.

### The Journal

`GET /v1/journal?year=&instrument=&season=&outcome=&multiNightOnly=&cursor=&limit=`
(either credential) answers `200 { sessions, groups, years, instruments, stats,
nextCursor }` (`journal.schema.json`, `list`), **the most recent night
first**. It is a **read, never held by a right**: no capability on any profile,
on a private server, nor in the read-only window of an ended subscription. It
lists finished sessions only (an open or paused session is « Nuit en cours »)
and, besides them, the past plans carried out without the application and not
debriefed (`sessionId: null`, `state: "to_debrief"`). It is not
`GET /v1/sessions`, which stays the ascending-by-`updatedAt` read of the
devices.

**The statistics of the gallery**. The statistics by target and by
instrument — a target over all its nights, a tube and its camera over all
theirs — are held by the capability `gallery.private`, which the profile of a
private server holds. The year, the months and the nights stay free. They are worked out from
the lines of this list by one pure function (`journal-statistics.js`, the shared
fixtures `journal-statistics-*.json`): an
**instrument** is the pair `instrumentLabel` (« tube · caméra »); the hours are
those of the sessions whose debriefing is confirmed (never a session « À
débriefer »); a **night** of a target is a confirmed night that acquired time of
it; the best is the longest integration, the sharper night (lowest FWHM) on a
tie. The answer of this route carries the header `X-Astro-Gallery-Statistics`,
`open` or `held`, so that a page which writes a figure of those statistics reads
the right rather than guessing; a profile that cannot be read sends no header
(the page draws nothing of them).

- **An item** (`item`): `sessionId`, `planId`, `planName`, `title` (the name
  the person gave to the session itself, else `null`; a reader calls the
  session by it when it is set, else by its night), `projectId`,
  `projectName`, `eveningDate`, `startedAt`, `finishedAt`, `placeName`,
  `placeTimeZone` (the IANA zone the session's hours are read in, else `null`:
  the hours are then written in UTC, with « UTC »; the same rule on the phone
  and on the web; `title` and `placeTimeZone` are optional and additive: an
  older server does not send them, which reads as `null`), `instrumentLabel`, `state` (`to_debrief`, `executed`, `partial`,
  `abandoned`, `no_plan`), `origin`, `plannedSeconds`, `acquiredSeconds`,
  `frameCount`, `lostReason`, `measured`, `quality` (`skyBackground`, `fwhm`,
  `eccentricity`: the medians of the night, or `null`), `captureCount`,
  `targets[]` and `readOnly`. A **target** (`target`): `targetId`, `label`,
  `planItemId`, `state` (`planned`, `added`, `removed`), `outcome` (`done`,
  `partial`, `skipped`, `null`), `reason`, `plannedSeconds`, `acquiredSeconds`,
  `frameCount`, `filter`, `startedAt`, `finishedAt`, `exposureSeconds`,
  `exposureFamilies`, `captureId`, `thumbnailUrl`, and, when there is
  something to say, `planned` and `measured` (EXPO-G) (the last five and the
  times and exposure are described in [What the screens of the Journal still
  read](#what-the-screens-of-the-journal-still-read)). The figures come from
  the debriefing, then from the events, then from Astro Sync's analysis when
  there is one.
- **Order**: `eveningDate` descending, then `startedAt` descending (a line
  without one last), then the line's id descending. The paging is by cursor:
  `nextCursor` is opaque and sent back verbatim; `limit` is 50 by default, 100
  at most.
- **Filters** add up. `year`: the calendar year of `eveningDate`. `instrument`:
  an `instrumentLabel` as `instruments` lists it. `season`: the four
  astronomical seasons of `astronomicalSeason` applied to `eveningDate`
  (`winter`, `spring`, `summer`, `autumn`). `outcome`: a `state`, or several
  separated by commas (`executed,partial`: either state, additive,
  for the outcomes a screen ticks). `multiNightOnly=true`: the sessions of a multi-night plan. Anything outside
  its values, or a bad cursor, answers `400 invalid_request`.
- **`years` and `instruments`** list what the space holds with no filter
  applied; **`stats`** (`sessions`, `acquiredSeconds`, `frameCount`) follows
  **all** the filters and covers everything they select, not only the page.
- **`toDebriefCount`** is **the one count of the sessions
  « À débriefer »** of the space: the number of lines whose `state` is
  `to_debrief`, **whatever the filters** (a badge does not move with them) —
  a plan carried out and not debriefed, with or without a session. It is the
  source of the badge of the Journal on both interfaces: the phone adds to it
  the sessions it holds that the service has not received yet, the web reads
  it alone. The debrief queue (`GET /v1/plans/debrief-queue`) is **not** the
  source of the badge. A deleted session is not counted. A client that wants
  the badge alone asks `GET /v1/journal?limit=1`.
- **Groups**: a group is a **finished** multi-night plan. Its sessions are
  still in `sessions[]`; `groups[]` lists, on each page, the groups having at
  least one session on that page, with `sessionIds` = every session of the plan
  matching the filters (wherever it falls in the paging), `acquiredSeconds`,
  `goalSeconds` and `planCaptureId`. A client draws a group at the place of its
  newest session and leaves the group's sessions out of the flat list. Before
  the plan is finished, its sessions are plain items carrying `projectName`.
  `acquiredSeconds` is the progress of the plan worked out from the same
  figures as the lines (what **all** the plan's sessions acquired of its
  target, whatever the filters), `planCaptureId` its newest plan image.
- **A private server** lists, besides its sessions, the nights of the
  operator's catalogue that match no session (by `eveningDate` and target) as
  sessions of `origin: "import"`, with a synthetic id (an opaque string, not a
  UUID) and `readOnly: true`. `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` reads such a session (see
  [What the screens of the Journal still
  read](#what-the-screens-of-the-journal-still-read)); every write to it
  answers `409 session_read_only`, every other read `404 session_not_found`.

### Reworking a session

`PUT /v1/sessions/{id}/rework` (either credential, CSRF for the web;
`session-rework.schema.json`) corrects a finished session after the fact.
**It is free**: no capability, on every profile and on a private server, and
**the read-only window of an ended subscription does not hold it either** (reworking is free; it is one's own journal, and, like every other
write of a session, an event or a debriefing, it is never refused in that
window).

- Headers: `If-Match: "<reworkRevision>"` (quoted, `"0"` for a session never
  reworked). Missing: `428 precondition_required`; malformed: `400
invalid_if_match`; stale: **`409 session_rework_conflict`** with the current
  `session`, nothing applied.
- Body, every member optional (an empty body is `400 invalid_request`):
  `targets[]` (the **whole** list of the session's targets in its new order:
  `targetId`, `state` `planned` | `added` | `removed`, `startedAt`,
  `finishedAt`, `frameCount`, `exposureSeconds`, `filter`, `reason`),
  `addedEvents[]` (`type`
  `note` | `condition` | `equipment_issue` | `pause`, `at`, `until` for a pause
  only, `targetId` or `null` = the whole night, `payload` = the type's
  payload), `eventReplacements[]` (`eventId`, `event` — see [Replacing a
  signal](#replacing-a-signal-and-the-targets-a-rework-takes-out)),
  `eventMoves[]` (`eventId`, `targetId` or `null`), `eventRemovals[]`
  (event ids; only events a rework added).
- Answer `200`: the session as `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` gives it, with
  `reworkRevision` + 1, `reworkedAt`, its effective events and its `targets`;
  `updatedAt` moves to `reworkedAt`.
- Refusals: `400 invalid_request`, `404 session_not_found` (another space's or
  unknown), `409 session_read_only` (a catalogue night of a private server),
  `404 event_not_found`, `409 night_not_finished`
  (a session still open or paused), `409 event_not_removable` (an observed
  event), `409 event_already_replaced` and `409 event_not_replaceable` (see
  [Replacing a signal](#replacing-a-signal-and-the-targets-a-rework-takes-out)),
  `422 target_not_found`, plus the general `401` and `403
csrf_invalid`; never `403 entitlement_read_only`. All the body is checked
  before anything is written. The details of a refusal (`eventId`,
  `targetId`, `replacedBy`, and the current `session` of a conflict) sit
  **beside** `error` at the top of the body, as the service has always written
  them.
- **The event log stays append-only.** A rework is a _correction layer_ stored
  beside the session: nothing is updated or deleted in the log. The effective
  events (`GET /v1/sessions/{id}`, `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/events`) are the log
  read through it: an added event reads `timing: "reconstructed"` and carries
  `rework: { added: true, targetId }`; a moved event carries `rework: { added:
false, targetId }`; a removed added event is absent; a removed target stays
  in the session as `removed`. A device reading with `since` sees a reworked
  session again through `updatedAt`, not through its events' `receivedAt`.
- **What a rework fixes.** The list sent in `targets` fixes the set and the
  order of the session's targets: a target sent `removed` is **kept in the
  session as `removed`, never erased** — a planned one, and one
  the night recorded without the plan (met in the field, added at the
  debriefing); only a target a rework itself added and the night never saw is
  dropped for good and does not come back. A target one of the
  sources brings **after** a rework (Astro Sync analysed the night later, say)
  is not hidden by it: it joins the end of the list. The times and the figures
  a rework sets for a target (`startedAt` and `finishedAt` give its acquired
  time, minus the pauses inside them) win over those of the debriefing, the
  events and the analysis.
- The declared integration lines of a session attached to a multi-night plan
  are recomputed after a rework.

### Replacing a signal, and the targets a rework takes out

Everything here is **additive**. Two rules
about reworking a session, which the service did not yet hold: a target is
never erased without a trace, and the log of events stays append-only.

- **A target the night recorded, taken out by a rework, stays `removed`.**
  A target met in the field (`target_started` / `target_finished`, or a note
  placed on it) or added at the debriefing (`addedTargets`) and sent
  `state: "removed"` in `targets[]` is kept in the session with `state:
"removed"`, `outcome: "skipped"`, its `reason` if one is given, no acquired
  figure, no time: in the detail (`targets`), in the Journal's line, in the
  export (`rework.targets`). It no longer counts in the figures of the session
  nor in the declared integration lines (the rule of a removed target). Sent
  `planned` / `added` again by a later rework, it comes back with the figures
  the night gave it. Only a target the rework itself added (never seen by the
  night) is dropped for good when sent `removed`, as before.
- **Its signals fall to the whole night.** A signal posed on a target a
  rework took out — a note whose payload names it, a signal an earlier rework
  moved or added on it — reads `rework: { added: false, targetId: null }`
  (an added one `{ added: true, targetId: null }`), **unless the same rework
  (or a later one) moves it onto another target**. `eventMoves` to a removed
  target reads the same way: the whole night.
- **A signal is replaced, not edited.** `eventReplacements[]` (at most 100;
  on the rework and on the debriefing) holds `{ eventId, event }`: `eventId`
  names a `note`, a `condition`, an `equipment_issue`, a `pause` or one of the
  four kinds Astro Sync detects (`exposure_change`, `guiding_timeouts`,
  `sensor_temperature_drift`, `guiding_spikes`) the session holds — an
  observed one or one a rework added — and `event` has the form of
  an `addedEvents[]` entry (`type`, `at`, `until` for a pause, `targetId` or
  absent = the whole night, `payload`) **of the same type, or of another of the
  four when the original is not a pause** (the genre of an incident that
  was a note is corrected; a pause is replaced by a pause only). **The replacement
  keeps the origin of what it replaces**: a signal Astro Sync wrote
  (`source: "astro-sync"`) and the person corrected still reads « Détecté par
  Astro Sync ». The original is
  kept in the log and **hidden** from the effective events (the detail's, the
  events page's, the debriefing summary's); the new one is stored as an added
  event (`timing: "reconstructed"`) that **replaces** it. A pause is replaced
  by a pause: the `resume` that closed it is hidden with it, and the new pair
  stands in their place.
- **The marks.** On a read of the effective events: the replacement reads
  `rework: { added: true, targetId, replaces: <id of the original> }`. The
  original is absent, unless the read asks for it with **`?includeReplaced=true`**
  (`GET /v1/sessions/{id}` and `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/events`): it then reads
  `rework: { added: false, targetId, replacedBy: <id of the replacement> }`
  (`targetId` is its placement by a move, otherwise `null`; it means nothing on
  a hidden event). The export keeps the log whole: a replaced event stays in
  `events` with that mark, and `rework.addedEvents[]` entries carry `replaces`
  and, when replaced in turn, `replacedBy`.
- **Replacing twice: the last wins, the chain stays readable.** Replacing the
  replacement (A → B → C) hides B and shows C; with the explicit read, A reads
  `replacedBy: B`, B reads `replaces: A, replacedBy: C`, C reads `replaces:
B`. **Removing a replacement** (`eventRemovals`: it is an event a rework
  added) gives the event it replaced back, with its own resume for a pause. An
  event a rework added is modified the same way (replaced) or removed.
- **Refusals** (nothing is written): `404 event_not_found` (no such event in
  the session, `eventId`); **`409 event_already_replaced`** (the named event
  was already replaced by a more recent revision — also when an
  `eventMoves` or an `eventRemovals` names it; `eventId` and `replacedBy`, the
  event that shows now); **`409 event_not_replaceable`** (`eventId`: the event
  is not one of those, e.g. a target's start or end, or a `resume` named on
  its own); `400 invalid_request` (a pause replaced by another `type`, the same
  event is named twice, or both replaced and moved or
  removed, in one request; `at` outside the session's window; a payload
  outside its schema); `422 target_not_found` (the replacement's `targetId`).
  **`If-Match` is not enough on its own**: it protects a client that has not
  read the latest revision (it gets `409 session_rework_conflict` and the
  current session), but not one that names a hidden event while holding the
  current revision; that one gets `event_already_replaced`.
- **On the debriefing**, `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/debrief` takes
  `eventReplacements[]` beside `addedEvents[]` and `eventMoves[]`, under the
  same rules and the same first layer (`reworkRevision` + 1): a signal observed
  in the field is modified at the debriefing without being lost. Read for
  `executed` and `partial`; `abandoned` answers `400 invalid_request`.
- **Free**, like the rework; the append-only log is never touched (no row of
  `session_events` is updated or deleted); the declared integration lines
  follow a replaced pause the way they follow an added one.

### Creating sessions in bulk

`POST /v1/sessions/bulk` (either credential, CSRF for the web;
`session-bulk.schema.json`) is « Créer … » of Astro Sync's
« Détecter les sessions »: what the detection of a folder of FITS found and the
person ticked, written in one call through the service's own gestures, so that
every rule they keep holds here too. Per night without a session: its plan of
one night, **created** from the frames (one item per target, its duration the
time taken), a planned plan of the same target **reused**, or a planned plan of
another target **replaced** by what is detected; then its session (`origin:
"astro-sync"`, shown « Importée »), finished, debriefed `executed` (« Exécutée »,
debriefable again later), and a rework that sets each target's times, frames,
exposure and filter from the frames (a target of a reused plan that the frames
did not take is marked `removed`, never erased). A target that gets both its
`frameCount` and its `exposureSeconds` from the rework has, as time acquired,
`frameCount × exposureSeconds` (what the sensor integrated), never the hours
between its first and its last frame: those stay its `startedAt` /
`finishedAt`. A night whose session already
exists receives, through the rework, the targets it does not hold (« Ajouter …
à la session »); nothing else of it is written. The multi-night plans are born
`finished` (« Terminé ») with the goal = the total acquired (30 min to 100 h),
their nights attached. Places to create (« Créer ce lieu ») come first.

**Idempotent**: every identifier the call creates (place, plan, session,
project) is derived from the space, the client's `importId` and the element's
`ref`; each gesture replays (an existing plan, session, debriefing, rework or
link is read back, never written twice), so the same body sent again answers the
same result with `replayed: true`. **Partial**: an element refused for its own
reason is answered `skipped` with its code and the others are written —
`one_plan_per_night` (a space without `planner.multiple` whose date already
holds a plan: « un plan par nuit »), `plan_limit_reached`, `plan_not_pending`
(the reused or replaced plan was executed meanwhile), `target_not_found` (a
target added to a session must be known to the space), `capability_required`
(a multi-night plan without `planner.mosaic`), `nights_missing` (fewer than two
of its nights written). A body that cannot be read is refused whole, `400
invalid_request`, nothing written.

### What the screens of the Journal still read

Everything here is **additive**: a reader of an
earlier answer still validates (the new fields are optional in the schemas and
written by the service on every answer).

- **The times of a target.** `target.startedAt` and `target.finishedAt`
  (`null` when unknown, and for a `removed` target) come, **each one apart**,
  from the rework's `startedAt` / `finishedAt` for the target, else the
  debriefing's (`sessionAdjustments.targets[].startedAt` / `finishedAt`, or
  those of `addedTargets[]`), else the events: the start of the target's first
  passage and the end of its last one (`target_started` to `target_finished`,
  the next target or the end of the night). A rework that sets the times of a
  target gives them back on the next read, in `GET /v1/sessions/{id}`
  (`detail.targets[]`) and in the Journal's line (`targets[]`) alike.
- **The exposure of a target.** `target.exposureSeconds` (the exposure of one
  frame, `null` when unknown) comes from Astro Sync's analysis when the night
  has one (its steps: the **family** of exposures that gathered the most time
  of the target, the longer exposure on a tie), else from what the rework,
  else the debriefing, declared (`exposureSeconds` of a rework target, of a
  debriefing adjustment, of an added target: a number of seconds above 0, a
  day at most, `400 invalid_request` otherwise). `target.exposureFamilies` is
  how many distinct exposures the analysis found (a family is one exposure
  value; the filters that share it make one), `1` for a declared exposure,
  `null` when `exposureSeconds` is: a screen that sees more than 1 knows the
  exposure shown is the main one, not the only one. The analysis's exposure
  stays above a declared one.
- **Planned and measured shooting (« Prise de vue par cible »,
  additive).** A target of the plan carries `planned` when the plan was
  launched with the freezing of its lines: `{ "ruleVersion", "lines": [{
"filter", "exposureSeconds", "gain", "frameCount" }] }`, 1 to 8 lines read
  from `launchedSchedule[].acquisition` of the plan item (the effective lines
  at the launch, never recomputed; see [What to shoot](#what-to-shoot-the-acquisition-lines-of-a-plan)).
  Nothing new is stored: the service reads the plan. It is **absent** for a
  plan launched before the freezing, for a free execution, for a night at the
  eyepiece, for an added and for a removed target. Every target that has
  something to say carries `measured`: `{ "source", "lines" }`. `source:
"astro-sync"` when the analysis of the night measured lines for that
  target (matched by target key and night, as the captures are; the frames
  that share a filter, an exposure and a gain add up), else `source:
"declared"`: the frames and the exposure the person declared at the
  debriefing or in a rework (one line, the filter only when one was said,
  never a gain), else the night's hand-written or imported steps. It is
  absent when nothing says and for a removed target. A line has the four
  fields of a capture's line, each `null` when not said (`gain` `null` is the
  device's own setting). Screens write « Prévu 28 × 120 s · gain 100 · L »
  against « Mesuré (Astro Sync) 27 × 120 s · gain 100 · L » or « Déclaré
  27 × 120 s ». The debriefing sends the exposure of a target next to its
  frames (`sessionAdjustments.targets[].exposureSeconds`, already accepted); the screens preselect both from `planned`.
  **The measurement wins over the declared count**: a target's
  `frameCount`, and so the `frameCount` of the night (the sum over its held
  targets), is the sum of Astro Sync's measured lines whenever `measured.source`
  is `astro-sync` (28 frames declared, 27 measured: 27 everywhere); the
  declared count (the rework's, else the debriefing's) stands only without a
  measurement.
- **Events and placements at the debriefing.** The debriefing request
  (`debrief.schema.json`, `request`) takes `addedEvents[]` (at most 100) and
  `eventMoves[]` (at most 200) in **the forms and under the rules of the
  rework** (`session-rework.schema.json`, `addedEvent`, `eventMove`: `at`
  inside the session's window, `until` for a pause only, a `targetId` that
  is a target of the session, the catalogue or the space else `422
target_not_found`, `404 event_not_found` for an event the session does not
  hold). The service applies them in the transaction of the decision, as the
  **first layer of a rework** of the session the debriefing writes (or
  finishes): `reworkRevision` is 1 in the answer, the events read
  `timing: "reconstructed"` with their `rework` mark, the append-only log is
  not touched, and the declared integration lines follow (the call after the
  transaction, as for the rework). Read for `executed` and `partial`;
  `abandoned` with them answers `400 invalid_request` (a night not carried out
  holds no event). A debriefing without these fields changes nothing; a replay
  of a decision applies nothing again.
- **A catalogue night of a private server.** `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` answers
  for the opaque id (`catalogue:<date>:<target key>`) of a night that
  `GET /v1/journal` lists as read only (`readOnly: true`, `origin: "import"`):
  `200` with `session` (`origin: "import"`, `status: "finished"`, no plan,
  `readOnly: true`), no supplement, an empty events page, `targets` (the same
  card as the Journal's line: the catalogue's figures and times, and
  `imageUrl`, the catalogue's picture of the target, since a read-only night has
  no capture of the space) and `readOnly: true` at the top of the detail (`false` on every
  other session). A write to such an id (`PUT …/rework`, `POST …/finish`,
  `PATCH …/night`, steps, supplements, analyses, imports, events) answers
  **`409 session_read_only`**; any other read of it (events, online, analyses)
  `404 session_not_found`. An id the operator's catalogue does not hold, a
  night a session of the space already holds, and any id where there is no
  catalogue night answer `404 session_not_found`.
- **The computer of a measured line.** An integration line of a multi-night
  plan with `source: "measured"` reads `computerLabel` (what Astro Sync sent;
  `null` for a declared line); a line of "Intégrations" of a capture with
  `provenance: "measured"` reads it too, when Astro Sync sent it with the line
  (see [A capture and its session](#a-capture-and-its-session)).
- **Recorded answers.** The Journal's exchanges with the real service are
  recorded in `fixtures/journal-exchange-<name>.json` (specification:
  `fixtures/journal-exchange-spec/<name>.json`; see `fixtures/README.md`):
  `list`, `list-multi-night-only`, `list-private-server`,
  `session-partial-with-signals`, `session-reworked`, `session-no-plan`,
  `session-measured`, `session-imported`, `project-declared-and-measured`,
  `rework-response`, `rework-replaced-and-removed`, `debrief-added-targets-and-events`, `upload-replaced`,
  `upload-variant`.
- **"Exporter mes données".** The export carries the new figures without a
  change of shape: the debriefing's `exposureSeconds`, `startedAt` and
  `finishedAt` in `debriefs[].targets` / `addedTargets`, and the rework's
  targets, added events and placements — the ones a debriefing applied as its
  first layer included — in `sessions[].rework`.

### Passages declared one at a time

Everything here is
**additive**. The debriefing and « Remanier » edit the hours of a target **one
passage at a time** (a target observed, left for another, then taken up again
has two passages; the frise draws one segment per passage). A single
`startedAt` / `finishedAt` per target could not say it: the two passages of a
target melted into one span.

- **On the way in.** A debriefing adjustment
  (`sessionAdjustments.targets[]`), a target the debriefing adds
  (`addedTargets[]`) and a target of a rework (`PUT /v1/sessions/{id}/rework`,
  `targets[]`) take `passages`: 1 to 50 `{ start, end }` (ISO 8601), `end`
  after `start`, none overlapping another (two passages that touch are one:
  the client joins them before it sends), `400 invalid_request` otherwise,
  nothing written. When `passages` is given, the service sets `startedAt` to
  the first start and `finishedAt` to the last end, whatever was sent beside
  it.
- **What it changes.** The acquired time of the target is the sum of its
  passages (a rework deducts the pauses inside each passage, as it already
  does for one span; a debriefing's `acquiredMinutes`, when sent, still wins).
  The debriefing stores them in its record (`record.targets[].passages`,
  `record.addedTargets[].passages`), the rework in its layer.
- **On the way out.** `target.passages` (`journal.schema.json`): the passages
  the latest rework that set them declared, else the debriefing's, `null` when
  none was declared and for a `removed` target. The passages the events give
  stay where they were, in `debriefSummary.perTarget[].passages`. A reader that
  draws the night draws one segment per passage, the declared ones first.
- **Recorded answers.** `fixtures/journal-exchange-rework-passages.json`.

### Deleting a session

A session
the owner deletes is **kept 30 days** in the deleted sessions, where it can be
restored, then erased with its captures and its data. The 30 days are one
constant of the service (`SESSION_GRACE_DAYS`); the answers say them
(`graceDays`, `purgeAt`, `daysRemaining`). The shapes are in
[`session.schema.json`](session.schema.json) (`deleteResponse`,
`restoreResponse`, `erasure`) and [`journal.schema.json`](journal.schema.json)
(`deletedList`). Everything here is **additive**; nothing is gated by a right,
the read-only window of an ended subscription included (a deletion is the
person cleaning up their own data).

- **Who.** The credentials of a space are its owner's — the only role a space
  has — so either credential of the space may delete (a web session carries its
  CSRF token). **The trash is the web's**:
  `GET /v1/journal/deleted`, `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/restore` and
  `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/erase` answer a **web session of the space** only —
  the two writes with its CSRF token — and a paired device's token is refused
  `403 web_session_required` (the phone never lists, restores or erases; it
  learns a deletion and a restoration by `GET /v1/sessions?deleted=`). A phone **with no space** has no session at
  the service: it deletes **on the device, definitively** (the sheet says so).
  A session of another space is `404 session_not_found`; a catalogue night of
  a private server is `409 session_read_only`.
- **`DELETE /v1/sessions/{id}`** deletes softly: `200 { session, graceDays,
daysRemaining }`, the `session` carrying `deletedAt` and `purgeAt`
  (`deletedAt` plus 30 days). Idempotent (a replay moves nothing and signals
  nothing). A night in progress is not deleted: `409 session_not_finished`.
  The signal is the `sessions` domain.
- **What a deleted session leaves.** It reads as **absent** on every route of a
  session (`GET`, `finish`, `reopen`, `events`, `rework`, `night`, `steps`,
  `analyses`, `supplements`, the debriefing of its session: `404
session_not_found`), in `GET /v1/journal`, its statistics and its
  `toDebriefCount`, in the debrief queue, in the nights and the progress of
  its multi-night plans, and in the export of the space. Its events, its
  captures and its data are **untouched** until the erasure. **Its plan is not
  written**: it stays what it was — « exécuté » — and says its session is
  missing (see [The history of a plan](#the-history-of-a-plan)). A plan whose
  only session was deleted is not in the queue of the debriefing and not in
  the Journal as a night to debrief: the deletion takes the session out of the
  file, not the plan.
- **Its captures are masked for the 30 days.** Wherever the service renders a capture — `GET /v1/captures`
  (with or without `sessionId`, in both orders), `GET /v1/captures/{id}` and the
  routes under it (media, original, variants, sky solution, acquisition
  lines), the web « Mes captures » and its routes, the private gallery and its
  shared address, the public gallery and its shares, the tags of the space and
  the figures of the Journal — a capture of a session in the trash is **absent**
  (`404 capture_not_found` by its id): it is neither listed nor counted. They
  come back, as they were, with the restoration, and go with the erasure. The
  restoration is the instant they are added to the space again: the service
  records it beside the capture (never back, never before the capture was
  added) and `GET /v1/captures?since=` compares the **later** of `createdAt` and
  that instant, so that a device receives them again; their `createdAt` — the
  date they were added — is **not touched**, and the answer still carries it.
  The change signal then carries `captures` beside `sessions`. Three
  things do not move: the storage and the capture count a limited account is
  held to still count them (deleting a session never frees room for more
  uploads before the erasure), an upload of the very bytes of a masked capture
  is answered as « already present », and a service with a team that reads
  the captures reads the rows as ever (nothing of them is shown to anyone).
- **`GET /v1/sessions?deleted=`** is the sync read: `exclude` (the default), or
  `include` (a deleted session is listed with its `deletedAt`, and its
  `updatedAt` moved: **how another device learns the deletion**, and removes
  the session from its own copy), or `only`. Anything else is `400
invalid_request`.
- **`GET /v1/journal/deleted`** lists the deleted sessions, the most recently
  deleted first: `{ entries, graceDays }`, each entry `{ session, deletedAt,
purgeAt, daysRemaining }` with `session` the line of the Journal it was (its
  targets, its place, its figures; `captureCount` is what the erasure would
  take with it). `daysRemaining` is the whole days left, rounded up.
- **`POST /v1/sessions/{id}/restore`** brings it back: `200 { session }`, in
  the Journal again with its events and its captures. A session that is not
  deleted is returned as it is. **A plan holds one live session**: if the plan
  has another since (see below), `409 session_plan_taken` with the `sessionId`
  that holds it. An execution (`clientSessionId`) whose session was deleted is
  not made again by `POST /v1/sessions`: `409 session_deleted` names the
  deleted session, which is restored.
- **`POST /v1/sessions/{id}/erase`** erases a session **that was deleted**, at
  once, definitively: its captures (their files, their derived images and their
  lines of « Intégrations » go through the owner's capture removal), its
  events, supplements, analyses, steps, package imports, the journals Astro
  Sync sent for the night and the declared or measured lines of its multi-night
  plans, then the session. `200 { sessionId, erased }`, `erased` counting what
  went (`captures`, `events`, `supplements`, `analyses`, `steps`,
  `packageImports`, `deviceJournalLines`, `integrations`). A session that is
  not deleted is `409 session_not_deleted`: it is deleted first, so that
  nothing is erased by one gesture. A sky measure the night gave to its place
  keeps its value and loses the link to the night. The erasure signals
  `sessions`, `projects` and `captures`.
- **The maintenance** (every hour, and at start) erases the sessions deleted
  more than 30 days ago the same way. A session that will not go (its captures'
  storage failed) does not hold the others: it is tried again the next hour.

## Uploads and captures

`POST /v1/uploads` with a `multipart/form-data` body is the initial,
**non-resumable** form: a `metadata` JSON part described by
[`capture.schema.json`](capture.schema.json), then the `file` part.
**`metadata` MUST come first**, so the server can refuse an oversized or
unentitled upload before reading a byte of the image. It remains, for a small
file, exactly as described in this section.

**A resumable transport** is added, which the capture resource below
is meant to survive: the same `POST /v1/uploads` URL, an `application/json`
body instead, plus `PUT /v1/uploads/{id}`, `GET /v1/uploads/{id}` and
`POST /v1/uploads/{id}/finalize`. The full protocol — headers, offset
semantics, idempotency at both the "already mid-transfer" and "already
finalized" levels, the 72-hour expiry — is
[`uploads.md`](uploads.md); this section stays the reference for the capture
resource itself and the non-resumable form.

The original bytes are stored unchanged under the tenant's media area, outside
the database: metadata points at the protected original, and nothing is
recompressed, converted or duplicated.

**Idempotency is content-addressing.** The server computes the SHA-256 of the
received bytes; the declared `sha256` and `byteSize` are an advisory pre-check
and a disagreement is `400` `checksum_mismatch`, never a stored claim. The pair
`(tenant, sha256)` is unique: the first upload answers `201` with
`deduplicated: false`, and every later upload of the same bytes answers `200`
with the **existing** capture and `deduplicated: true`. A replay does not
re-associate that capture with another session or plan — the first association
stands, as everywhere else in this contract. The alternative, a second capture
row pointing at the same stored blob so the same processed image can belong to
two sessions, is not what "re-sending the same bytes returns the existing
capture" says and is flagged rather than chosen.

A complete single-request upload is recorded with `status: "uploaded"`;
`status: "draft"` is reserved for the resumable protocol's create step.

**A capture may show several targets.** One
processed image is one capture — a wide field or a mosaic pane genuinely
covers more than one catalogue object, and "the same image twice because it
shows two targets" is exactly the duplication content-addressing exists to
prevent. `capture.targetIds` (0 to 16 entries, deduplicated, order preserved)
replaces the single `targetId` in every response; `POST /v1/uploads`'
`metadata` still accepts `targetId` as a tolerated single-item alias for
`targetIds` (additive, for callers predating this decision) — a request
carrying both is their union. A replay's `targetIds` are **unioned** onto the
existing capture's list, idempotently: sending the same file with one target
already recorded and one new one leaves a single capture with both; sending it
with exactly the same target(s) changes nothing. `uploadResponse.targetsAdded`
counts how many were newly added by this call (0 on an unchanged replay).
Exceeding 16 distinct targets — in one request or once unioned onto an
existing capture — is `400` `invalid_request` and writes nothing: no partial
union, no partial write. `captures.target_id` remains in the persistence layer
as the first target, for a simple read; the join table is the truth.
`GET /v1/captures?targetId=…` lists captures whose `targetIds` contains it.

A capture is still linked to **at most one** session and **at most one**
plan — that part of the model is unchanged.

**Capability refusals.** Three capabilities gate the route, in this order,
after the read-only gate (`entitlement_read_only`) and before any byte is
read:

1. `capture.count_limit` — refused with `409`
   `capture_limit_reached` when the space's captures, plus its uploads in
   progress, plus this one, would exceed it (`0` is no allowance, the
   unlimited sentinel no cap). A file the space already holds (the
   deduplication by SHA-256) is never counted twice. Deleting a capture
   frees a place. A space over its limit after a subscription ended keeps
   every capture, readable and exportable; it only cannot add one until it
   is under the limit;
2. `storage.quota_bytes` — refused with `409` `storage_quota_reached` when it
   is `0`, or when `usedBytes + byteSize` would exceed it;
3. `storage.max_file_bytes` — refused with `413` `file_too_large` when it is
   `0`, or when `byteSize` exceeds it.

The count is checked first, then the quota: a space that holds its three
captures hears "your free space keeps 3 captures" and not "no media storage".
The quota is checked before the file's size on purpose: a profile that
carries `0` for both is told "media storage is not part of this
account", where "this file is too large" would be misleading. The refusal
body repeats `storage` (`usedBytes`, `quotaBytes`, `maxFileBytes`,
`captureCount`, `captureCountLimit`) so an interface can explain itself
without a second call, and names no offer, tier or product. `captureCount`
counts the stored captures and the uploads in progress, as `usedBytes`
counts their bytes; `GET /v1/captures` reports the stored captures.

Making a capture public needs the capability `capture.public`: every route
that does (the owner's visibility, the request for a review) answers `403`
`capture_public_unavailable` when the profile does not carry it. That is a
capability of the space, not the block on publishing
(`publishing_blocked`, a state of the person).

> **Zero means the opposite of what it means for `sync.plan_limit`.** A
> `storage.*` value of `0` is **no allowance at all** — the acceptance
> criterion for uploads is a refusal when the quota is zero. But
> `sync.plan_limit: 0` already means **no numeric cap** in shipped
> code. Both conventions are
> stated here because both are now load-bearing; unifying them (for instance
> on `-1` for "unlimited") would change existing behaviour and is flagged for
> decision, not done here.

No new capability name is required: `storage.max_file_bytes` and
`storage.quota_bytes` already exist in
[`capabilities.schema.json`](capabilities.schema.json). The open-source
profile has no cap on either (the unlimited sentinel); `capture.count_limit`, `capture.public` and
`portal.invitations` are further keys. Sessions, events and the debriefing are never
gated: plan correctness and the truth of the field are never a paid feature.

Captures are **private to their tenant** until their owner makes one public
`visibility` is `private` (the default of every capture)
or `public`. This contract carries no public URL and no slug; `originalName`
is echoed exactly as the person named the file and is never used as a
filesystem path. Nothing here makes a capture public: that is an explicit act
of its owner, through the routes of the space.

**The two checks.** A capture carries, for its owner,
`checks`: the result of the automatic check (`automatic`: `null` until it
has run, then `sky`, `undetermined` or `other`) and its time, the verification
flag (`team`: `null` until an administrator looks, then `approved` or `refused`,
with its time and, when refused, its reason `not_sky`, `offensive`,
`identifiable` or `other`), when the owner asked for a verification
(`reviewRequestedAt`), the notice of a private capture that did not pass and
the lifting of its private protection (`protectionNoticeAt`,
`protectionLiftedAt`), and its removal from the public gallery after reports
(`removedFromGalleryAt`, `null` again once it is approved). The machine
reason of the automatic check is never sent to the owner. What the owner may
do with these states is answered by one pure rule, written once
(`portal/captures/capture-state.js`, conformance fixture
`capture-state`); the routes that change them are the space's. The verification wins
over the automatic check: a private capture that did
not pass it may be made public once an administrator has approved it; the service
decides, and the phone only draws what it is given.

**Since when a capture is public.** A capture also carries
`madePublicAt`: the instant it last turned public, the date of
« Publique depuis le … ». The service writes it as the capture goes from
private to public (the owner's « Publique », or an administrator's « Valider » on a
verification the owner asked for) and clears it whenever the capture turns
private again (« Privée », « Refuser », the replacement of its image); a write
that keeps a capture public leaves it alone. It is on a public capture only
(absent while the capture is private), and `null` for a capture that was
already public when the service began to keep the date: nothing recorded it,
so it is unknown, never guessed, and a reader says « date inconnue » rather
than showing a date. The field is
optional on the resource (a reader of an earlier answer still validates) and
read only: no route writes it.

**Publication status (retired).** The publications are retired.
The service no longer writes `publication` on a capture,
and the field and its `publicationStatus` are out
of `capture.schema.json` and out of the phone: a capture's own `visibility`
replaces them.

**On the phone.** `GET /v1/captures` writes, on each item
that did not pass the automatic check and runs a notice, `protectionLiftsAt`:
the date the private protection is (or was) lifted, the notice's date plus
`ASTRO_PORTAL_CHECK_NOTICE_DAYS` (14 by default), computed by the service by
the rule `captureState`; `null` otherwise. Each item also carries `tags`:
the owner's own tags of the capture,
read only — the phone shows none and writes none. On the page itself it writes
`publishingBlocked`: whether the person is blocked from making a capture
public (four warnings). The phone shows the visibility, the two checks and
these two; it never changes a visibility, and uploading is never refused for
the block. Both fields are optional so that a reader of an earlier answer
still validates; the answer of an upload never carries `protectionLiftsAt`.

## The owner's captures

« Mon espace » → « Captures » on the web. Four routes of
the web session of a space, never of a device (a request that carries a
device bearer answers `403` `web_session_required`, even with a cookie
beside it); writes carry the session's CSRF token. Every route is bounded
by the space: another space's capture answers `404` `capture_not_found`,
exactly as one that does not exist. The shapes are
[`manage-captures.schema.json`](manage-captures.schema.json).

- `GET /v1/manage/captures` — the space's captures, **newest first**
  (`?before=<createdAt>,<id>` continues after the last item of a page,
  `?limit=` 50 by default and 200 at most, `?locale=` names the targets).
  Each capture is the resource of [`capture.schema.json`](capture.schema.json)
  plus `state` (the rule `captureState`: the state drawn, whether
  « Publique » may be chosen and why not, the « équipe » line, the date the
  private protection is lifted), `preview` (the stored bytes are an image a
  browser draws), `thumbnail` (the capture has its thumbnail), `showable` (the file can be shown in a gallery: a raster image, never
  a FITS, an XISF or a camera's raw file), `protectionLapsed` (a lifted
  protection was lifted by the end of its delay, not by « Accepter la
  vérification »), `detail` (targets, night,
  equipment, exposure, sky quality, and the **place, for its owner
  alone**) and `tags` (the owner's own tags, below). Around them: `storage`
  (with `captureCount`, and `captureCountLimit` only when the profile caps
  it), `publishing` (`capture.public`, the person's warnings, the end of a
  block on publishing while it lasts) and `names` (the names of the space
  refused on review, until another is saved).
- `GET /v1/manage/captures/{id}` — one capture, as the list gives it.
- `PATCH /v1/manage/captures/{id}` — exactly one of `{ "visibility":
"private" | "public" }`, `{ "requestReview": true }` (« Demander une
  vérification », a capture whose check is undetermined; else `409`
  `review_not_applicable`), `{ "acceptVerification": true }` (« Accepter la
  vérification », a private capture that did not pass the check; else `409`
