  `verification_not_applicable`). **The rule is the server's**: « Publique »
  is refused with `403` `capture_public_unavailable` when the profile's
  `capture.public` is false (true when the profile does not name it), and
  with `403` `publishing_blocked` while the person's block on publishing
  lasts (the error carries `publishingBlockedUntil`,
  the end of the block; a review request is refused the same way, uploading
  and « Privée » never are), and with `409` and the reason of
  `captureState` otherwise — `refused_by_team`, `check_pending`,
  `needs_review`, `review_pending`, `check_not_passed`. **A server with no
  team** (a private server: its owner is the only person who sees a capture,
  the list says `publishing.moderated: false`) runs no check: every capture is
  `sky`, is made public at once, has no `teamLine`, notice or lifting, and
  `requestReview` is `409` `review_not_applicable` there. Once the rule allows it, the **shared copy** is written first — the file the galleries
  serve: JPEG, without EXIF, XMP or IPTC, 4096 px at most on its long side,
  the original untouched — then the capture becomes public: a file that
  cannot be shown in a gallery is refused with `409` `not_showable`, and a
  copy that could not be written leaves the capture private with `503`
  `shared_copy_failed`. Neither a review request nor an accepted
  verification makes a capture public. `{ "tags": [...] }` replaces the whole list of the capture's own
  tags (`capture_tags`, `[]` removes them): each tag trimmed, the empty
  ones and the exact repetitions dropped, **never otherwise normalised**
  (case, accents and inner spaces kept); 40 characters at most each and 20
  tags at most, else `400` `invalid_request` and nothing written; written in
  any state of the capture. They are read in the order of their text, and
  shown in the private gallery only — never in the public gallery, whose
  tags are derived from the target's object type. A capture takes
  its target's generic tag (« Nébuleuse » for an emission nebula; the
  words of `gallery.schema.json` `$defs.tag`, keys in `x-keys`) among its own
  tags **when the service creates it** (and, once, for the captures that
  existed: migration 99), only when it has no generic tag yet — the owner's
  later edits are never overwritten, and the owner may remove it; a change
  of target replaces a tag that was the previous target's default. The
  stored word is the French one; every page and the phone translate it, and
  an owner's word that is none of these stays as typed. Answers
  `{ "capture" }`.
  The body is read under the common ceiling of the JSON bodies: past it,
  `413` `payload_too_large`, nothing read further, nothing written.
- `DELETE /v1/manage/captures/{id}` — « Supprimer la capture »: the row,
  its targets, its tags, its reports, the completed uploads that named it, then its
  stored bytes and the files derived from them (the thumbnail, the shared
  copy); `204`. A change of the domain `captures` is published, so a
  paired device reads its list again.
- `GET /v1/manage/captures/{id}/media` — the bytes of the capture for its
  owner: the type from the bytes, a raster image shown and anything else an
  attachment, `nosniff` and the sandboxing policy,
  `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, one byte range honoured
  (`Accept-Ranges: bytes`, `206`, `416`).
  `?variant=thumbnail` serves its thumbnail instead (WebP, without
  metadata), `404` `capture_not_found` while it has none. `?variant=preview`
  serves the copy without location a gallery serves (JPEG, 4 096 px
  at most, never enlarged), written at the first need: the opened capture's
  image over its thumbnail; `404` for a file that cannot have one. `?download=1`
  serves the original as an attachment under the name it was sent
  with (`Content-Disposition: attachment; filename*=UTF-8''…`), a TIFF
  included wherever the owner is: the owner's download of « Mon espace ›
  Captures »; a gallery never serves the original.

## Images of a capture, its stage, the device routes

One clean migration (62): no compatibility layer, the dev
database may be reset.

**The stage of a capture.** `stage` is `draft` (« Brouillon ») or
`finalized` (« Finalisée »), distinct from the visibility. An upload from a
device creates a draft, private; one from the web session a finalized
capture, private. A draft is never public (`409 capture_draft` on
`visibility: public`) and in no gallery; a public capture does not go back
to a draft (`409 capture_public`). Every capture stored before migration 62
is finalized. Migration 69: the owner's resource
carries `finalizedAt`, written once at the passage « Brouillon » →
« Finalisée » (a web upload finalized, the stage written by the web session
or by a device) and never cleared nor moved by a later passage; `null` for a
draft and for a capture finalized before the date was kept. Never in a
gallery.

**The names of the targets.** `GET /v1/captures/{id}` and the writes that
answer the capture (`PATCH /v1/captures/{id}`, `PATCH /v1/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}`,
`POST /v1/captures/{id}/acquisition/sync`) take an optional `?locale=`, a
shipped locale tag: the `name` of each target of `detail.targets` is then the
one the galleries show in that language (« Nébuleuse du Cœur », « Herznebel »),
the English one when the locale is absent, not shipped, or the target has no
name in it.

**Images of a capture** ([`capture-variant.schema.json`](capture-variant.schema.json)).
A capture has **one main image** and **variants** — no history, no « go
back » (word on screen: « variante », « image principale »). An upload
naming an existing `captureId` with bytes the space does not hold adds a
variant, not main, private (refused past `capture.variant_limit`, the
variants besides the main image: `409 capture_variant_limit_reached`); the
same bytes as any image of the space are « already present » and store
nothing ([uploads.md](uploads.md), « Checking before sending »). The
capture's `sha256`, `byteSize` and `originalName` are its main image's; the
galleries, the thumbnails and the public page serve it. The automatic check
belongs to each image (it runs on each one after its upload); the visibility
and the verification stay on the capture. Every stored image counts
in the space's storage. The owner's gestures, from the web session only (a
device bearer: `403 web_session_required`):

- `PATCH /v1/manage/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}` `{ "main": true }` —
  « Utiliser comme image principale »: the image must have passed its own
  automatic check (`409 variant_check_not_passed`); the former main image
  becomes a variant and keeps its visibility (public while the capture is
  public). `{ "public": true | false }` — a variant's own visibility: public
  only while its capture is public (`409 capture_not_public`), after its own
  check, under the rights of « Publique » (`capture.public`, the block on
  publishing); the main image follows its capture (`409 main_image`).
  Answers `{ "capture" }`.
- `DELETE /v1/manage/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}` — a variant and its
  files; the main image is never deleted alone (`409 main_image`: choose
  another main image first, or delete the capture), nor, therefore, a
  capture's last image.
- `PATCH /v1/manage/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}`
  `{ "title": string | null }` (90 characters at most) or
  `{ "description": string | null }` (280 at most), one field per request —
  the owner's names of an image, the main one or a variant. The outer
  spaces are trimmed and nothing else changes; a blank value or `null`
  gives the name by default back (`400 invalid_request` past the bound or
  with two fields). Every image carries `title`, `description` (`null` by
  default) and, on the answers that list every image of a capture,
  `arrivalRank` (its rank of arrival, the first sent is 1, computed and
  never stored): a screen names an image without a title « Image
  principale » or « Variante N ». The original of each image downloads from
  `GET /v1/manage/captures/{id}/media?download=1&imageId=…`, never from a
  gallery.

A visitor sees the main image, then the variants that are public while the
capture is public, newest first: `GET /v1/gallery/{id}` writes them in
`variants` (their files at `GET /v1/gallery/{id}/media?imageId=…`, with
their `title`, `description` and `arrivalRank`) after `mainImage` (the
main image's names), and the private gallery shows the same ones by the same
rule (`?imageId=` of its media route). A draft is in no gallery. On every media route, `?imageId=` selects an image and
`variant=thumbnail|preview` stays a size. `GET /v1/manage/captures/{id}` and
`GET /v1/captures/{id}` carry `variants[]`, every image, the main one first.

**Device routes** (a device token or the web session):

- `GET /v1/captures` — each item also carries `stage`, `visibilityEditable`
  (never for a device), `thumbnail`, `galleryLabel`, `nightDate`, `width`,
  `height`, `targets` (catalogue targets with designation, type, aliases and
  position; personal targets with name and label), `mainImageId`,
  `imageCount`; additive: `title`,
  `acquisitionSummary` and `revision` (see [A capture written by two hands](#a-capture-written-by-two-hands)).
- `GET /v1/captures/{id}` — the owner's capture (the resource of
  `GET /v1/manage/captures/{id}`, the place included) with `variants[]`.
- `PATCH /v1/captures/{id}` — a device changes `stage`, one field of the
  metadata or the tags as the owner's hand, or proposes as a producer (`403
visibility_web_only` when it names the visibility); the web session (with
  its CSRF token) `stage`, `visibility` or both, the stage first, under the
  rules of `PATCH /v1/manage/captures/{id}`. One change per request, with
  `If-Match: "<revision>"` (below).
- `GET /v1/captures/{id}/media?variant=thumbnail|preview[&imageId=…]` —
  the thumbnail, or the copy without location (JPEG, 4096 px at most), to a
  device token; without `variant`, the original, to the web session only on
  this route (`403 web_session_required`). Another space's capture: `404`.
- `POST /v1/uploads/check` — [uploads.md](uploads.md), « Checking before sending ».
- `GET /v1/space/storage` ([`space-storage.schema.json`](space-storage.schema.json))
  — `usedBytes`, `quotaBytes`, `remainingBytes`, `maxFileBytes`,
  `captureCount`, `captureCountLimit`, `variantLimit` and the composed
  `capabilities` of `GET /v1/capabilities`.
- `POST /v1/targets/personal`, `GET /v1/targets/personal`
  ([`personal-target.schema.json`](personal-target.schema.json)) — the
  space's « cibles personnelles », in the target library with the
  catalogue's identifiers; a capture names one among its `targetIds`;
  another space's is `404 target_not_found`.

**Capabilities.** No new key: the contract keeps its fourteen.
The stacking follows `analysis.progression` (« Synchronisation avancée+ » and
the administrator true, the free account and « Synchronisation avancée »
false). The open core grants `analysis.logs` and `analysis.progression`:
everything is open on a private server.

**Removed.** The capture detail's `exposureSeconds` (always `null`, no
source) is gone from the contract and the service.

## The metadata of a capture

One clean migration (69): no data to carry, no
compatibility layer.

**Reading.** The owner's resource (`GET /v1/manage/captures/{id}`,
`GET /v1/captures/{id}`, the list) carries `metadata`
([`manage-captures.schema.json`](manage-captures.schema.json) `$defs.metadata`),
read through four levels — **plan → night → capture → image** — with the
origin of each value `{ value, source, at, edited }`, `source` among `plan`,
`night`, `astro-sync`, `photo`, `import`, `owner` (`null`: nothing holds it).
`null` is taken from the level above: the title from the targets, the
equipment from the night (or its plan), the acquisition from the night's
steps for the capture's targets **until a line is corrected**; then the
capture keeps its own lines, the corrected ones `edited` (« · modifié »).
Computed at reading and never stored: the integration of each line and the
total, the sky quality (`computed.skyQuality`, the plan's Bortle), the Moon
(`computed.moon`: illumination at the night's date, the instant it sets during
the night, from the night's place). The **place** is the night's, for the
owner only: a capture never has one of its own, and no gallery carries it.
Every image carries `titleSource` (`astro-sync`, `import`, `owner`; `null`
without a title) and `edited`; the resource carries `finalizedAt` (above).

**The texts of an image in the reader's language.** An image's `title` and
`description` are the owner's alone: what he typed, `null` while he typed
none. The texts a producer generated for the image — Astro Sync's name of the
processing and its settings (« Natural render · Contrast 3.6 · Colours
+20% ») and the pipeline's description — travel apart, in the ten languages
of the locale registry, English required: `generatedTitle` (90 characters a
language) and `generatedDescription` (280), sent at the upload
(`variantGeneratedTitle`, `variantGeneratedDescription`) or on the image
route (`generatedTitle`, `generatedDescription`), and carried by the
owner's resource and by both galleries. Every reader shows the same: the
owner's text, else the generated one in its language (the tag the registry's
device rule matches), else English, else the name by default (« Image
principale », « Variante N ») or no description. The owner's edit never
erases the generated texts: clearing his title shows the generated one again.
They are fixed when sent: renaming a processing later changes no image
online. **What made an image** travels with the image the same way: `variantProcessing`
at the upload or `processing` on the image route (`{ software?, steps? }`, 120
and 500 characters), kept on the image. The capture shows the steps of its
main image: those of the image that creates a capture are the capture's;
those of a variant wait on the variant and become the capture's when the
owner picks it as the main image (`PATCH /v1/manage/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}`
`main`), never over steps the owner wrote. Generated texts arriving on an image whose title the same producer
proposed in one language before clear that title (and the description the
owner never wrote).

**The owner's hand.** `PATCH /v1/manage/captures/{id}` (web session) and
`PATCH /v1/captures/{id}` take `{ "metadata": { <field>: value } }` — one
field per request (`metadataChange`): `title` (80), `description` (2 000),
`notes` (4 000, never published), `calibration` (120), `sensor`
(`{ temperatureC, binning }`), `processing.software` (120),
`processing.steps` (500), `equipment.telescopeId`, `equipment.cameraId`,
`equipment.mountId`, `equipment.filterIds` (the space's equipment, `404
equipment_not_found` otherwise), `sessionId` (« Changer la nuit de la
capture »: another night of the space, `404 session_not_found`), and
`acquisition`: `{ add }`, `{ line, set }`, `{ line, delete: true }` (`404
acquisition_line_not_found`) or `null`, and (web session only: `403 web_session_required` from a device) `targetIds`,
the capture's whole ordered list of targets, 16 at most, `[]` removing them
all (« Ajouter / changer la cible »; `404 target_not_found` for a target
neither of the catalogue nor the space's own). `null` gives a field back to its
source — the value a producer proposed if there was one, else the level
above; any other value is the owner's (`source: owner`, `edited: true`).
Refusals: `400 invalid_metadata` with `field` and `limit`; `403
entitlement_read_only` on **every** write of this section in the read-only
window of an ended subscription (producers included); `404` for another
space's capture, night or equipment. `PATCH
/v1/manage/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}` also takes `{ "title",
"description" }` together: `titleSource: owner`, `edited`.

**Who writes what, by which credential.** (a) The web session: every field
above, always as the owner's hand. (b) A device token, as the owner's hand
on the phone: `title`, `description`, `notes`, `acquisition`, `calibration`
through `PATCH /v1/captures/{id}` `metadata`; additive: also `sensor`,
`processing.software`, `processing.steps`, `equipment.telescopeId`,
`cameraId`, `mountId`, `filterIds` and `targetIds` in `metadata`, and the tags
through `{ "tags": […] }` (the web route's rule: the whole list, 20 tags of 40
characters, never normalised); the rest — `sessionId` — `403
web_session_required` ([A capture written by two hands](#a-capture-written-by-two-hands)); additive: the title and the description of an image
through `PATCH /v1/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}` `{ "title"?, "description"? }`
with no `source` (one or both, a string or `null`; the owner's hand, judged
against the capture's revision). Never the visibility (`visibility_web_only`),
the main image, the deletion or the original: web session only. (c) A device token, as a **producer** (Astro
Sync, the import of the old nights): **proposals** carrying their source
(`astro-sync`, `import`) — at the upload (`uploadMetadata`: `title`,
`description`, `acquisition[]`, `calibration`, `sensor`, `processing`,
`variantTitle`, `variantDescription`, under `metadataSource`; absent, Astro
Sync's token or an image of Astro Sync propose under `astro-sync`, a phone
without it is refused `400 invalid_metadata`, the web session proposes
nothing at an upload), on a capture that exists (`PATCH /v1/captures/{id}`
`{ "proposed": { "source", … } }`), and on an image that exists (`PATCH
/v1/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}` `{ "title"?, "description"?,
"source" }`, never over an image whose texts the owner wrote). The resumable
create step keeps the proposals and applies them at completion.

**The rule of proposals.** A value the owner edited (`edited`, an image
whose texts he wrote) is never overwritten nor cleared; a value proposed
earlier that nobody touched is updated; a missing one is added; nothing is
deleted (an acquisition line is matched by its filter and its exposure; a
line the owner deleted is not brought back). The answer lists what was left
— `skipped: [{ field, reason }]`, `reason` `edited` or `deleted` (on
`proposed`, the image route, and an upload's answer when not empty). The
same proposals twice change nothing. Titles of the images of one capture
need not be unique (the import numbers them). The photo: the program a main
image names (`Software`, XMP `CreatorTool`) becomes `processing.software`
under `photo` while the capture has none; nothing else is read from it,
never a position nor a date.

**What a visitor reads.** [`gallery.schema.json`](gallery.schema.json) and
[`private-gallery.schema.json`](private-gallery.schema.json) carry the
capture's `metadata` for a visitor: `title`, `description`, `acquisition[]`
(filter, frames, exposure, gain, integration — no origin), the total,
`calibration`, `sensor`, `processing`, the filters' names and
`moonIllumination`; never the place, the notes, an origin, `edited`,
`finalizedAt`, the file name, the fingerprint or the dates of sending. A
draft is in no gallery.

## A capture and its session

Additive throughout.

**The link capture → session.** A capture belongs to **one session at most**
(`capture.sessionId`). `GET /v1/captures?sessionId=` lists a session's
captures (it adds up with `?targetId=`). `PATCH /v1/manage/captures/{id}` with
`{ "metadata": { "sessionId": null } }` detaches the capture (a session id
attaches it to another one, as before: `404 session_not_found`). A capture
uploaded **without** `sessionId` but with a target and a night date joins the
session of that `eveningDate` that carries the target (the place breaks a tie);
otherwise it stays without a session: no session is invented for a capture.

**One capture per session and target.** An upload with a `sessionId`, one
target and no `captureId` that finds a capture of that session and that target
**with an image** does not create a second capture:

- the capability `capture.variant_limit` is read, never an offer's name: at
  `0` the new image **replaces** the capture's image (the old bytes released
  from the quota); the answer says `replaced: true` and the capture
  `replacedAt` and `replacedSource` (the source of the image that was replaced,
  `phone` in the usual case). The screen says so;
- above `0` (or unlimited) the new image is **added**: it becomes the main
  image when it comes from Astro Sync (`astro-sync`, `stacking`) and the former
  one becomes a variant, otherwise it is a variant; beyond the limit, the
  existing `409 capture_variant_limit_reached`;
- a capture of role `plan` or `progression` is outside the rule. `capture.variant_limit`
  is a number per profile; every profile of the hosted service (the free
  space too) and a private server are unlimited;
- two precautions of the service: a **public** capture keeps its
  main image, so an image from Astro Sync is added to it as a variant, never as
  the main one; and a replacement on a public capture (a space that keeps one
  image per capture) turns the capture **private**, the new image having
  passed no check yet. The same rule applies at the end of a resumable upload
  (`POST /v1/uploads/{id}/finalize` answers `replaced` too), and `replacedAt`
  and `replacedSource` are on the owner's resource of the capture as well;
- when that replacement turned a **public** capture private, the
  capture says `replacedMadePrivate: true` (present only then), in the answer
  of the upload and on the resource, until the owner next writes the
  visibility. The screens tell the owner at the replacement (« Votre capture
  est redevenue privée, car son image a été remplacée. Publiez-la à nouveau
  si vous le souhaitez. ») and show it on the capture's visibility line
  afterwards (« Redevenue privée : l’image a été remplacée »): a public
  capture must be validated again explicitly.

**The lines of "Intégrations" of a capture** (the word replaces
« Acquisition » on the web; the field stays `metadata.acquisition`). Each
`acquisitionLine` gains `provenance`, **derived by the service and read only**:
`declared` (origin `session`), `measured` (origin `astro-sync`, and origin
`night` when the night's step is of origin `analysis`), `manual` (`owner`,
`import`, and `night` otherwise). Its `origin` enum gains `session`. A line
also reads `computerLabel`: the computer Astro Sync measured it on
when it sent the name with the line (`proposedAcquisitionLine.computerLabel`,
80 characters at most, accepted from a producer only; `null` otherwise, and
for every line typed by the owner).
`POST /v1/manage/captures/{id}/acquisition/sync` (« Synchroniser avec la
session »; web session only, no body) fills the capture's lines from its
session and target: Astro Sync's measured steps when the session has an
analysis, else one declared line per target from the debriefing or the events
(seconds, frames and filter when known). It replaces the uncorrected lines of
origin `session`, `night` and `astro-sync`, keeps the lines of origin `owner`
and `import` and every line corrected by hand, and answers `{ acquisition,
capture }`. A capture with no session answers `409 capture_without_session`;
`404 capture_not_found`, `403 entitlement_read_only`. A declared line whose
frame count is not known has `frameCount: 0` and `exposureSeconds: 0`.

**An intermediate capture of a multi-night plan.** The detail of a capture
(`detail.intermediate`, `manage-captures.schema.json`) is
`{ projectId, projectName, sessionsDone[], sessionsPlanned[] }` (each night
`{ eveningDate, sessionId, planId }`) when the capture's session belongs to a
multi-night plan that is **not finished**; `null` otherwise.

## A capture written by two hands

The phone's page of a capture joins the web gallery, and the **web and the
phone write the same capture**. Additive throughout: nothing is removed or
renamed, no capability is added (`analysis.logs` and `capture.variant_limit`
keep their meaning; a right is read, never an offer's name). Reading is never
held by a right.

**One field per request, against a revision.** A capture has a `revision`
(`capture.schema.json` `capture.revision`, an integer that is 1 at the creation
and moves by 1 at each write of the capture that changed something: a metadata
field, the tags, the stage, the visibility, the session, an image added,
replaced or removed, a producer's proposal that changed a value, a
synchronisation of the lines). It is on the owner's resource, on each item of
`GET /v1/captures` and of `GET /v1/manage/captures`, and in the answer of every
write. **No route takes the whole record**: an owner write is one change —
`{ "metadata": { <one field> } }`, `{ "tags": […] }`, `{ "stage": … }`,
`{ "visibility": … }` — or one gesture (a synchronisation); a screen saves each
field when the person confirms it, never a form. The request names the
revision it read in `If-Match: "<revision>"` (quoted, a positive integer;
`manage-captures.schema.json` `ifMatch`) on `PATCH /v1/captures/{id}`,
`PATCH /v1/manage/captures/{id}` and both `…/acquisition/sync` routes. A
producer's `proposed` and the producers' image route (a body with a `source`)
send none (the rule of proposals already protects what the owner edited); the
phone's own write of an image's texts on that route, with no `source`, names the
revision like any owner write; a write without `If-Match`
(an older client) is applied and moves the revision.

**The conflict is judged by field, by the service.** A write is refused with
**`409 capture_conflict`** only if the field it writes changed after the
revision named; a change of another field never refuses it, the write is
applied and the answer carries the new revision. The check and the write are
one step. The body is the error envelope with `error.field` (the metadata field
by its name — `title`, `description`, `notes`, `calibration`,
`processing.software`, `processing.steps`, `sensor`, `equipment.telescopeId`,
`equipment.cameraId`, `equipment.mountId`, `equipment.filterIds`, `sessionId`,
`targetIds` — or `tags`, `stage`, `visibility`, `acquisition`, `imageTexts`),
`error.line` (the id of the line, for `acquisition`; the id of the image, for
`imageTexts`) and **`capture`**, the owner's resource
as it stands, nothing of the write applied. Judged line by line,
`acquisition` conflicts for `{ line, set }` and `{ line, delete }` only if _that
line_ was added, changed or deleted after the revision named, never for
`{ add }`; a synchronisation and `acquisition: null` (back to the night's steps)
replace many lines and conflict if _any_ line changed after the revision. A
write that leaves the stored value as it is changes nothing, does not move the
revision and never conflicts (two clients that rename to the same title). An
image added, replaced or removed, a main image chosen, a variant's visibility,
`requestReview`, `acceptVerification` and `skyAnnotations` move the revision and
take no `If-Match` conflict of their own (their own `409` stay). A revision the
capture never had (above the current one, `"0"`, not quoted) is `400
invalid_if_match`. Nothing is written on a refusal.

| The case                                                                        | The answer                                                                                     |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| The phone renames at revision 5; the web tagged at 6                            | applied, revision 7: both kept                                                                 |
| The phone and the web both rename at revision 5; the web's write came first (6) | the phone gets `409 capture_conflict`, `field: "title"`, the capture at 6 with the web's title |
| The phone retries the rename at revision 6                                      | applied, revision 7                                                                            |
| Both rename to the same title                                                   | the second changes nothing, 200, the revision does not move                                    |
| The phone sets line L at 5; the web changed another line at 6                   | applied: both kept                                                                             |
| The phone sets line L at 5; the web changed L at 6                              | `409`, `field: "acquisition"`, `line: "L"`, the current capture                                |
| The phone deletes line L at 5; the web deleted L at 6                           | `404 acquisition_line_not_found` (the existing refusal)                                        |
| The phone adds a line at 5; anything changed at 6                               | applied (`{ add }` never conflicts)                                                            |
| « Synchroniser avec la session » at 5; the web edited a line at 6               | `409`, `field: "acquisition"`, no `line`, the current capture                                  |
| « Synchroniser avec la session » at 5; the web changed the title at 6           | applied                                                                                        |
| « Finaliser cette capture » at 5; Astro Sync replaced the image at 6            | applied (an image is not the stage)                                                            |
| A write without `If-Match`                                                      | applied, revision + 1                                                                          |
| A producer's `proposed` changed a value Astro Sync proposed earlier             | applied, revision + 1; nothing over a value the owner edited (`skipped`)                       |

**What a device writes as the owner's hand** (`PATCH /v1/captures/{id}`, the
phone): `{ "metadata": … }` for `manage-captures.schema.json`
`deviceMetadataField` — `title`, `description`, `notes`, `calibration`,
`sensor`, `processing.software`, `processing.steps`, `equipment.telescopeId`,
`equipment.cameraId`, `equipment.mountId`, `equipment.filterIds`, `acquisition`
and `targetIds` —, `{ "tags": […] }` (the web route's rule: the whole list, the
empty ones and the repetitions dropped, **never otherwise normalised**, 40
characters and 20 tags at most, else `400 invalid_request`; written in any state
of the capture), `{ "stage": "finalized" | "draft" }` (« Finaliser cette
capture ») and, `{ "qualityPrivate": boolean }` / `{ "qualityPublic":
boolean }` (the owner's two switches of the quality of the images in the
galleries, one per request). The limits, the `404 equipment_not_found`, the `404
target_not_found` and the `403 entitlement_read_only` of the read-only window
are those of the web route. What stays the web session's alone, each `403`
(`web_session_required`, `visibility_web_only` for the visibility): the
visibility, the main image, an image's visibility, the deletion,
the original (`GET /v1/captures/{id}/media` without `variant`), changing the
capture's **session** (`metadata.sessionId`), `skyAnnotations`. There is no
public gallery and no original on the phone.

**« Synchroniser avec la session » for a device, and for the image of a plan.**
`POST /v1/captures/{id}/acquisition/sync` (either credential, CSRF for the
web session; no body; `If-Match` optional) answers `200 { acquisition, capture }`
and refuses as `POST /v1/manage/captures/{id}/acquisition/sync` does (`400
invalid_if_match`, `403 entitlement_read_only`, `404 capture_not_found`, `409
capture_without_session`, `409 capture_conflict`): one function behind two
routes. On a capture of role `plan` (the image of a multi-night plan) both
routes fill the lines from **the sessions the image combines**
(`plan.combinedNightIds`): the measured lines of the multi-night plan's
integrations of those nights, else the declared ones, **one line per session,
target and family**, each carrying `sessionId` and `eveningDate`
(`acquisitionLine`); the uncorrected lines of origin `astro-sync` and `session`
are replaced, those of the owner and the corrected ones kept. `409
capture_without_session` is for a capture that has neither a session nor a plan
(no `projectId`).

**Both qualities.** A capture shows two, side by side:

- **the session's** — the medians of the night Astro Sync measured, with a way
  to the session: `detail.sessionQuality` of the owner's resource,
  `{ sessionId, eveningDate, frameCount, skyBackground, fwhm, eccentricity }`,
  the units and the nulls of `journal.schema.json` `quality`; `null` for a capture
  without a session and for a session Astro Sync has not analysed;
- **the stacked image's own** — `imageQuality`, measured by Astro Sync on the
  master it stacked (linear, before any stretch) when it sends it: `{ fwhmPixels,
fwhmArcsec | null, eccentricity | null, starCount, measuredOn: "linear",
measuredAt, tool: { name, version } }` (`capture.schema.json` `imageQuality`).
  A producer proposes it in `uploadMetadata.imageQuality` and in the producers'
  image route (`PATCH /v1/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}`, `imageQuality`
  with `source`); it is read as `variants[].quality` and, for the main image, as
  `quality` on the owner's resource (`null` when none). The phone and the web
  session never send it (`400 invalid_metadata`, `field: "imageQuality"`).
  **Written under `analysis.logs`** (the capability that already holds the
  session's quality): without it the service drops the field, stores the image
  all the same and lists `{ "field": "imageQuality", "reason": "capability" }` in
  `skipped`; a private server stores it. **Reading it needs no capability.** An
  image processed elsewhere (stretched, deconvolved) has none.

**What the lists and the cards read.** On each item of `GET /v1/captures`:
`title` (the capture's own, else `null`), `acquisitionSummary` (`{ lineCount,
frameCount, exposureSeconds | null, integrationSeconds }`, or `null` without a
line: « Intégration 4 h », « 40 × 180 s »), `revision`, with the fields of
« Images of a capture » (`thumbnail`, `galleryLabel`, `nightDate`, `stage`,
`replacedAt`…). On a line of « Intégrations » the computer that measured it is
`computerLabel`. On a night of `detail.intermediate`,
`acquiredSeconds`.

**An image's title and description, written by the owner on the phone.**
`PATCH /v1/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}` by a device token, a body of
`{ "title"?, "description"? }` and no `source` — one or both, a string or `null`
(`capture-variant.schema.json` `deviceTexts`; the bounds, 90 and 280 characters,
the trimming and the refusals are those of `PATCH
/v1/manage/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}`: a blank or `null` text gives the
generated text of the image back, else the name by default; the generated texts
are never written nor cleared by it). It **overwrites** what is there — a
producer's proposal and the owner's own earlier text alike — and is the owner's
hand (`titleSource: owner`, `edited`: a producer's proposal never changes the
texts afterwards). The request may carry `If-Match: "<revision>"`: it is
refused with `409 capture_conflict` (`error.field: "imageTexts"`, `error.line`:
the image's id, the current `capture`; nothing written) only if the title or
the description of _that image_ changed after that revision — by the web or by
another device —, never for a change of another image or of another field of the
capture. A write that leaves both texts as they were changes nothing and does
not move the revision; one that changed them moves it once. The web route
(`PATCH /v1/manage/captures/{id}/variants/{imageId}`) writes the same texts and
is judged by the same field for the device that wrote before it. Answers `200`
`{ capture, skipped: [] }` (`manage-captures.schema.json` `proposedAnswer`,
`skipped` empty: the route never skips what the owner writes); `403
entitlement_read_only` in the read-only window of an ended subscription; `404
capture_not_found`, `404 image_not_found`; `400 invalid_request` for a text over
its bound (`field`, `limit`) or a value that is neither a string nor `null`,
`400 invalid_if_match`. A `source` in the body is a producer's proposal, as
before.

**What a client does.** Nothing is written offline: a pencil waits for the
network and the screen says so (no queue of edits, so no conflict at the
return). The phone writes the title, the tags, the description, the lines of
« Intégrations », the stage, the target, the equipment and the title and
description of each image — not the session —;
on a `409 capture_conflict` it shows the current value (`capture`), says the
field changed on another device, and the person makes the change again with the
new revision. The web does the same on its side.

**As the service implements it (additive to the above).**

- **The lines arrive filled, without a touch.** A capture that joins a session
  — at its upload (`POST /v1/uploads`, an image of a plan included), by the
  owner's hand (`metadata.sessionId`), as a definition made on a night — and
  every capture of a session whose debriefing or rework is written, has its
  lines of « Intégrations » filled as « Synchroniser avec la session » would
  (declared from the debriefing, as the rework left it; replaced by the
  measured ones when Astro Sync's analysis arrives). The lines the owner
  corrected or added are never replaced; a line is never doubled. « Synchroniser
  avec la session » stays the gesture that asks again.
- **What moves the revision is each write**, so an upload that brings a title, a
  quality and lines moves it once for each of them; a client reads the
  revision from the answer or the next `GET`, never counts.
- **Judging.** A write that would leave the stored value as it was (the same
  title, the tags in another order, a line set to the values it has, a
  synchronisation with nothing to change) answers `200`, moves nothing and never
  conflicts, whatever `If-Match` it carries — which must still be a revision the
  capture had (`400 invalid_if_match` otherwise).
- **`detail.sessionQuality`** is on the single resource (`GET /v1/captures/{id}`
  and the answer of each write), not on the items of `GET /v1/manage/captures`;
  `quality` and `variants[].quality` are on both.
- **The lines of the image of a plan** have the identifier
  `plan:<sessionId>:<targetId>:<family>` (`declared` for a declared line) and
  carry `sessionId` and `eveningDate`.
- **`acquisitionSummary`** reads the capture's own lines, else its night's steps
  for its targets; `null` for a capture with neither. `title` is the capture's
  own title.
- **The quality of an image** is also refused to the phone on the producers'
  image route (`400 invalid_metadata`, `field: "imageQuality"`), as on the
  upload; `null` clears it.
- **Audit and signal.** Each owner write names `capture.field_changed` in the
  space's audit (the credential, the names of the fields, never a value) and
  tells the other hand to read again (`captures`).

## Sky solutions

See [`sky-solution.schema.json`](sky-solution.schema.json). One
clean migration (64, `capture-sky-solutions`; 63 is the weather lot's).

**What it is.** The sky solution of an image is a typed object, never shown
to a person: field centre (J2000), scale, orientation (`rotationDeg`, the
position angle of the image's up direction from north towards east),
field, parity, the pixel size it was solved at, the time and the solver
(`source`: `header`, `astap`, `astrometry-net`, `import`). It is computed on
the person's computer by Astro Sync (or read from a header NINA or ASIAIR
already solved, or carried over by an import). It belongs to **one image** of a capture — the main image or a
variant — and to that image's SHA-256: deleting the image deletes it,
and so does deleting the capture or the space. The centre of the field is a
position in the sky; it says nothing of the place on Earth.

**Routes.**

- `PUT /v1/captures/{id}/sky-solution` `{ sha256, solution }` — a device
  token of the capture's space only (no token, or a web session: `401
device_authentication_required`; another space's capture: `404
capture_not_found`). `sha256` names the image the solution was computed on;
  no image of the capture holding it is `409 image_changed` (the capture
  changed since). Proportions more than 1 % away from the stored image's are
  `422 sky_solution_aspect_mismatch` (a reduced copy is accepted, a cropped
  or turned image is not). Idempotent by digest: `201` a new solution, `200`
  the same one again (`changed: false`) or a replacement. Answers `{
skySolution, created, changed }`.
- `GET /v1/captures/{id}/sky-solution[?imageId=]` — a device token or the
  web session: `{ skySolution }` (the main image's, or a variant's), `404
sky_solution_not_found` without one.
- `DELETE /v1/captures/{id}/sky-solution[?imageId=]` — the owner's web
  session with its CSRF token (a device: `403 web_session_required`).
- `GET /v1/captures?skySolution=missing` — the captures whose **main image**
  has no solution, whatever device or page sent them (Astro Sync's
  « Annotations »). Every item of `GET /v1/captures` also says
  `mainImageSource` and `skySolved`.
- `GET /v1/captures/{id}/original[?imageId=]` — **the original for the
  resolution**: a device token of the capture's space only (no
  token, a visitor or a web session: `401`; another space's capture: `404`),
  the captures sent from the phone and the web included. `Range` honoured
  (one range, `206`/`416`, `If-Range` on the ETag); `ETag` and
  `X-Astro-Image-Sha256` the image's digest, `X-Astro-Image-Id` its id,
  `X-Astro-Original-Name` the name it was sent with (percent-encoded UTF-8),
  `Content-Disposition: attachment`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. The
  owner's media route (`GET /v1/captures/{id}/media`) keeps its own
  rules.
- `PATCH /v1/manage/captures/{id}` (or `PATCH /v1/captures/{id}` from a device)
  `{ "qualityPrivate": boolean }` / `{ "qualityPublic": boolean }` — the owner's two switches « Afficher la qualité en
  privé » (on by default) and « Afficher la qualité en public » (off by
  default), one pair per capture, written whether or not a quality was
  measured. The owner's capture carries them as `qualityShown: { private,
public }`. The private gallery's capture page carries `quality` (on the
  capture and on each variant it shows, `gallery.schema.json` `imageQuality`)
  only while the first is on, the public gallery's (`GET /v1/gallery/{id}`)
  only while the second is on; a visitor reads nothing otherwise.
- `PATCH /v1/manage/captures/{id}` `{ "skyAnnotations": boolean }` — the
  owner's switch « Afficher le plate solving » (one per capture, on by
  default; `409 sky_solution_not_found` while no image has a solution). The
  owner's capture carries `skySolution` (the main image's, with
  `annotationsEnabled`) as soon as one exists, and each image `skySolved`.
- The gallery (`GET /v1/gallery/{id}`) carries `skySolution` on the capture
  and on each public variant **only** while that image has a solution and the
  switch is on (absent otherwise: the page of before); a variant without one
  hides nothing of the main image's.
- `GET /v1/gallery/{id}/annotations[?imageId=][&magnitude=]` — open to
  anyone, the gallery's one `404 capture_not_found` otherwise: the
  annotations the portal draws alone (gnomonic projection, its target
  catalogue and the HYG stars it has installed; no `solve-field`, no
  `wcsinfo`), in the pixels of the copy the gallery serves — the capture's
  targets first (`target: true`), two other catalogue objects, the stars to
  `magnitude` (10 by default; `99` = every star of the catalogue in the field),
  the list `PlateOverlay` reads. The stars come in **batches by threshold**
  (3.5, 5.5, 8.5, 10, all: at most 400, 800, 2000, 4000 and 10000 stars,
  brightest first) and the viewer asks for the batch of its selector. A star
  fainter than magnitude 8.5 that has no name of its own (a proper name or a
  Bayer–Flamsteed designation) is a **point**: `names: []`, no label. The
  answer carries `starsAvailable`, `false` when the server has no HYG star
  catalogue installed (the annotations then hold the catalogue's objects
  only, and nothing of that is kept: the next request looks again).
- `GET /v1/captures/{id}/sky-solution/annotations[?imageId=][&magnitude=]`
  The same answer for the owner's own capture, read by a device
  token or the web session: the phone's full-screen viewer draws the owner's
  sky whether the capture is public or not and whatever the switch says (the
  switch only decides what a visitor sees). `404 sky_solution_not_found`
  while the image has none, `404 capture_not_found` for another space's
  capture.
- `GET /v1/portals/{portalToken}/annotations/{captureId}[?imageId=][&magnitude=]`
  The same annotations for the image a visitor of a **private
  gallery** sees, behind the gallery's own gate (the media route's: a capture
  may be private, so the public route is no way in); the gallery's one
  `404 gallery_not_found` otherwise (the gate refuses, the capture or the
  image is not shown, the image has no solution, the switch is off),
  `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. The private
  gallery's capture page reads the solution of each image it shows from its
  own view, not from `GET /v1/portals/{portalToken}`.

**The private server's fallback.** `sky.solve_on_server` is a **server
feature**, read from the server's environment (`ASTRO_PORTAL_SKY_SOLVE_ON_SERVER=1`
with `ASTRO_PORTAL_SOLVE_FIELD` and `ASTRO_PORTAL_ASTROMETRY_INDEX_DIR`),
and **not a capability key** (the readers of
`GET /v1/capabilities` are strict: the map keeps its fourteen keys). It
travels as `serverFeatures` with `GET /v1/manage/captures[/{id}]`. Where it is true, `POST
/v1/manage/captures/{id}/sky-solution/solve[?imageId=]` (the owner's web
session) runs `solve-field` on the original and stores the solution under
the rules of `PUT` (`202 { state: "running" }`; `GET` on the same path:
`idle`, `running`, `solved`, `failed` with its `reason`); where it is false,
both answer `404 not_found`. The catalogue's own plate solving
(`/api/plate-solve/…`) is unchanged.

## The owner's equipment

« Mon espace » → « Matériel » on the web. Every signed-in
person creates, edits, orders and archives the space's own equipment;
nothing is deleted (a capture or a plan may name an item), « Archives »
brings an item back. The read takes a web session or a paired device;
every write takes either the web session of the space with its CSRF token,
or (« Mon matériel » on the phone) the
bearer of a paired device, which carries no cookie and so no CSRF token;
either write passes the same soft-downgrade gate as `POST /v1/equipment`
(`403` `entitlement_read_only` in the read-only window of an ended
subscription: the web session's too, the gate is the space's). Either way
the space is the credential's own: a device writes only in the space it is
paired to. Every write is bounded by the space: a
row of « Matériel par défaut » or another space's item answers `404`
`equipment_not_found`, exactly as one that does not exist. A device never
restores an archived item (`403` `web_session_required`): an archived item
is no longer seen in the app, « Archives » lives on the web. The shapes are
[`manage-equipment.schema.json`](manage-equipment.schema.json).

- `GET /v1/manage/equipment` — `{ equipment, defaults,
hideDefaultEquipment }`: the space's items, archived ones included
  (`archivedAt`), by kind then preference order; the active default rows; the
  option.
- `POST /v1/manage/equipment` `{ "equipment": { type, name, … } }` — a new
  item (its `id`, if sent, is not used); `201` `{ "equipment" }`.
- `PATCH /v1/manage/equipment` — `{ "hideDefaultEquipment": true | false }`
  (« Masquer le matériel par défaut »), or `{ "action": "reorder", "type",
"ids" }`: the active items of one kind in their new order (exactly those,
  else `400` `invalid_equipment_order`). Answers the read. Or `{ "action":
"import", "items": [ … ] }` (200 items at most, each the shape of a POST,
  its `id` the phone's own): a phone's list sent to the space — what a
  device kept without a space, at its pairing, and each item the phone
  creates. Each item is the space's item that already carries its `id`, or
  an active item of the space with the same kind, name (trimmed, case
  ignored) and characteristics (specifications, planner fields, binoculars;
  « 72 » reads as 72), or a new item under its `id` when no row uses it (a
  fresh one otherwise); nothing of the space is changed, and sending the
  same list again creates nothing. Answers the read and `imported`, one
  `{ localId, id, created }` per item, in order.
- `PATCH /v1/manage/equipment/{id}` — `{ "equipment": { … } }` edits the
  item, or `{ "action": "restore" }` brings an archived one back. Answers
  `{ "equipment" }`.
- `DELETE /v1/manage/equipment/{id}` — « Archiver ». Answers `{ "equipment" }`
  with its `archivedAt`.

- `GET /v1/manage/default-configuration` — « Ma configuration par défaut »:
  `{ configuration, updatedAt }`, the
  mode and the equipment that fill every new plan of the space, both `null`
  when none is set. `configuration` holds the keys of a plan's `equipment` it
  fixes (`mode`, `telescopeId`, `cameraId`, `eyepieceId`,
  `eyepieceFocalLengthMm`, `modifierId`, `filterIds`, `mountId`); a key
  left out is « à choisir ». The same
  object travels with the reads, additively, as `defaultConfiguration`
  (`GET /v1/manage/equipment`, `GET /v1/equipment`, the web planner's
  `GET /api/equipment`).
- `PUT /v1/manage/default-configuration` `{ "configuration": { … } | null }`
  — replaces it (its other keys dropped, each value bounded:
  `normalizeDefaultConfiguration` of `packages/portal/src/planner/`
  `default-configuration.js`), or removes it with `null`; `400`
  `invalid_request` for anything else. Answers `{ configuration, updatedAt }`.
  Only « Enregistrer ma configuration » and « Enregistrer comme configuration
  par défaut » write it: a plan's own equipment never does. Archiving an item
  the configuration names leaves that place out (« à choisir »).

Each write publishes a change of the domain `equipment`, so a paired device
reads its inventory again. The administration keeps the default rows with
the same gestures under `/api/admin/default-equipment` (an administration
route, outside v1).

## The private gallery

« Mon espace » → « Galerie privée », and the pages a
viewer of its address opens. Every space has one private gallery, at a
random address (`organizations.portal_token`: 18 random bytes, 24 base64url
characters, never derived from anything) minted at its first read and
replaced only on request. At its creation it is shared with nobody
(`portal_mode` `none`). The shapes are
[`private-gallery.schema.json`](private-gallery.schema.json).

- `GET /v1/manage/portal` — the gallery of the web session's space (a
  device bearer answers `403` `web_session_required`): `address` (absolute,
  `…/p/{portalToken}`), `mode` as stored, `publicName` (60 characters at
  most, the signature of the public captures) and
  `portalName` (80 at most, the gallery's title), and `modes`: `none` and
  `link`, always `available`. A private server has one account, its owner's,
  and these two modes; a service that opens spaces may add one of its own
  (the hosted service's is in `v1-hosted.md`, « The people invited »).
- `PATCH /v1/manage/portal` — exactly one of `{ "mode" }`, the names
  `{ "publicName", "portalName" }` (each optional; trimmed, never otherwise
  changed; empty or `null` = none) or `{ "regenerateToken": true }` (the
  former address answers `404` at once); the session's CSRF token; the body
  is read under the common ceiling of 1 MiB (`413` `payload_too_large`
  beyond it, `400` `invalid_request` when it is not JSON). **The
  rule is the server's**: `{ "mode": "link" }` needs `"confirmVerifiers":
true` (the dialog that says the captures become visible to the
  verifiers), else `409` `confirmation_required`; a mode the service does
  not offer is `400` `invalid_request`, whatever the page drew; on a service with a team that keeps a dictionary of
  forbidden words, a name that holds one of them (both normalised: NFKC, lower
  case, no accent, no space or punctuation) is `422` `name_forbidden` with its
  `field` and the dictionary's `word`, and nothing is saved (a private server
  keeps none); a name too long is `400`
  `invalid_request` with its `field`. Saving a new name clears the
  refusal of that name. Answers the settings.
- `GET /v1/portals/{portalToken}` — the gallery as its viewer reads it:
  its name, the owner's public name, and every uploaded capture of the
  space, private and public mixed, newest first — **never one refused
  on review** — each with its targets (as the public gallery's cards read them), file
  name, night and its month, the telescope of that night, date added,
  whether it has a thumbnail, and its tags for the gallery's filter bar: `tags`, the generic ones derived
  from the first target's object type as in the public gallery, and
  `ownTags`, the owner's own. Never the place.
- `GET /v1/portals/{portalToken}/media/{captureId}` — the file of one
  capture the gallery shows: `?variant=thumbnail` its thumbnail; otherwise
  its shared copy (JPEG without EXIF, XMP or IPTC, 4096 px at most, written
  at the first need), or its thumbnail when the file cannot have one (a
  FITS). **Never the original.** The type from the bytes,
  `USER_FILE_SECURITY_HEADERS`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, a TIFF
  refused from outside the local network; `404` `gallery_not_found` for
  anything the gate refuses, a capture the gallery does not show, or a
  capture with no such file yet.

**The gate**, one function for the pages, the JSON and the media
(`resolveGalleryAccess` of `portal/private-gallery/private-gallery.js`):
the owner, signed in, in every mode; in `link`, anyone. `none`, an unknown
or a replaced address: `404` `gallery_not_found`, one answer whichever it is.
A service that adds a mode of its own gives that mode its gate and its
refusals (`v1-hosted.md`, « The people invited »).

**Never indexed, never linked.** Every answer of these routes and of the
pages `/{locale}/p/{portalToken}[/{captureId}]` carries `X-Robots-Tag:
noindex, nofollow` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` (the address is the
secret); the pages also say `noindex, nofollow` in their metadata. No
sitemap lists them and no public page links to them.

**The administrator's look.** An administrator of the service who is not
the owner and opens a private capture of a gallery in `link` mode (its
page or its media) leaves one row in `admin_access_log` (reason
`gallery_link`), at most one per administrator, capture and day. No route
reads it.

## The public gallery

The gallery of the home page, fed by the public captures
of every space. Three routes, open to anyone (no session, no device), the
same in both compositions, free on every server (no capability is read). The
shapes are [`gallery.schema.json`](gallery.schema.json): nothing that schema
does not name leaves — never the place, its name nor its coordinates, the
day of the night (`nightMonth`, `YYYY-MM`, only), an e-mail address, a space
or a person, the original file name, a device. The signature is the space's
« Nom public » (`signature`), `null` when it has none.

A capture is public when its `visibility` is `public`, it was not removed
from the gallery after reports, it was not refused on review, its upload is
complete and its space is not closed. Any other identifier — private,
unknown, removed, refused, of a closed space, unfinished — answers the same
`404` `capture_not_found`, with the same body.

- `GET /v1/gallery?locale=&query=&tag=&offset=&limit=&order=` — a page of
  the public captures, **newest night first** (the session's start,
  otherwise the upload's date; `order=oldest` reverses it), `limit` 24 by
  default and 48 at most, `offset` from `0`. `tag` keeps one generic tag
  (« Galaxie », « Nébuleuse », « Amas », « Lune », « Planète », « Soleil »,
  « Étoile », « Comète », « Grand champ »: derived from the object type of the first target, never
  a free word, and never one of the owner's own tags of `capture_tags`,
  which only the private gallery shows); `query` searches the designations,
  names and aliases of the targets. Answers
  `{ captures, total, publicTotal, nextOffset, tags }`;
  `Cache-Control: no-store`.
- `GET /v1/gallery/{captureId}?locale=` — one public capture: the card plus
  the equipment of the night by kind, the sky quality (a Bortle class and
  its source, never the place) and the pixel size of the published file.
- `GET /v1/gallery/{captureId}/media` — the published file: the shared copy
  (JPEG, without EXIF, XMP or IPTC, so without position, 4096 px
  at most), **never the original bytes**; `?variant=thumbnail` its
  thumbnail (WebP), or the shared copy while it has none. `nosniff`, the
  sandboxing policy, `Cache-Control: no-cache` (a capture made private
  again stops being served at once).

## Open session package import

The open,
versioned session package — `packages/astro-contracts/schemas/
session-package/`: a `manifest.json`, a `checksums.sha256`, and
the assets they name — is only ever produced outside this route (Astro
Sync's CLI or GUI, or a personal script written by hand
against the public contract). `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/imports/preview` and
`.../imports/confirm` are how the portal reads one back in, on the shape
[`session-package-import.schema.json`](session-package-import.schema.json).

**Transport: no ZIP reader, on purpose.** Both routes take one JSON body:
`manifestJson` and `checksums`, the _exact raw text_ of the package's own
`manifest.json`/`checksums.sha256` files (never a re-serialized object — the
manifest fingerprint below must be reproducible from the same bytes a
producer actually wrote), plus an optional `documents` map. Image and
progression assets are stored media; this request never carries their
bytes. They **must already be uploaded** ahead of the import through the
existing `POST /v1/uploads` — a plain, already-hashed,
already quota/entitlement-gated content-addressed write — and are resolved
here by the `sha256` `checksums.sha256` names for them, exactly the way
`POST /v1/uploads`'s own idempotency already resolves bytes by digest. A
metric-log/event-log/other asset is not stored media and is never a
capture; its bytes travel inline, base64-encoded, in `documents[path]`, and
are hashed directly against `checksums.sha256` by this route itself. This
is deliberately the simplest transport that stays testable without a new
dependency or a hand-rolled ZIP/deflate reader.

**Hard rules, enforced identically by both routes** (the session-package
contract's own README, "Hard rules for importers", restated here as this is
the concrete importer that document has in mind):

1. **Relative paths only.** Every asset path is checked twice, independently:
   the same pattern `manifest.schema.json`'s `relativePath` def uses (no
   leading slash, no drive letter, no `.`/`..` segment), and a second,
   separate resolution against a virtual package root that must not escape
   it. Either failing alone is `400` `invalid_path`.
2. **SHA-256 verification is mandatory**, at both transports: an
   already-uploaded image/progression asset's digest was verified once,
   already, by `POST /v1/uploads/{id}/finalize`'s own hash check (or the
   legacy single-request form's); an inline document asset is verified here,
   directly, against `checksums.sha256`. Either kind of mismatch, a
   `checksums.sha256` line missing for a listed asset, or one naming a path
   the manifest never lists, is `422` `checksum_mismatch`. An image/
   progression asset whose `sha256` names no capture yet in this tenant is
   `409` `asset_not_uploaded` — upload it first, then retry the same
   preview/confirm call.
3. **Idempotency is keyed on the manifest, at the tenant level.** The
   fingerprint is the SHA-256 of `manifestJson`'s exact bytes. `preview`
   reports `alreadyImported` (with a `previousImport` summary) without ever
   writing; `confirm` is idempotent on `(tenant, manifestSha256)` — a second
   confirm of the same bytes, even one naming a _different_ session than the
   first, replays the first result and writes nothing new. This is the
   literal reading of "re-importing the same package changes nothing": once
   a manifest fingerprint is confirmed for a tenant, it is confirmed, full
   stop.
4. **The caller's authenticated session is the only session an import can
   ever affect.** `manifest.json` carries no session/tenant id (the schema
   has none); `session.label`/`.startedAt`/`.finishedAt`, when present, are
   display metadata only and are never read as an attachment point.
5. **`preview` persists nothing.** Every check `confirm` performs, `preview`
   performs identically and in the same order, and then simply does not
   write. A package that previews clean confirms the same way, barring a
   race with another confirm of the same manifest in between.

**Captures are linked, never recopied** (`confirm` only): every image/
progression asset is passed through the exact same
`insertCaptureWithTargets` path a manual upload uses — content-addressed
dedup by `sha256` means an asset already a capture is associated with this
session, not duplicated. A capture's session association is fixed at its
own first creation everywhere in this contract (see "Uploads and captures"
above); if the referenced capture already belongs to a _different_ session,
or to none at all, `confirm` still succeeds but records a `warning`
(`capture_attached_elsewhere`/`capture_session_unset`) rather than
force-moving it — the same non-negotiable invariant `POST /v1/uploads`
already documents ("a replay does not re-associate that capture with
another session"). Metric-log/event-log/other assets are never captures:
they are recorded as small documents attached directly to the session,
outside the capture/quota system entirely.

**Capability.** Only the soft-downgrade write gate applies
(`entitlement_read_only`, `403`) — `confirm` writes no new
stored-media bytes of its own (every capture-like asset's bytes were
already accounted for at its own upload), so `storage.quota_bytes`/
`storage.max_file_bytes` are not re-checked here.

## The structured night

A night is the observation session, now with fields of its own, its
acquisition steps and the summaries of its analysis, all editable by its
owner from the web and the phone, never as raw JSON. Three producers write
the same objects: Astro Sync (the report of [Session analyses](#session-analyses)),
the import of the old nights (the same report, `producer: "import"`) and
the person's hand. Reading is never gated by a capability.

**The night's fields.** `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` returns `night`
([`session.schema.json`](session.schema.json) `$defs.night`): `title`,
`startedAt`, `finishedAt`, `place`, `equipment`, `targetIds`, `notes`, each
`{ value, source }` — `source: "night"` when the night holds it, `"plan"`
when it is read through the night's plan because nobody set it (the plan's
name, its place's name or locality and coordinates, its equipment
selection's `telescopeId`/`cameraId`/`mountId`/`filterIds`, its items'
targets). The place **never carries a time zone** (the place rule): a
reader shows instants in the viewer's own time. A night without a plan
(`POST /v1/sessions` with `clientSessionId`, idempotent) holds its own
fields only.

`PATCH /v1/sessions/{id}/night` (`$defs.nightPatchRequest`), by the owner's
web session (with its CSRF token) or a device token of the space, writes
the fields named; `null` gives a field back to the plan. A place with a
`timeZone` is `400`; a target that is neither in the catalogue nor one of
the space's, or an equipment identifier that is neither the space's nor
the platform's default, is `422` `target_not_found`/`equipment_not_found`;
`finishedAt` on a night still open is `409` `night_not_finished`. The
answer is `{ session, night }`. Every write moves the session's
`updatedAt` and signals the `sessions` domain.

**Steps.** `steps[]` (`$defs.step`): target, filter (free text, never
normalized; null reads « Sans filtre »), frames, exposure, gain, and
`integrationSeconds` computed (frames × exposure, never entered);
`origin` `analysis` · `import` · `manual`; `edited` for a step corrected
by hand. `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/steps` adds a `manual` step;
`PATCH /v1/sessions/{id}/steps/{stepId}` corrects one and marks it
`edited`; `DELETE` removes it (`204`). Same credentials as the night.

**Summaries, computed at read time, never stored.** `qualitySummary`
(`$defs.qualitySummary`): medians of the measured frames for the night and
by step — FWHM in pixels, and in arcseconds when the report gives
`pixelScaleArcsec` (« 2,6 px · 8,1″ »), eccentricity, sky background, its
noise and the stars detected when the producer gave them, the frames
measured. `guidingSummary` (`$defs.guidingSummary`): RMS in right
ascension, declination and total over the night, guided duration,
samples, dithers, expired settles, focus runs launched and succeeded,
meridian flips. `guidingSeries`: slices of about 2 minutes, the report's
own or computed from its samples. Null (an empty series) without an
analysis.

**What is online for a night.** `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/online`
([`session-online.schema.json`](session-online.schema.json)), by a device
token of the space or the owner's web session, is what Astro Sync's resume
and its « Nuits » list read before sending anything: per target of
the night (its own or its plan's first, then those only a capture names,
then the captures without a target), each capture with its source
(`phone` · `web` · `astro-sync` · `stacking`), stage, visibility, its main
image and every image (the main one, then the variants) with its
SHA-256, size, name, date and `skySolution: present | missing`
(the solution belongs to each image); and the night's analysis in
place (sent when, by which device, its report version and producer, the
steps it proposed and how many were corrected). Another space's night is
`404` `session_not_found`; no credential, `401`. It does not count as a
plan synchronization (`lastSyncAt` untouched).

## Session analyses

**One analysis per night**, the next sending replacing it; the versions of
3 are gone, with their selector. The report is the **report v3** or **v2**
([`session-analysis.schema.json`](session-analysis.schema.json),
`$defs.report`, `reportVersion: 3` or `2`; the v1 is refused): Astro Sync builds
it on the person's computer from the night's logs (PHD2, ASIAIR, NINA) and
the headers of its frames, the import of the old nights from their
measures (`producer: "import"`). It carries `acquisitionSteps[]` (in place
of the v1's `acquisitionFamilies`), each quality row's `stepKey`, the
optional `skyBackgroundNoiseAdu` and `starCount` (the old portal's two
extra measures, carried), `pixelScaleArcsec`, `guidingSeries[]`, and
`guiding[]` made optional (the import has no samples).
`additionalProperties: false` at every object level keeps the evidence
rule at the wire boundary: a stray `score`/`rating`/`grade` field, or any
field this schema never named, is rejected outright, from any producer.

### Report v3 (w61)

Astro Sync sends `reportVersion: 3`; the reports v2 already stored are
served as they were sent. The v3:

- **no longer carries the raw samples** (`guiding[]`, 1.8 to 3.8 MB a
  night) but `guidingTrace`: at most **one point per 10 s**
  (`intervalSeconds` ≥ 10, `t` seconds from `startUtc`), each with the mean
  and the peak of the offsets in right ascension and declination
  (arcseconds), the **corrections** sent over the window with their
  direction (`raPulseMs` east positive, `decPulseMs` north positive, null
  when the log did not write the direction), the samples `n` and those left
  out of the statistics `out` (a window with `out == n` is a settle, never a
  spike); the guide camera's scale `scaleArcsecPerPx` (pixels = arcseconds ÷
  scale); the night's `rms` over the samples that count; `markers` (dither,
  settle, star-lost, pause, meridian-flip, autofocus, with their end). A night
  of six hours is about 0.4 MB;
- counts **one dither per dither** in `timeline.events` (PHD2 and the ASIAIR
  both log it; PHD2's is kept);
- says the **clocks** of its logs (`logClock`: `+HH:MM` per kind, and how the
  PHD2 one was known — `given`, `asiair`, `frames`);
- gives each quality row `rejected`/`rejectReason` (the stacking left it out:
  `unregistered`, or the reason « Mesure des poses » gave: `dawn`, `sky`,
  `clouds`, `trailed`, `wide`, `manual`) and the frame measure's FWHM,
  eccentricity, sky and `starCount` when the header has none.

The service checks the trace (points in order, at most one per interval,
`out ≤ n`: else `400 invalid_request`) and refuses a v3 above 2 MB
(`413 analysis_too_large`); the route's own limit (16 MB) stays for the v2.
`GET /v1/sessions/{id}` adds, computed at read time: `guidingTrace` (the
v3's, or rebuilt from a v2's raw samples with null corrections),
`posesSummary` (frames kept and left out, by reason and by filter) and
`zone` (`{ timeZone, utcOffsetMinutes }`: the place's zone and its offset at
the night's start — every instant stays UTC and is written in the place's
hours); `guidingSummary` counts dithers once (also for a stored v2) and adds
`starLost` and `guideScaleArcsecPerPx`. `GET /v1/projects/{projectId}/analyses`
gives the nights of a multi-night plan night by night and together
(`project.schema.json` `nightsStatistics`: RMS weighted by each night's
guided time).

### Sending

`POST /v1/sessions/{id}/analyses` — body: the report on its own. In one
transaction it replaces the night's analysis and the steps the previous
analysis or import proposed that nobody corrected; a step corrected by
hand stays (« modifié »), and a report's step under the same key is not
added again (the correction wins, the step is never doubled); a manual
step stays. Each report step's `targetHint` (the `OBJECT` header) is
resolved to a target of the space when its designation matches (spaces
and case ignored; the space's own target first). `201`:

```json
{
  "analysis": {
    "id": "…",
    "sessionId": "…",
    "reportVersion": 2,
    "createdAt": "2026-10-08T09:12:00Z",
    "originDeviceId": "…",
    "deviceName": "Astro Sync",
    "report": { "reportVersion": 2, "…": "…" }
  },
  "replaced": true,
  "steps": { "proposed": 3, "keptEdited": 1 }
}
```

A report holding nothing — no step, no quality row, no guiding sample,
trace point nor slice, no event — is `422` `analysis_empty`: a night's measures are never
wiped by an empty sending. Two steps under one key are `400`.

### Reading and deleting

`GET /v1/sessions/{id}/analyses` returns the night's analysis
(`$defs.resource`), `404` `analysis_not_found` without one.
`DELETE /v1/sessions/{id}/analyses` (« Supprimer cette analyse », the
owner's web session or a device token of the space) removes it, `204`; the
steps stay (the frames were taken: only the measures go). An unknown or
another space's night is `404` `session_not_found`.

### Capability

`analysis.logs` gates **creating** an analysis only (the open core grants
it):
`POST` refuses with `403` `analysis_not_entitled` without it and
honours the expired-subscription lock (`403` `entitlement_read_only`).
Reading — this route, `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` and its summaries, the
guiding losses, `…/online` — is **never** gated (a free account whose
night was imported reads its graphs). `DELETE` is not gated either.
Authentication is `eitherV1Access` (either credential), like every route
of [Observation sessions and debriefing](#observation-sessions-and-debriefing).

## Entitlements

`GET /v1/entitlements` is the application's read of what its space holds:
`{ "purchases": [...], "capabilities": {...} }`. `capabilities` is the
complete map of `GET /v1/capabilities` ([Capability
enforcement](#capability-enforcement)). `purchases` lists what the space has
bought: **a private server sells nothing, so it is always `[]`**.

```json
{
  "purchases": [],
  "capabilities": { "...": "the private server's profile" }
}
```

A client reads any other field of the answer as additive and ignores what it
does not know. The route answers on every server, so that a client which gets
`404` or `401` can take it as a refusal. It never touches `lastSyncAt`.

Shape: [`entitlements-private-server.schema.json`](entitlements-private-server.schema.json).

## Clocks, ordering and pagination

`recordedAt` is the **device clock and informational**. It is what the person's
phone believed at the moment of the note, and it is stored as sent: no
range-check, no clamping, no rejection for being implausible. Losing a
field observation because a phone's clock was wrong would be a worse failure
than an odd timestamp, and a batch response returns `clockSkewMs` (`receivedAt`
minus the newest submitted `recordedAt`) so a device can correct its own
display.

`receivedAt` is the **server clock and authoritative**. It orders a session's
journal, it is what `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/events?since=` compares against, and
the server never emits one earlier than a `receivedAt` already stored for that
session. Neither clock is ever used for identity, idempotency or authorization.

On an event the server generated, `recordedAt` is not a clock reading at all:
it is the plan's own slot, the window the person declared, or a night anchor,
and `timing` says which — `observed`, `planned` or `reconstructed`. A whole
generated batch shares one `receivedAt`, so `receivedAt` cannot order it;
within one instant the order is the `eventId`'s, which carries the emission
ordinal for exactly that reason. See
[Deterministic event identifiers](#from-a-plan-to-an-observation-session-the-initial-event-mapping).

All timestamps are ISO 8601 UTC with a `Z` suffix and millisecond precision.
"The night has passed" means exactly `nightEndAt < now` with both read as
instants; the comparison is never done on a calendar date and never in a local
time zone, so a person travelling between time zones sees the same queue on
both surfaces. `nightStartAt` and `nightEndAt` are themselves derived, not
declared — [The night boundaries of a plan](#the-night-boundaries-of-a-plan) —
and are truncated to whole seconds so that a plan's identifiers cannot move
with a library patch.

`since` cursors — on `GET /v1/sessions` (against `updatedAt`),
`GET /v1/sessions/{id}/events` and `GET /v1/captures` (against `receivedAt` and
the later of `createdAt` and the instant the capture came back from the trash
of its session, which is `createdAt` for a capture that never did) — are
**inclusive**, and the response's `nextSince` is the last returned row's own
value (on `GET /v1/captures`, the instant the cursor compared, which is not
`createdAt` for a restored capture). A client may therefore see a row twice
and never misses one, which is the right trade for an append-only journal whose
rows are idempotent by identifier anyway. `limit` defaults to 50 and is capped
at 200; `hasMore` says whether the page was cut. An opaque cursor token would
remove the duplicate row but cannot be resumed from a value the client already
holds, which offline clients do hold.

`GET /v1/captures` has a second reading, for the screens that show the list
(Journal F, additive): `?order=recent` returns the **most recent first** — by
the night of the capture (its plan's date, else its session's day, else the day
it was added), then by the moment it was added — so that a client that stops
at a ceiling of captures keeps the recent ones. It is walked by `?before=`
(the opaque `nextBefore` of the page before, `null` on the last page) and is
exact: no row twice, none missed; `nextSince` is then `null`. `order=recent` is
not asked together with `since` (`400 invalid_request`), and any other value of
`order`, or a `before` that no page gave, is `400 invalid_request`. Without
`order` the route reads as before, oldest first, which is the synchronisation's
reading.

A session's `updatedAt` is the latest of its creation, its finish, and the
`receivedAt` of its newest event or supplement — a high-water mark, so a
`since` walk sees a session again whenever anything was appended to it.

Nothing in these responses exposes a precise observing location: a session
carries `planId`, not coordinates, and the debrief queue carries the plan
payload the client already had. Every record described here is private to its
tenant and derived from authenticated membership; none of it has a public
route.

## Reactive change signal

`GET /v1/changes` is
an accelerator, never a requirement: a device or a web session that never
opens it still works fully on the routes above, catching up on the next
ordinary synchronization. The stream carries **only a signal** — never a plan
id, a device name or any other business data — so there is no second merge
path riding this wire; a client that receives one always falls back to
re-running its existing synchronization, which is what actually reads and
resolves anything.

The response is `text/event-stream`, defined by
[`change-signal.schema.json`](change-signal.schema.json). Two event kinds:

- `change`: `{ "cursor": "<boot>:<n>", "domains": [...] }` — something changed,
  in at least one of the named domains, for this account;
- `resync`: `{ "cursor": "<boot>:<n>" }` — the same shape without `domains`,
  meaning "the client should re-read everything" (a buffer overrun, a missed
  `Last-Event-ID`, or a `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY` reconnect that may have dropped
  notifications).

`domains` is drawn from a closed, ten-member list: `plans`, `sessions`,
`debrief`, `equipment`, `places`, `framings`, `favorites`, `captures`,
`devices`, `projects` (every successful project write or
archive, see [Projects](#projects)). No other field is ever present on either shape — the schema's
`additionalProperties: false` is normative, not incidental.

**`framings` is emitted (dated 24/09/2026).** Every
successful write of a framing resource (`PUT`/`DELETE`/restore) emits
`framings`. A plan write that changes which framings it references emits
`plans` **and** `framings` (a plan's own revision does not change just
because a framing it references was edited elsewhere — only the framing's own
revision moves — but the set of framings a plan points at is itself part of
what `framings` covers). Creating an observation session for a plan that
references at least one framing emits `sessions` **and** `framings` (that
framing may have just become `locked`).

The SSE `id:` line carries the same value as `cursor`; a reconnecting client's
`Last-Event-ID` request header is honored to replay events the connection
missed, or answered with an immediate `resync` when the cursor is unknown (a
different server boot, or past the retained buffer). A `: ping` comment line
(not a JSON payload, and outside `change-signal.schema.json`'s scope) is sent
every 25 seconds to hold the connection through proxies and idle timeouts.

**Client instance, not device or session.** Every write-capable client
instance — the mobile app at launch, each browser tab — generates a random
`clientInstanceId` and sends it as the `X-Astro-Client-Instance` header on
every write it makes, and as the `?client=` query parameter when it opens
`GET /v1/changes`. The channel never echoes a signal back to the instance
whose own write produced it, but does deliver it to every other instance on
the same account — including a second browser tab of the same web session, or
a second paired device. A write with no `X-Astro-Client-Instance` header (an
older client) is treated as belonging to no instance, so its signal reaches
