everyone, including the writer's own other tabs if it has any.

## Additive routes awaiting approval

Three read routes are **not part of the original route list** and are proposed here
because the interfaces cannot be built without them.
They are additive, read-only and tenant-scoped, and they are listed separately
so they can be approved or dropped as a group:

- `GET /v1/captures` — the private "My Captures" list: the person's own
  captures with their targets, date, related plan and session, upload
  status, its visibility and the state of its checks. The screen
  specification names no route to feed it. It is
  deliberately **not** capability-gated: a profile that cannot upload has no
  captures, so gating a read that can only be empty would add a failure mode
  without protecting anything, and the interface hides the section on the
  capability instead;
- `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` — one session with its supplements, the first page of
  its events, and the read-through `plan` projection that carries the night's
  equipment and boundaries
  ([Equipment on a session](#equipment-on-a-session)). Proposed supplements are shown
  on a session page; there is otherwise no way to read
  them, nor to honour "equipment pre-filled" without copying the equipment onto
  the session. It also carries `debrief` (the stored debriefing, null
  before one or for a direct session) and `debriefSummary`, the summary of
  the night computed at request time (`debrief.schema.json`'s `summary`:
  planned, acquired and lost minutes, the lost minutes by cause with their
  spans, the result per target; never a score) and the
  planned `slot` of each target, `{ start, end }` inside the session's window,
  so a reader can draw the night as a strip without the plan, and,
  additively, the `passages` of each target — `{ start, end,
minutes, auto, confirmed, pauses }`, a target may be observed in several —
  its `autoUnconfirmedMinutes` and the night's, the time the clock counted in
  automatic mode that no `auto_time_confirmed` has confirmed yet, with the minutes its passages spent while the application did
  not run, the `note` events carrying `appStopped`, pauses deducted), and
  `launchedSlots`, the plan as it was launched, on the clock; `segments`
  carries the computed strip geometry in epoch minutes, with planned boxes,
  acquired, paused, rejected and abandoned stretches and passage markers;
  null when there is nothing to draw), and
  `guidingLosses`, the
  stretches lost to guiding read from the night's analysis — whenever the
  service holds one, whoever reads (reading
  an analysis is never gated; `analysis.logs` gates its creation), null
  otherwise with `guidingLossesReason` `no_analysis`; and the
  structured night (`night`, `steps`, `qualitySummary`, `guidingSummary`,
  `guidingSeries`, [The structured night](#the-structured-night));
- `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/events` — the event page a mobile client syncs back
  after writing its own, and the paging companion of the route above.

## Sessions and debriefing error codes

New codes, named on the existing pattern (`<subject>_<state>`, the subject
first). Codes already defined earlier — `invalid_request`,
`plan_not_found`, `plan_frozen`, `plan_conflict`,
`device_authentication_required`, `authentication_required`, `csrf_invalid` —
keep their meaning and status unchanged.

| Code                          | Status | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ----------------------------- | ------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `pairing_not_found`           | `404`  | `GET /v1/devices/pairings/{code}` named a code outside this tenant, or never issued.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `session_not_found`           | `404`  | No such session in this tenant.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `event_batch_too_large`       | `400`  | More than 500 events in one batch; nothing was written.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `supplement_not_found`        | `404`  | No such supplement on this session.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `supplement_already_accepted` | `409`  | Its events are appended and cannot be withdrawn.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `plan_not_deletable`          | `403`  | `DELETE /v1/plans/{planId}` of an `executed`, `partial` or `archived` plan that a live session still names (`sessionIds`): a refusal; it goes to the trash once those sessions are there.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `plan_night_not_begun`        | `409`  | `closedAs` `executed`, `partial` or `abandoned` on a plan whose night begins more than 24 hours from now; nothing was written.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `plan_night_place_conflict`   | `409`  | A write that would give a night (a date) two places: the plans still to plan of a date share the place; `planIds`, `date` and `nightPlace` say what the night holds; nothing was written.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `plan_not_debriefable`        | `409`  | `executed`/`partial` while `nightEndAt` is known and still in the future and the execution session is not finished, or `abandoned` on a plan whose session already holds a field event. `error.reason` (additive) says which: `night_in_future` or `session_has_events`; only the second advises a `partial` debriefing.                                                                                                            |
| `set_aside_version_required`  | `400`  | Two versions exist and the decision did not say which describes the night.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `set_aside_version_not_found` | `404`  | `setAsideVersionId` names no set-aside version of this plan.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `invalid_plan_field`          | `400`  | A merge's `fields` names no plan setting, or chooses neither `local` nor `other`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `plan_field_choice_required`  | `400`  | A merge left a setting both versions changed without a choice; `fields` lists them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `unknown_target_adjustment`   | `400`  | An adjustment names a target absent from the chosen base payload.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `checksum_mismatch`           | `400`  | The received bytes do not hash to the declared `sha256`, or their size differs.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `capture_limit_reached`       | `409`  | `capture.count_limit` would be exceeded by this upload (checked before the quota); `storage` repeats `captureCount` and `captureCountLimit`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `capture_public_unavailable`  | `403`  | The profile lacks `capture.public`: a capture of this space cannot be made public.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `storage_quota_reached`       | `409`  | `storage.quota_bytes` is `0` or would be exceeded.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `file_too_large`              | `413`  | `storage.max_file_bytes` is `0` or is exceeded by this file.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `framing_not_found`           | `404`  | No such framing in this tenant.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `capability_required`         | `403`  | The tenant capability profile lacks `planner.mosaic` for a mosaic framing write or restore.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `framing_conflict`            | `409`  | `If-Match` names a revision that is no longer current; the server's `framing` is returned and the client's version becomes a copy.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `framing_locked`              | `409`  | The framing is referenced by an executed plan and the write changes more than `name`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `framing_archived`            | `409`  | The framing is archived; only `POST .../restore` is accepted.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `framing_in_use`              | `409`  | `DELETE` on a `shared` framing; `pendingPlans` names the plans still referencing it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `framing_not_archived`        | `409`  | `POST .../restore` on a framing that is not archived.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `framing_missing`             | `409`  | A plan write references a `framingId` unknown to this tenant; the plan is kept in the client's pending queue for the next synchronization.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

Per-event rejection reasons inside a `200` batch response are a separate,
closed vocabulary and are never HTTP statuses: `unknown_event_type`,
`invalid_event_payload`, `invalid_event_id`, `invalid_recorded_at`,
`duplicate_event_id_in_batch`.

## Journal error codes

New codes of the Journal, the rework of a session and the captures' link to
their session. `invalid_request`, `session_not_found`, `night_not_finished`,
`target_not_found`, `precondition_required`, `invalid_if_match` and
`entitlement_read_only` keep their meaning and status.

| Code                               | Status | Meaning                                                                                                                                       |
| ---------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `session_rework_conflict`          | `409`  | `If-Match` names a rework revision that is no longer current; the server's `session` is returned.                                             |
| `event_not_found`                  | `404`  | A rework names an event the session does not hold (`eventId` is returned).                                                                    |
| `event_not_removable`              | `409`  | A rework removes an event it did not add: an observed event is never removed.                                                                 |
| `event_already_replaced`           | `409`  | A rework touches an event a more recent revision already replaced (`eventId`, and `replacedBy`: the event that shows).                        |
| `event_not_replaceable`            | `409`  | A rework replaces an event that is not a note, a condition, an equipment issue, a pause or a kind Astro Sync detects (`eventId` is returned). |
| `capture_without_session`          | `409`  | `acquisition/sync` on a capture with neither a session nor a plan.                                                                            |
| `integration_night_not_in_project` | `409`  | An integration names a night that does not belong to the multi-night plan (`nightId` is returned).                                            |
| `capture_conflict`                 | `409`  | An owner write names a field changed after its `If-Match` revision (`capture` returned).                                                      |
| `session_read_only`                | `409`  | A write to a catalogue night of a private server (an opaque `catalogue:` id): it is read only.                                                |
| `session_not_finished`             | `409`  | `DELETE /v1/sessions/{id}` of a night in progress: finish it first.                                                                           |
| `session_deleted`                  | `409`  | `POST /v1/sessions` of an execution whose session was deleted (`sessionId`): restore it.                                                      |
| `session_plan_taken`               | `409`  | `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/restore` while the plan has another live session (`sessionId`).                                                       |
| `session_not_deleted`              | `409`  | `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/erase` of a session that is not deleted: delete it first.                                                             |
| `plan_not_frozen`                  | `409`  | `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/archive` of an active plan: write it with `closedAs` `archived`.                                                     |
| `plan_not_debriefed`               | `409`  | `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/session` for a plan no debriefing froze: nothing to make again.                                                      |
| `plan_archived`                    | `409`  | `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/session` for an archived plan: give it back first.                                                                   |
| `session_exists`                   | `409`  | `POST /v1/plans/{planId}/session` while the plan has a live session (`sessionId`).                                                            |

## Read-only refusals

A service may put a space in a read-only window: it is read and exported, not
written. A write the window refuses answers `403`
`entitlement_read_only`, with nothing in the refusal that names an offer or a
price. A private server never opens such a window.

| Code                    | Status | Meaning                                                                        |
| ----------------------- | ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `entitlement_read_only` | `403`  | The space is in its read-only window; this write is refused. Reads still work. |

**The write gate.** While a space is in that window, `POST /v1/uploads`,
`PUT`/`POST .../restore` on `/v1/plans/{id}`, and `PUT` on `/v1/framings/{id}`
all refuse with `403` `entitlement_read_only` before touching storage. Reads,
exports, and every deletion (`DELETE /v1/plans/{id}`, `DELETE`/`POST
.../restore` on `/v1/framings/{id}`, and setting a favorite to `false`) stay
allowed: nothing is ever deleted by this window, and the person is never
stopped from reading their own data or cleaning it up. Adding a favorite
(`PUT /v1/favorites/{targetId}` with `favorite: true`, or the import of `POST
/v1/favorites`) is refused the same way; removing one is not. Session, event and
debrief writes are never gated at all, by design, matching "Sessions, events
and the debriefing are never gated" above. `writeAccessFor` on
`packages/portal/src/capabilities/provider.ts`'s `EntitlementProvider` is the
provider-side hook this gate reads; a `staticProfile` (every self-hosted
"owner"/open-source deployment) never implements it and is therefore never
read-only.

## Space error codes

New codes of the space sign-in. `invalid_request` and
`authentication_required` keep their earlier meaning. The codes of the
sign-in are detailed in [Refusals](#refusals), those of the devices in
[Space devices](#space-devices), those of the deletion in
[Deleting a space](#deleting-a-space).

| Code                        | Status | Meaning                                                                                    |
| --------------------------- | ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `not_found`                 | `404`  | The route does not exist on this kind of service.                                          |
| `not_owner`                 | `403`  | A private server: the address is not its owner's; nothing is sent.                         |
| `not_initialized`           | `409`  | A private server not installed yet; `bootstrapPath`.                                       |
| `email_invalid`             | `400`  | The address has no normalised form.                                                        |
| `sign_in_code_incorrect`    | `400`  | Wrong code, or a link of another address; `attemptsRemaining`.                             |
| `sign_in_code_expired`      | `400`  | No valid code for this address.                                                            |
| `sign_in_link_invalid`      | `400`  | The link was used, replaced, or never issued.                                              |
| `sign_in_locked`            | `429`  | Too many failures: codes wait 15 min, 1 h, then 24 h; `retryAt`.                           |
| `sign_in_rate_limited`      | `429`  | Too many or too frequent requests; `retryAt`.                                              |
| `email_delivery_failed`     | `502`  | The message could not be sent; nothing counted.                                            |
| `sign_in_unavailable`       | `503`  | Settings incomplete, or e-mail budget spent (`retryAt`).                                   |
| `space_unavailable`         | `403`  | The address belongs to a person who can no longer sign in, or to a space being erased.     |
| `service_starting`          | `503`  | The service cannot reach its database yet; `retryAt`.                                      |
| `device_attached_elsewhere` | `409`  | The phone belongs to another space; `attachment`.                                          |
| `device_change_limited`     | `409`  | The device left a space less than 24 hours ago; `retryAt`.                                 |
| `device_detached`           | `401`  | The device was detached; it erases its copy.                                               |
| `space_deleted`             | `401`  | The space was deleted; the application erases its copy. Answered before `device_detached`. |

## Isolation between spaces

With open sign-up, any person holds a session or a device token of their own
space. These rules keep one space from reaching another's rows, from learning
whether another space's identifier exists, and from taking more than its
share of the service. They add codes and never change an existing answer to
an ordinary request; a private server (one space) accepts and shows what it
did before.

**Files a person sent are never served as active content.** The type a
person's file is served with comes from its first bytes, never from its name:
only a raster image recognised by its signature (JPEG, PNG, WebP, AVIF, TIFF)
is served as `image/*` (`Cache-Control: private, max-age=3600`); anything
else — SVG, HTML, XML, text, FITS, XISF, a camera's raw file, an unknown
format — is `application/octet-stream` with `Content-Disposition:
attachment` (`Cache-Control: private, no-store`). Every such answer carries
`X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Content-Security-Policy: default-src
'none'; sandbox`. This applies to every route that serves a person's file;
every other answer of the service carries `nosniff` too. An SVG or HTML
document is refused as a capture by its bytes, whatever its name, with `415`
`capture_content_refused`; every other file is accepted as before.

**Identifiers of another space.** A reference to another space's row (a
capture's or an upload's `sessionId`, a plan, a framing, a piece of
equipment) answers exactly as an identifier that exists nowhere: `404`
(`session_not_found`, `plan_not_found`…), never a database error. Identifiers
are global: creating a plan or a framing (`If-Match: "0"`) under an
identifier another space already uses answers `409`
`invalid_creation_revision`, the answer an identifier already used in the
caller's own space gets, and nothing about the other row. A plan or a
multi-night plan **deleted** in the caller's own space at that identifier (in
the trash) does not block the creation: it is erased — the path of « Supprimer
définitivement », audited `plan.erased` or `project.erased` with
`replacedByCreation` — and the creation proceeds, at revision `1`. This is what
a second sending of the same content after a deletion means (Astro Sync
derives its identifiers from what it sends); an erased row blocks nothing.

**Technical ceilings per space.** Settable by the operator, far above any real
use, and distinct from capabilities:

| Ceiling                                       | Setting                                          | Default | Refusal                                                                             |
| --------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | ------: | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Observation sessions of a space               | `ASTRO_PORTAL_MAX_SESSIONS_PER_SPACE`            |  10 000 | `409` `session_limit_reached` (an idempotent replay is never refused)               |
| Events of a session                           | `ASTRO_PORTAL_MAX_EVENTS_PER_SESSION`            |  10 000 | `409` `session_event_limit_reached`, the batch whole, nothing written               |
| Proposed supplements of a session             | `ASTRO_PORTAL_MAX_SUPPLEMENTS_PER_SESSION`       |     200 | none: past it the frozen-plan refusal stays the bare `409` `plan_frozen`            |
| Active (not revoked) devices of a space       | `ASTRO_PORTAL_MAX_DEVICES_PER_SPACE`             |      50 | `409` `device_limit_reached`                                                        |
| Unfinished resumable uploads of a space       | `ASTRO_PORTAL_UPLOAD_MAX_OPEN_SESSIONS`          |      32 | `409` `upload_sessions_limit_reached` (resuming an open one is never refused)       |
| Change streams of a space (`GET /v1/changes`) | `ASTRO_PORTAL_CHANGES_MAX_CONNECTIONS_PER_SPACE` |      16 | none: the space's oldest stream is closed, like the per-device and per-session caps |

The device ceiling answers `POST /v1/devices/claim` (private server) and
`POST /v1/sign-in/verify` with a device (where spaces are open). It counts every
application (PlanAstro, Astro Sync, a paired phone) and is not the
rule of one space per phone ([Space devices](#space-devices)): an
installation the space already knows — signing in again, or coming back
after being revoked — is never refused by it, and Astro Sync stays outside
the one-space-per-phone rule as before.

**Quota and unfinished uploads.** The bytes a space's unfinished resumable
uploads already hold count in its `storage.quota_bytes` like stored captures,
at creation, at every `PUT` (`409` `storage_quota_reached` before a byte of
the chunk is written) and at finalize; `storage.usedBytes` in these refusals
includes them. Abandoned uploads are purged when the service starts, then
along the upload requests at most once an hour.

**Request bodies.** Every route reads a JSON (or text) body up to a ceiling,
whatever `Content-Length` says: 1 MiB by default; 2 MiB for `PUT
/v1/plans/{id}`, 16 MiB for `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/analyses`, 32 MiB for the
session-package import routes (their `documents` travel inline). Past it:
`413` `payload_too_large`, nothing read further, nothing written.

**Pairing attempts.** `POST /v1/devices/claim` answers `429`
`pairing_rate_limited` (`Retry-After`) to a network origin that failed ten
claims within ten minutes, the right code included; a successful claim is
never counted.

| Code                            | Status | Meaning                                                                     |
| ------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `capture_content_refused`       | `415`  | The file's bytes are an SVG or HTML document.                               |
| `upload_sessions_limit_reached` | `409`  | The space already keeps its ceiling of unfinished uploads open.             |
| `session_limit_reached`         | `409`  | The space holds its ceiling of observation sessions.                        |
| `session_event_limit_reached`   | `409`  | The batch would carry the session past its ceiling of events.               |
| `device_limit_reached`          | `409`  | The space has its ceiling of active devices; revoking one makes room again. |
| `pairing_rate_limited`          | `429`  | Too many failed pairing claims from this network origin; `Retry-After`.     |
| `payload_too_large`             | `413`  | The request body passes the route's ceiling.                                |

## Capability enforcement

Creation is limited by the current tenant capability profile through
`sync.plan_limit`. The provider is composed server-side; no API conditional
contains a commercial offer name.

`GET /v1/capabilities` returns the complete composed map directly, using
capability names and primitive boolean/integer values only. It exposes no
product name, tenant, user, device, or bearer field. Capability discovery
updates device `lastSeenAt`, not `lastSyncAt`.

**`pointing.solve` (additive).** A boolean: the mobile application's aiming
screen — full-screen plate solving with its direction arrow and reticle, and
the lens screen — and what later builds on it (the axis calibration, the polar
alignment by plate solving, the satellite phone). A service grants it
wherever it grants `planner.mosaic`; the open-source profile sends `false`, like `planner.mosaic`.
It never holds back a pointing-safety check (horizon mask, zenith exclusion,
the Sun by day, STOP): those are free and outside every capability. No route
checks it today; the map carries it so that every client reads one complete
map.

**Unlimited (contract 0.2.0).** A numeric capability that sets no cap carries
`9007199254740991` (`Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`, named `CAPABILITY_UNLIMITED` in
`packages/astro-contracts`): a plain integer, so every reader keeps comparing
integers and a cap that is never reached needs no special case. `0` keeps its
meaning — no allowance — except for `sync.plan_limit`, where it has always
meant no numeric cap. A self-hosted server that serves the open-source profile
sends this value for every numeric capability.

The normative machine-readable shapes are
[`plan.schema.json`](plan.schema.json),
[`framing.schema.json`](framing.schema.json),
[`project.schema.json`](project.schema.json),
[`briefing.schema.json`](briefing.schema.json),
[`device-pairing.schema.json`](device-pairing.schema.json),
[`capabilities.schema.json`](capabilities.schema.json),
[`target-library-query.schema.json`](target-library-query.schema.json),
[`target-index.schema.json`](target-index.schema.json),
[`offline-pack.schema.json`](offline-pack.schema.json),
[`target-favorites.schema.json`](target-favorites.schema.json),
[`equipment.schema.json`](equipment.schema.json),
[`weather.schema.json`](weather.schema.json),
[`weather-history.schema.json`](weather-history.schema.json),
[`place-climate-trend.schema.json`](place-climate-trend.schema.json),
[`sky-quality.schema.json`](sky-quality.schema.json),
[`sky-quality-direction.schema.json`](sky-quality-direction.schema.json),
[`sky-quality-map.schema.json`](sky-quality-map.schema.json),
[`place-resolution.schema.json`](place-resolution.schema.json),
[`place-search.schema.json`](place-search.schema.json),
[`session.schema.json`](session.schema.json),
[`session-event-ingest.schema.json`](session-event-ingest.schema.json),
[`debrief.schema.json`](debrief.schema.json),
[`capture.schema.json`](capture.schema.json),
[`service.schema.json`](service.schema.json),
[`sign-in.schema.json`](sign-in.schema.json),
[`space-devices.schema.json`](space-devices.schema.json), and
[`space-deletion.schema.json`](space-deletion.schema.json). The payload of each session-event
type is normative in the event-type registry,
[`session-events.md`](https://github.com/sqound/astro-portal/blob/main/packages/astro-contracts/schemas/session-events.md) and the schemas beside it.

## Application error logs

A phone sends
the entries of the application's error logs with its **device token** (no
credential, or a web session alone, answers `401`) unless its person turned
« Envoyer les journaux d'erreur de l'application » off in « Réglages » →
« Données » (**on by default for a fresh installation**; an installation
that already chose keeps its choice). The device token only opens the door:
a line is **anonymous** and carries the installation's **diagnostic token**
alone. A service that opens
spaces takes the entries; a private server answers `404 not_available`
before reading any credential, unless its operator set
`ASTRO_PORTAL_APP_LOGS=accept`.

- `GET /v1/app-logs` — `200 { accepting: true, retentionDays: 30 }`. The
  phone asks it before it sends anything: a `404` there means the server
  does not take them, and nothing leaves the phone.
- `POST /v1/app-logs` `{ token, entries }` — `token` is the installation's
  diagnostic token, a UUID the phone drew at installation (never derived
  from the device or the space; drawn again when the app is reinstalled or
  its data erased). 1 to 20 entries, 256 KiB of JSON at most. An entry: `id` (a UUID the phone drew), `at` (UTC), `level`
  (`info`, `warning`, `error`), `event` (`device.failure`,
  `device.launch`…), `message`, `stack` (optional), the
  `diagnostic` (`platform`, `osVersion`, `appVersion`, `appBuild`, the raw
  hardware `model`, `locale`, `screen`, the `route`'s path without its query
  string, `network`: `wifi`, `wifiWithoutInternet`, `cellular`,
  `cellularWithoutInternet`, `ethernet`, `none`, `other`) and up to 30
  `breadcrumbs` (`at`, `kind`: `navigation`, `action`, `launch`, `device`,
  `name`, up to 12 scalar `fields`: the steps of an execution's launch carry
  the device's model and the state read back). Additive: an entry
  may carry `debugTrail`, up to 200 lines of the same shape as a breadcrumb (kind
  `debug` besides the four others), the last minutes of the phone's rolling
  local debug log. That log lives in the phone's memory only (10 minutes or 2 000
  lines, the smaller), is never sent alone, and is joined to an error entry and
  to a problem report (the phone then sends one `feedback.report` entry under the
  diagnostic token). The same sanitizing and the same second check apply.

The phone sanitizes every entry before it queues it; the service checks
every entry again with the same rules
(`packages/portal/src/app-log-redaction.js`, held by the
fixtures `app-log-redaction` and `app-log-fields`): a coordinate (a decimal
of three decimals or more within ±180, degrees and minutes), an e-mail
address, a network address, a secret (a PEM block, a bearer, a keyed
secret, a long key-like run), a route's query string, or a value under a
field named for a place, a person, a plan, a network or a secret other than
`[retiré]` answers `422 app_log_unredacted` with the index of the first
entry and the rules' names (`secret`, `identity`, `network`, `place`,
and `plan` from a service's stricter reading of an incident), **never the value**, and nothing of the batch is kept. Anything else
malformed (a missing token or one that is not a UUID included) answers
`400 invalid_request`. A diagnostic token sends at most 500 entries
in any rolling 24 hours (`429 app_log_limit` with `Retry-After`); an entry
whose `id` the service holds is a duplicate. The answer is
`201 { received, duplicates }`.

**Device incidents.** An entry whose `event` is `device.incident` (level
`warning`, a short `message` such as `telescope seestar iscope_start_view 251`,
its `diagnostic` and `breadcrumbs`, no `debugTrail`) carries an `incident`
(no other entry may): what a smart telescope or a mount refused or did not
do, sent on its own under the same switch, with nothing asked of the person.

- Its header, flat: `id` (the incident's UUID), `kind` (`telescope`,
  `mount`), `family` (`seestar`, `vespera`, `dwarf`, `lx200`, `synscan`,
  `ioptron`, `nexstar`, `alpaca`, `indi`…, `^[a-z0-9_-]{1,24}$`), `model`
  (80), `firmware` (40), `makerApp` (40), `linkKind` (`telescopeWifi`,
  `network`, `ethernet`, `bluetooth`, `bridge`), `verified` (proven on the
  bench or on trial), `action` (`pointing`, `stacking`, `schedule`, `init`,
  `eviction`, `busy`, `plan`, `client`, `connect`, `sync`, `goTo`, `move`,
  `park`, `home`, `tracking`, `linkLost`, `silence`, `stop`, `other`),
  `command` (120, as the driver wrote it: `iscope_start_view`, `:CM#`),
  `params` (12 scalar fields at most, a breadcrumb's rules), `variant`,
  `step` (80 each), `attempt`, `attempts`, `durationMs`, `code` (80: `251`,
  `e11`, `ASTROMETRY.IMAGE_TOO_DARK`), `answer` (500, the raw answer,
  sanitized), `elapsedMs`, `outcome` (`answer`, `silence`, `error`,
  `refused`), `cause` (a short stable code, `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_.:-]{0,119}$`:
  `seestar.pointing.251`, `lx200.sync.e11`, `synscan.goto.silence`),
  `before`, `after` (200 each), `occurrences` (1 or more), `connectedMs`,
  `sealedAt`. Every text but `family` and `cause` may be `null`.
- Up to 90 `lines`, oldest first: `seq`, `at` (UTC, to the second), `part`
  (`head`: the first lines of the connection; `before`: the last useful
  exchanges up to the failure; `after`: what followed), `channel` (24),
  `kind` (`sent`, `answer`, `event`, `state`, `link`, `error`, `note`,
  `stub`), `name` (120), `code` (40), `ms`, `repeat` (identical routine
  readings grouped, `×18`), `cause` (the line of the failure) and up to 12
  scalar `fields` (a breadcrumb's rules).
- A follow-up entry may repeat an incident's `id` with `lines: []` and a
  larger `occurrences` (the same cause again on the same link).
- The service checks the incident with the entry's rules: its texts, its
  `params` and each line's name, channel, code and fields; under a removed key
  a field may hold `[retiré]` or one of the device journal's marks (`[secret]`,
  `[id]`, `[réseau]`, `[plan]`, `[lieu]`, `[coord]`, `[e-mail]`). A member the
  contract does not name is dropped; a value outside its enumeration or its
  pattern answers `400 invalid_request`. The incident is kept with its entry
  (« Journaux »). A service may read it more strictly — the hosted service
  refuses any place value, even a whole degree, under a key of place or of
  height (`lat`, `lon`, `alt`, `az`, `elevation`, `sunAltitude`…), a mount's
  altitude and azimuth readings (`:GA#`, `:GZ#`) and an unmasked Bluetooth
  token (`:BS…#`), with the same `422 app_log_unredacted` (rule `plan`
  included) — and keep it apart: the hosted service keeps the incidents
  90 days in its administration's « Incidents d'appareil » (`v1-hosted.md`).
- An incident weighs about 25 KB; the phone cuts its batches at 200 KB, under
  the route's 256 KiB.

The entries are kept under `diagnostic_token` and **nothing else that names a
person**: neither the space nor the device is kept, and nothing on the service
links a token to a person; being tied to no space, they are not erased with the
space. The person hands the token over (the Réglages show it with « Copier »;
the problem report joins it, box checked by default).

Shapes and limits: [`app-log-batch.schema.json`](app-log-batch.schema.json).

## A capture without an image

A capture can be created without an image: the definition alone, the
images sent later with Astro Sync. `POST /v1/manage/captures` (web session
and CSRF token; shape `create` of
[`manage-captures.schema.json`](manage-captures.schema.json)) takes `{
sessionId?, targetIds? }` and answers `201 { capture }`: a private draft with
no image (`hasImage: false`, `variants: []`, `sha256`, `originalName` and
`byteSize` `null`, `state.state` `no_image`). It counts as a capture of
the space (`409 capture_limit_reached` past `capture.count_limit`) and holds
no byte. It is never public: `visibility: public` is refused with `409
no_image` (`state.publishRefusal` `no_image`). The first image sent to it —
`POST /v1/uploads` with its `captureId`, or without one for its night and a
target it shows (Astro Sync's way: the oldest definition of that night
showing one of the declared targets is filled instead of a second capture
being created) — becomes its **main image** (`capture.variant_limit` does not
count it) and is processed like the main image of a new capture; the next
ones are variants. `GET /v1/captures` (the devices' list) leaves a capture
without an image out until its first image arrives; `GET
/v1/manage/captures[/{id}]` lists it, and `GET /v1/captures/{id}` answers it.
It is not in the review queue (nothing to check), and its media route
answers `404`. Its image columns are NULL until then (migration 72,
`capture-without-image`); no placeholder is stored.
